| 408.3.8 | Interior exit stairway and ramp construction | flag | draft | One interior exit stairway with glazing at its landings: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_3_8_glazed_exit_stairway). | [BE] 408.3.8 Interior exit stairway and ramp construction. One interior exit stairway or ramp in each building shall be permit- ted to have glazing installed in doors and interior walls at each landing level providing access to the interior exit stairway or ramp, provided that the following conditions are met: 1. The interior exit stairway or ramp shall not serve more than four floor levels. 2. Exit doors shall be not less than 3/4-hour fire door assemblies complying with Section 716. 3. The total area of glazing at each floor level shall not exceed 5,000 square inches (3.2 m[fn2]) and individual panels of glazing shall not exceed 1,296 square inches (0.84 m[fn2]). 4. The glazing shall be protected on both sides by an automatic sprinkler system. The sprinkler system shall be designed to wet completely the entire surface of any glazing affected by fire when actuated. 5. The glazing shall be in a gasketed frame and installed in such a manner that the framing system will deflect without breaking (loading) the glass before the sprinkler system operates. 6. Obstructions, such as curtain rods, drapery traverse rods, curtains, drapes or similar materials shall not be installed between the automatic sprinklers and the glazing. | drafted:general.bc_408_3_8_glazed_exit_stairway |
| 408.4 | Locks | flag | draft | Group I-3 egress doors locked per the use condition: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_4_locks). | [BE] 408.4 Locks. Egress doors are permitted to be locked in accordance with the applicable use condition. Doors from a refuge area to the outside are permitted to be locked with a key in lieu of locking methods described in Section 408.4.1. The keys to unlock the exterior doors shall be available at all times and the locks shall be operable from both sides of the door. | drafted:general.bc_408_4_locks |
| 408.4.1 | Remote release | flag | draft | Remote release of egress door locks: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_4_1_remote_release). | [BE] 408.4.1 Remote release. Remote release of locks on doors in a means of egress shall be provided with reliable means of operation, remote from the resident living areas, to release locks on all required doors. In Occupancy Condition 3 or 4, the arrangement, accessibility and security of the release mechanisms required for egress shall be such that with the minimum avail- able staff at any time, the lock mechanisms are capable of being released within 2 minutes. Exception: Provisions for remote locking and unlocking of occupied rooms in Occupancy Condition 4 are not required provided that not more than 10 locks are necessary to be unlocked in order to move occupants from one smoke compartment to a refuge area within 3 minutes. The opening of necessary locks shall be accomplished with not more than two separate keys. | drafted:general.bc_408_4_1_remote_release |
| 408.4.2 | Power-operated doors and locks | flag | draft | Power-operated doors and locks, manual release, emergency power: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_4_2_power_operated_doors_and_locks). | [BE] 408.4.2 Power-operated doors and locks. Power-operated sliding doors or power-operated locks for swinging doors shall be operable by a manual release mechanism at the door. Emergency power shall be provided for the doors and locks in accor- dance with Section 2702. Exceptions: 1. Emergency power is not required in facilities with 10 or fewer locks complying with the exception to Section 408.4.1. 2. Emergency power is not required where remote mechanical operating releases are provided. | drafted:general.bc_408_4_2_power_operated_doors_and_locks |
| 408.4.3 | Redundant operation | flag | draft | A redundant release for remote-release doors and locks: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_4_3_redundant_operation). | [BE] 408.4.3 Redundant operation. Remote release, mechanically operated sliding doors or remote release, mechanically oper- ated locks shall be provided with a mechanically operated release mechanism at each door, or shall be provided with a redundant remote release control. | drafted:general.bc_408_4_3_redundant_operation |
| 408.4.4 | Relock capability | flag | draft | Doors unlocked in an emergency not relocking by themselves: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_4_4_relock_capability). | [BE] 408.4.4 Relock capability. Doors remotely unlocked under emergency conditions shall not automatically relock when closed unless specific action is taken at the remote location to enable doors to relock. | drafted:general.bc_408_4_4_relock_capability |
| 408.6 | Smoke barrier | flag | draft | Group I-3 stories divided into two smoke compartments: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_6_smoke_barrier). | [BE] 408.6 Smoke barrier. Occupancies in Group I-3 shall have smoke barriers complying with Sections 408.6 and 709 to divide every story occupied by residents for sleeping, or any other story having an occupant load of 50 or more persons, into not fewer than two smoke compartments. Exception: Spaces having a direct exit to one of the following, provided that the locking arrangement of the doors involved complies with the requirements for doors at the smoke barrier for the use condition involved: 1. A public way. 2. A building separated from the resident housing area by a 2-hour fire-resistance-rated assembly or 50 feet (15 240 mm) of open space. 3. A secured yard or court having a holding space 50 feet (15 240 mm) from the housing area that provides 6 square feet (0.56 m[fn2]) or more of refuge area per occupant, including residents, staff and visitors. | drafted:general.bc_408_6_smoke_barrier |
| 408.6.1 | Smoke compartments | flag | draft | At most 200 residents a compartment, 150 ft and 200 ft to its door: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_6_1_smoke_compartments). | [BE] 408.6.1 Smoke compartments. The number of residents in any smoke compartment shall be not more than 200. The distance of travel to a door in a smoke barrier from any room door required as exit access shall be not greater than 150 feet (45 720 mm). The distance of travel to a door in a smoke barrier from any point in a room shall be not greater than 200 feet (60 960 mm). | drafted:general.bc_408_6_1_smoke_compartments |
| 408.6.2 | Refuge area | flag | draft | At least 6 net sq ft of refuge a person each side: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_6_2_refuge_area). | [BE] 408.6.2 Refuge area. Not less than 6 net square feet (0.56 m[fn2]) per occupant shall be provided on each side of each smoke barrier for the total number of occupants in adjoining smoke compartments. This space shall be readily available wherever the occupants are moved across the smoke barrier in a fire emergency. | drafted:general.bc_408_6_2_refuge_area |
| 408.6.3 | Independent egress | flag | draft | Egress from each Group I-3 smoke compartment without going back: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_6_3_independent_egress). | [BE] 408.6.3 Independent egress. A means of egress shall be provided from each smoke compartment created by smoke barriers without having to return through the smoke compartment from which means of egress originates. | drafted:general.bc_408_6_3_independent_egress |
| 408.8 | Subdivision of resident housing areas | flag | draft | Resident housing areas separated per 408.8.1 to 408.8.4: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_8_subdivision_of_resident_housing_areas). | 408.8 Subdivision of resident housing areas. Sleeping areas and any contiguous day room, group activity space or other common spaces where residents are housed shall be separated from other spaces in accordance with Sections 408.8.1 through 408.8.4. | drafted:general.bc_408_8_subdivision_of_resident_housing_areas |
| 408.8.1 | Occupancy Conditions 3 and 4 | flag | draft | Sleeping areas over 50 ft through a common space: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_8_1_occupancy_conditions_3_and_4). | 408.8.1 Occupancy Conditions 3 and 4. Each sleeping area in Occupancy Conditions 3 and 4 shall be separated from the adja- cent common spaces by a smoke-tight partition where the distance of travel from the sleeping area through the common space to the corridor exceeds 50 feet (15 240 mm). | drafted:general.bc_408_8_1_occupancy_conditions_3_and_4 |
| 408.8.2 | Occupancy Condition 5 | flag | draft | Smoke-tight partitions in Occupancy Condition 5: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_8_2_occupancy_condition_5). | 408.8.2 Occupancy Condition 5. Each sleeping area in Occupancy Condition 5 shall be separated from adjacent sleeping areas, corridors and common spaces by a smoke-tight partition. Additionally, common spaces shall be separated from the corridor by a smoke-tight partition. | drafted:general.bc_408_8_2_occupancy_condition_5 |
| 408.8.3 | Openings in room face | flag | draft | At most 120 sq in of openings in a sleeping room's face: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_8_3_openings_in_room_face). | 408.8.3 Openings in room face. The aggregate area of openings in a solid sleeping room face in Occupancy Conditions 2, 3, 4 and 5 shall not exceed 120 square inches (0.77 m[fn2]). The aggregate area shall include all openings including door undercuts, food passes and grilles. Openings shall be not more than 36 inches (914 mm) above the floor. In Occupancy Condition 5, the openings shall be closeable from the room side. | drafted:general.bc_408_8_3_openings_in_room_face |
| 408.8.4 | Smoke-tight doors | flag | draft | Doors in smoke-tight partitions: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-3 (general.bc_408_8_4_smoke_tight_doors). | 408.8.4 Smoke-tight doors. Doors in openings in partitions required to be smoke tight by Section 408.8 shall be substantial doors, of construction that will resist the passage of smoke. Latches and door closers are not required on cell doors. | drafted:general.bc_408_8_4_smoke_tight_doors |
| 410.5 | Means of egress | flag | draft | Stage and platform egress, Chapter 10 as 410.5 modifies it: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_means_of_egress). | [BE] 410.5 Means of egress. Except as modified or as provided for in this section, the provisions of Chapter 10 shall apply. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_means_of_egress |
| 410.5.1 | Arrangement | flag | draft | An exit or exit access doorway on each side of a stage: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_1_arrangement). | [BE] 410.5.1 Arrangement. Where two or more exits or exit access doorways from the stage are required in accordance with Section 1006.2, not fewer than one exit or exit access doorway shall be provided on each side of a stage. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_1_arrangement |
| 410.5.2 | Stairway and ramp enclosure | flag | draft | Stairways and ramps serving a stage or platform unenclosed: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_2_stairway_and_ramp_enclosure). | [BE] 410.5.2 Stairway and ramp enclosure. Exit access stairways and ramps serving a stage or platform are not required to be enclosed. Exit access stairways and ramps serving technical production areas are not required to be enclosed. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_2_stairway_and_ramp_enclosure |
| 410.5.3 | Technical production areas | flag | draft | Technical production areas' means of egress and escape: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_3_technical_production_areas). | [BE] 410.5.3 Technical production areas. Technical production areas shall be provided with means of egress and means of escape in accordance with Sections 410.5.3.1 through 410.5.3.5. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_3_technical_production_areas |
| 410.5.3.1 | Number of means of egress | flag | draft | At least one means of egress from a technical production area: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_3_1_number_of_means_of_egress). | [BE] 410.5.3.1 Number of means of egress. Not fewer than one means of egress shall be provided from technical production areas. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_3_1_number_of_means_of_egress |
| 410.5.3.2 | Exit access travel distance | flag | draft | Technical production area travel, 300 ft, 400 ft with NFPA 13: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_3_2_exit_access_travel_distance). | [BE] 410.5.3.2 Exit access travel distance. The exit access travel distance shall be not greater than 300 feet (91 440 mm) for buildings without an automatic sprinkler system and 400 feet (122 mm) for buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_3_2_exit_access_travel_distance |
| 410.5.3.3 | Two means of egress | flag | draft | Common path at most 100 ft where two are required: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_3_3_two_means_of_egress), and a review band on the Chapter 10 rules it tightens. | [BE] 410.5.3.3 Two means of egress. Where two means of egress are required, the common path of travel shall be not greater than 100 feet (30 480 mm). Exception: A means of escape to a roof in place of a second means of egress is permitted. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_3_3_two_means_of_egress, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r3_r4 |
| 410.5.3.4 | Path of egress travel | flag | draft | Stairs, ladders and catwalks serving technical areas: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_3_4_path_of_egress_travel). | [BE] 410.5.3.4 Path of egress travel. The following exit access components are permitted where serving technical production areas: 1. Stairways. 2. Ramps. 3. Spiral stairways. 4. Catwalks. 5. Alternating tread devices. 6. Permanent ladders. | drafted:general.bc_410_5_3_4_path_of_egress_travel |
| 410.5.3.5 | Width | flag | draft | At least 22 in of egress width in technical support areas: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may have a stage, a platform or a technical production area (general.bc_410_5_3_5_width). | [BE] 410.5.3.5 Width. The path of egress travel within and from technical support areas shall be not less than 22 inches (559 mm). | drafted:general.bc_410_5_3_5_width |
| 411.4 | Exit marking | flag | draft | Exit signs and directional markings in special amusement areas: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may hold a special amusement area, a space the code gives its own exits or travel limit (general.bc_411_4_exit_marking). | [BE] 411.4 Exit marking. Exit signs shall be installed at the required exit or exit access doorways serving special amusement areas in accordance with this section and Section 1013. Approved directional exit markings shall be provided. Where mirrors, mazes or other designs disguise the path of egress travel such that the path of egress travel is not apparent, approved and listed low-level exit signs that comply with Section 1013.5, and directional path markings listed in accordance with UL 1994, shall be provided and located not more than 8 inches (203 mm) above the walking surface and on or near the path of egress travel. Such markings shall become visible in an emergency. The directional exit marking shall be activated by the automatic smoke detection system and the automatic sprin- kler system in accordance with Section 411.3.2. | drafted:general.bc_411_4_exit_marking |
| 411.4.1 | Photoluminescent exit signs | flag | draft | Photoluminescent exit signs listed: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may hold a special amusement area, a space the code gives its own exits or travel limit (general.bc_411_4_1_photoluminescent_exit_signs). | [BE] 411.4.1 Photoluminescent exit signs. Where photoluminescent exit signs are installed, such signs shall be listed and the activating light source and viewing distance shall be in accordance with the listing and markings on the signs. | drafted:general.bc_411_4_1_photoluminescent_exit_signs |
| 412.2.2 | Means of egress and evacuation | flag | draft | Airport traffic control tower egress per 412.2.2.1 to 412.2.2.3: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_2_2_airport_traffic_control_towers). | [BE] 412.2.2 Means of egress and evacuation. The means of egress in airport traffic control towers shall comply with Sections 412.2.2.1 through 412.2.2.3. | drafted:general.bc_412_2_2_airport_traffic_control_towers |
| 412.2.2.1 | Stairways | flag | draft | Tower stairways per 1011, exit stairways smokeproof: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_2_2_1_stairways). | [BE] 412.2.2.1 Stairways. Stairways in airport traffic control towers shall be in accordance with Section 1011. Exit stairways shall be smokeproof enclosures complying with one of the alternatives provided in Section 909.20. Exception: Stairways in airport traffic control towers are not required to comply with Section 1011.12. | drafted:general.bc_412_2_2_1_stairways |
| 412.2.2.2 | Exit access | flag | draft | One means of exit access from a tower's observation levels: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_2_2_2_exit_access). | [BE] 412.2.2.2 Exit access. From observation levels, airport traffic control towers shall be permitted to have a single means of exit access for a distance of travel not greater than 100 feet (30 480 mm). Exit access stairways from the observation level need not be enclosed. | drafted:general.bc_412_2_2_2_exit_access |
| 412.2.2.3 | Number of exits | flag | draft | One exit stairway for 15 occupants, 1,500 sq ft a floor: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_2_2_3_number_of_exits). | [BE] 412.2.2.3 Number of exits. Not less than one exit stairway shall be permitted for airport traffic control towers of any height provided that the occupant load per floor is not greater than 15 and the area per floor does not exceed 1,500 square feet (140 m[fn2]). | drafted:general.bc_412_2_2_3_number_of_exits |
| 412.2.2.3.1 | Interior finish | flag | draft | Class A or B finishes in a tower with one exit stairway: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_2_2_3_1_interior_finish). | [BE] 412.2.2.3.1 Interior finish. Where an airport traffic control tower is provided with only one exit stairway, interior wall and ceiling finishes shall be either Class A or Class B. | drafted:general.bc_412_2_2_3_1_interior_finish |
| 412.2.2.3.2 | Exit separation | flag | draft | A sprinklered tower's exits a quarter of its length apart: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_2_2_3_2_exit_separation). | [BE] 412.2.2.3.2 Exit separation. Where an airport traffic control tower is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 and two exits are required, the exit separation distance required by Section 1007 shall be not less than one-fourth of the length of the maximum overall dimension of the area served. | drafted:general.bc_412_2_2_3_2_exit_separation |
| 412.4.2 | Egress | flag | draft | A residential aircraft hangar's two means of egress: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_4_2_residential_aircraft_hangar_egress), and a review band on the Chapter 10 rules it tightens. | [BE] 412.4.2 Egress. A hangar shall provide two means of egress. One of the doors into the dwelling shall be considered as meet- ing only one of the two means of egress. | drafted:general.bc_412_4_2_residential_aircraft_hangar_egress, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_u |
| 412.6 | Aircraft manufacturing facilities | flag | draft | Aircraft manufacturing travel increased per Table 412.6: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_6_aircraft_manufacturing_facilities). | [BE] 412.6 Aircraft manufacturing facilities. In buildings used for the manufacturing of aircraft, exit access travel distances indi- cated in Section 1017.1 shall be increased in accordance with the following: 1. The building shall be of Type I or II construction. 2. Exit access travel distance shall not exceed the distances given in Table 412.6. | drafted:general.bc_412_6_aircraft_manufacturing_facilities |
| Table 412.6 | AIRCRAFT MANUFACTURING EXIT ACCESS TRAVEL DISTANCE | flag | draft | Aircraft manufacturing exit access travel distance: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_table_412_6_aircraft_manufacturing_travel). | [BE] TABLE 412.6—AIRCRAFT MANUFACTURING EXIT ACCESS TRAVEL DISTANCE MANUFACTURING AREA HEIGHT (square feet)[fna] (feet)[fnb] ≥ 150,000 ≥ 200,000 ≥ 250,000 ≥ 500,000 ≥ 750,000 ≥ 1,000,000 ≥ 25 400 450 500 500 500 500 ≥ 50 400 500 600 700 700 700 ≥ 75 400 500 700 850 1,000 1,000 ≥ 100 400 500 750 1,000 1,250 1,500 For SI: 1 foot = 304.8 mm. a.Contiguous floor area of the aircraft manufacturing facility having the indicated height. b.Minimum height from finished floor to bottom of ceiling or roof slab or deck. | drafted:general.bc_table_412_6_aircraft_manufacturing_travel |
| 412.6.1 | Ancillary areas | flag | draft | Ancillary areas egressing through the manufacturing area: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_6_1_ancillary_areas). | [BE] 412.6.1 Ancillary areas. Rooms, areas and spaces ancillary to the primary manufacturing area shall be permitted to egress through such area having a minimum height as indicated in Table 412.6. Exit access travel distance within the ancillary room, area or space shall not exceed that indicated in Table 1017.2 based on the occupancy classification of that ancillary area. Total exit access travel distance shall not exceed that indicated in Table 412.6. | drafted:general.bc_412_6_1_ancillary_areas |
| 412.7.3 | Means of egress | flag | draft | Two exits from a heliport or helistop landing area: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be or hold an aircraft-related occupancy (general.bc_412_7_3_heliports_and_helistops). | [BE] 412.7.3 Means of egress. The means of egress from heliports and helistops shall comply with the provisions of Chapter 10. Landing areas located on buildings or structures shall have two or more exits or access to exits. For landing areas less than 60 feet (18 288 mm) in length or less than 2,000 square feet (186 m[fn2]) in area, the second means of egress is permitted to be a fire escape, alternating tread device or ladder leading to the floor below. | drafted:general.bc_412_7_3_heliports_and_helistops |
| 415.11.3.3 | Means of egress | flag | draft | Service corridor travel, dead ends, exits and doors: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group H-5 with a service corridor (general.bc_415_11_3_3_service_corridor_egress), and a review band on the Chapter 10 rules it tightens. | [F] 415.11.3.3 Means of egress. The distance of travel from any point in a service corridor to an exit, exit access corridor or door into a fabrication area shall be not greater than 75 feet (22 860 mm). Dead ends shall be not greater than 4 feet (1219 mm) in length. There shall be not less than two exits, and not more than one-half of the required means of egress shall require travel into a fabrication area. Doors from service corridors shall swing in the direction of egress travel and shall be self-closing. | drafted:general.bc_415_11_3_3_service_corridor_egress, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_3_4_2__h_i_r, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h5, drafted:general.bc_1020_5_dead_end_other_groups |
| 415.11.3.4 | Minimum width | flag | draft | Service corridor 5 ft wide, or 33 in past the widest cart: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group H-5 with a service corridor (general.bc_415_11_3_4_service_corridor_width), and a review band on the Chapter 10 rules it tightens. | [F] 415.11.3.4 Minimum width. The clear width of a service corridor shall be not less than 5 feet (1524 mm), or 33 inches (838 mm) wider than the widest cart or truck used in the service corridor, whichever is greater. | drafted:general.bc_415_11_3_4_service_corridor_width, drafted:general.bc_1020_3_corridor_capacity, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_3_corridor_width |
| 415.11.6.6 | Exits | flag | draft | An HPM, gas or liquid storage room's exit directly out: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group H-5, or the H-2, H-3 or H-4 of an HPM, gas or liquid storage room (general.bc_415_11_6_6_hpm_room_exits). | [F] 415.11.6.6 Exits. Where two exits are required from HPM rooms, liquid storage rooms and gas rooms, one shall be directly to the outside of the building. | drafted:general.bc_415_11_6_6_hpm_room_exits |
| 415.11.6.7 | Doors | flag | draft | Self-closing 3/4-hour fire doors in their fire barrier walls: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group H-5, or the H-2, H-3 or H-4 of an HPM, gas or liquid storage room (general.bc_415_11_6_7_hpm_room_doors). | [F] 415.11.6.7 Doors. Doors in a fire barrier wall, including doors to corridors, shall be self-closing fire door assemblies having a fire protection rating of not less than 3/4 hour. | drafted:general.bc_415_11_6_7_hpm_room_doors |
| 420.1 | General | flag | draft | Groups I-1, R-1, R-2, R-3 and R-4, Sections 420.1 to 420.11: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 or R (general.bc_420_1_groups_i1_and_r). | 420.1 General. Occupancies in Groups I-1, R-1, R-2, R-3 and R-4 shall comply with the provisions of Sections 420.1 through 420.11 and other applicable provisions of this code. | drafted:general.bc_420_1_groups_i1_and_r |
| 420.2 | Separation walls | flag | draft | Walls between dwelling and sleeping units as fire partitions: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 or R (general.bc_420_2_separation_walls). | 420.2 Separation walls. Walls separating dwelling units in the same building, walls separating sleeping units in the same building, walls separating dwelling units from sleeping units in the same building and walls separating dwelling or sleeping units from other occupancies contiguous to them in the same building shall be constructed as fire partitions in accordance with Section 708. | drafted:general.bc_420_2_separation_walls |
| 420.3 | Horizontal separation | flag | draft | Floors between dwelling and sleeping units as horizontal assemblies: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 or R (general.bc_420_3_horizontal_separation). | 420.3 Horizontal separation. Floor assemblies separating dwelling units in the same buildings, floor assemblies separating sleeping units in the same building, floor assemblies separating dwelling units from sleeping units in the same building and floor assemblies separating dwelling or sleeping units from other occupancies contiguous to them in the same building shall be constructed as hori- zontal assemblies in accordance with Section 711. | drafted:general.bc_420_3_horizontal_separation |
| 420.6 | Smoke barriers in Group I-1, Condition 2 | flag | draft | Group I-1, Condition 2 smoke compartments: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 (general.bc_420_6_smoke_barriers_in_group_i1_condition_2). | 420.6 Smoke barriers in Group I-1, Condition 2. Smoke barriers shall be provided in Group I-1, Condition 2 to subdivide every story used by persons receiving care, treatment or sleeping and to provide other stories with an occupant load of 50 or more persons, into not fewer than two smoke compartments. Such stories shall be divided into smoke compartments with an area of not more than 22,500 square feet (2092 m[fn2]) and the distance of travel from any point in a smoke compartment to a smoke barrier door shall not exceed 200 feet (60 960 mm). The smoke barrier shall be in accordance with Section 709. | drafted:general.bc_420_6_smoke_barriers_in_group_i1_condition_2 |
| 420.6.1 | Refuge area | flag | draft | A refuge area in each Group I-1 smoke compartment: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 (general.bc_420_6_1_refuge_area). | 420.6.1 Refuge area. Refuge areas shall be provided within each smoke compartment. The size of the refuge area shall accommo- date the occupants and care recipients from the adjoining smoke compartment. Where a smoke compartment is adjoined by two or more smoke compartments, the minimum area of the refuge area shall accommodate the largest occupant load of the adjoining compartments. The size of the refuge area shall provide the following: 1. Not less than 15 net square feet (1.4 m[fn2]) for each care recipient. 2. Not less than 6 net square feet (0.56 m[fn2]) for other occupants. Areas or spaces permitted to be included in the calculation of the refuge area are corridors, lounge or dining areas and other low-hazard areas. | drafted:general.bc_420_6_1_refuge_area |
| 420.7 | Group I-1 assisted living housing units | flag | draft | Shared spaces open to a Group I-1 corridor: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 (general.bc_420_7_assisted_living_housing_units). | 420.7 Group I-1 assisted living housing units. In Group I-1 occupancies, where a fire-resistance corridor is provided in areas where assisted living residents are housed, shared living spaces, group meeting or multipurpose therapeutic spaces open to the corridor shall be in accordance with all of the following criteria: 1. The walls and ceilings of the space are constructed as required for corridors. 2. The spaces are not occupied as resident sleeping rooms, treatment rooms, incidental uses in accordance with Section 509, or hazardous uses. 3. The open space is protected by an automatic fire detection system installed in accordance with Section 907. 4. In Group I-1, Condition 1, the corridors onto which the spaces open are protected by an automatic fire detection system installed in accordance with Section 907, or the spaces are equipped throughout with quick-response sprinklers in accor- dance with Section 903.3.2. | drafted:general.bc_420_7_assisted_living_housing_units |
| 420.8 | Group I-1 cooking facilities | flag | draft | Group I-1 cooking facilities open to the corridor: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 (general.bc_420_8_group_i1_cooking_facilities). | 420.8 Group I-1 cooking facilities. In Group I-1 occupancies, rooms or spaces that contain a cooking facility with domestic cooking appliances shall be permitted to be open to the corridor where all of the following criteria are met: 1. In Group I-1, Condition 1 occupancies, the number of care recipients served by one cooking facility shall not be greater than 30. 2. In Group I-1, Condition 2 occupancies, the number of care recipients served by one cooking facility and within the same smoke compartment shall not be greater than 30. 3. The space containing the cooking facilities shall be arranged so as not to obstruct access to the required exit. 4. The cooking appliances shall comply with Section 420.9. | drafted:general.bc_420_8_group_i1_cooking_facilities |
| 420.9 | Domestic cooking appliances | flag | draft | Domestic cooking appliances in Group I-1: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be Group I-1 (general.bc_420_9_domestic_cooking_appliances). | 420.9 Domestic cooking appliances. In Group I-1 occupancies, installation of cooking appliance used in domestic cooking facilities shall comply with all of the following: 1. The types of cooking appliances permitted shall be limited to ovens, cooktops, ranges, warmers and microwaves. 2. Domestic cooking hoods installed and constructed in accordance with Section 505 of the Mechanical Code of New York State shall be provided over cooktops or ranges. 3. Cooktops and ranges shall be protected in accordance with Section 904.15. 4. A shutoff for the fuel and electrical supply to the cooking equipment shall be provided in a location to which only staff has access. 5. A timer shall be provided that automatically deactivates the cooking appliances within a period of not more than 120 minutes. 6. A portable fire extinguisher shall be provided. Installation shall be in accordance with Section 906 and the extinguisher shall be located within a 30-foot (9144 mm) distance of travel from each domestic cooking appliance. Exceptions: 1. Cooking facilities provided within care recipients’ individual dwelling units are not required to comply with this section. 2. Cooktops and ranges used for care-recipient training or nutritional counseling are not required to comply with Item 3 of this section. | drafted:general.bc_420_9_domestic_cooking_appliances |
| 422.1 | General | flag | draft | Ambulatory care facilities, Sections 422.1 to 422.7: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be an ambulatory care facility (general.bc_422_1_ambulatory_care_facilities). | 422.1 General. Occupancies classified as ambulatory care facilities shall comply with the provisions of Sections 422.1 through 422.7 and other applicable provisions of this code. | drafted:general.bc_422_1_ambulatory_care_facilities |
| 422.2 | Separation | flag | draft | An ambulatory care facility separated by a fire partition: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be an ambulatory care facility (general.bc_422_2_separation). | 422.2 Separation. Ambulatory care facilities where the potential for four or more care recipients are to be incapable of self-preserva- tion at any time shall be separated from adjacent spaces, corridors or tenants with a fire partition installed in accordance with Section 708. | drafted:general.bc_422_2_separation, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_a_b_e_f_m_s_u |
| 422.3 | Smoke compartments | flag | draft | Smoke compartments for over 10,000 sq ft of ambulatory care on a story: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be an ambulatory care facility (general.bc_422_3_smoke_compartments). | 422.3 Smoke compartments. Where the aggregate area of one or more ambulatory care facilities is greater than 10,000 square feet (929 m[fn2]) on one story, the story shall be provided with a smoke barrier to subdivide the story into not fewer than two smoke compart- ments. The area of any one such smoke compartment shall be not greater than 22,500 square feet (2092 m[fn2]). The distance of travel from any point in a smoke compartment to a smoke barrier door shall be not greater than 200 feet (60 960 mm). The smoke barrier shall be installed in accordance with Section 709 with the exception that smoke barriers shall be continuous from outside wall to an outside wall, a floor to a floor, or from a smoke barrier to a smoke barrier or a combination thereof. | drafted:general.bc_422_3_smoke_compartments |
| 422.3.1 | Means of egress | flag | draft | The defend-in-place plan where compartments are required: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be an ambulatory care facility (general.bc_422_3_1_means_of_egress). | 422.3.1 Means of egress. Where ambulatory care facilities require smoke compartmentation in accordance with Section 422.3, the fire safety evacuation plans provided in accordance with Section 1002.2 shall identify the building components necessary to support a defend-in-place emergency response in accordance with Sections 403 and 404 of the Fire Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_422_3_1_means_of_egress |
| 422.3.2 | Refuge area | flag | draft | Refuge of 30 net sq ft a nonambulatory care recipient: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be an ambulatory care facility (general.bc_422_3_2_refuge_area). | 422.3.2 Refuge area. Not less than 30 net square feet (2.8 m[fn2]) for each nonambulatory care recipient shall be provided within the aggregate area of corridors, care recipient rooms, treatment rooms, lounge or dining areas and other low-hazard areas within each smoke compartment. Each occupant of an ambulatory care facility shall be provided with access to a refuge area without passing through or utilizing adjacent tenant spaces. | drafted:general.bc_422_3_2_refuge_area |
| 422.3.3 | Independent egress | flag | draft | Egress from each ambulatory care smoke compartment without going back: not in the model, so a person's check where the building may be an ambulatory care facility (general.bc_422_3_3_independent_egress). | 422.3.3 Independent egress. A means of egress shall be provided from each smoke compartment created by smoke barriers with- out having to return through the smoke compartment from which means of egress originated. | drafted:general.bc_422_3_3_independent_egress |
| 508.2.1 | Occupancy classification | flag | draft | Each accessory occupancy is classified on its own (Section 302.1, not brought in) and the code applies to each portion by its classification: the architect's statement, a person's check. | 508.2.1 Occupancy classification. Accessory occupancies shall be individually classified in accordance with Section 302.1. The requirements of this code shall apply to each portion of the building based on the occupancy classification of that space. | drafted:general.bc_508_2_1_classification |
| 508.2.2 | Allowable building height | flag | draft | The building's allowable height and stories follow Section 504 (not brought in) for the main occupancy: a person checks. | 508.2.2 Allowable building height. The allowable height and number of stories of the building containing accessory occupancies shall be in accordance with Section 504 for the main occupancy of the building. | drafted:general.bc_508_2_2_height |
| 508.2.3 | Allowable building area | rule | draft | Caps aggregate accessory occupancies at 10 percent of the floor area of the story: measurable. Its second test (Table 506.2's tabular values, and Section 506) is not brought in: a person checks it. | for the main occupancy of the building. Aggregate accessory occupancies shall not occupy more than 10 percent of the floor area of the story in which they are located and shall not exceed the tabular values for nonsprinklered buildings in Table 506.2 for each such accessory occupancy. | drafted:general.bc_508_2_3_accessory_area, drafted:general.bc_508_2_3_tabular_values |
| 508.2.4 | Separation of occupancies | flag | draft | No separation is required between accessory occupancies and the main occupancy, except Group H-2 to H-5 (508.4, not brought in); the dwelling and sleeping units of I-1, R-1, R-2 and R-3 are separated per 420.2 and 420.3 (flags of Chapter 4 part e): a person checks. | 508.2.4 Separation of occupancies. No separation is required between accessory occupancies and the main occupancy. | drafted:general.bc_508_2_4_separation |
| 703.2.1.1 | Nonsymmetrical wall construction | flag | draft | An interior wall of nonsymmetrical construction is rated by the shorter of its two tests, one from each face: how its walls were tested isn't in the model, a person's check (general.bc_703_2_1_1_nonsymmetrical_walls). | 703.2.1.1 Nonsymmetrical wall construction. Interior walls and partitions of nonsymmetrical construction shall be tested with both faces exposed to the furnace, and the assigned fire-resistance rating shall be the shortest duration obtained from the two tests conducted in compliance with ASTM E119 or UL 263. Where evidence is furnished to show that the wall was tested with the least fire-resistant side exposed to the furnace, subject to acceptance of the building official, the wall need not be subjected to tests from the opposite side (see Section 705.5 for exterior walls). | drafted:general.bc_703_2_1_1_nonsymmetrical_walls |
| 703.5 | Marking and identification | flag | draft | Rated walls are identified with signs or stenciling in accessible concealed spaces (at most 15 feet from each end, every 30 feet, 3-inch letters): not in the model, a person's check (general.bc_703_5_marking). | 703.5 Marking and identification. Where there is an accessible concealed floor, floor-ceiling or attic space, fire walls, fire barriers, fire partitions, smoke barriers and smoke partitions or any other wall required to have protected openings or penetrations shall be effectively and permanently identified with signs or stenciling in the concealed space. Such identification shall: 1. Be located within 15 feet (4572 mm) of the end of each wall and at intervals not exceeding 30 feet (9144 mm) measured hori- zontally along the wall or partition. 2. Include lettering not less than 3 inches (76 mm) in height with a minimum 3/8-inch (9.5 mm) stroke in a contrasting color incorporating the suggested wording, “FIRE AND/OR SMOKE BARRIER—PROTECT ALL OPENINGS,” or other wording. | drafted:general.bc_703_5_marking |
| 705.2 | Projections | flag | draft | Cornices, eave overhangs, exterior balconies and similar projections beyond the exterior wall conform to 705.2 and Section 1405, exterior egress balconies also to 1021 and exterior exit stairways and ramps to 1027; no projection comes closer to the line used to determine the fire separation distance than Table 705.2 allows; but buildings on the same lot considered portions of one building per 705.3 need not comply for projections between them: a person's check (general.bc_705_2_projections). | 705.2 Projections. Cornices, eave overhangs, exterior balconies and similar projections extending beyond the exterior wall shall conform to the requirements of this section and Section 1405. Exterior egress balconies and exterior exit stairways and ramps shall comply with Sections 1021 and 1027, respectively. Projections shall not extend any closer to the line used to determine the fire sepa- ration distance than shown in Table 705.2. Exception: Buildings on the same lot and considered as portions of one building in accordance with Section 705.3 are not required to comply with this section for projections between the buildings. | drafted:general.bc_705_2_projections |
| Table 705.2 | MINIMUM DISTANCE OF PROJECTION | flag | draft | Table 705.2 gives the least distance a projection keeps from the line used to determine the fire separation distance, typed row by row in its flag: a person's check (general.bc_table_705_2_projection_distances). | TABLE 705.2—MINIMUM DISTANCE OF PROJECTION FIRE SEPARATION DISTANCE (FSD) (feet) MINIMUM DISTANCE FROM LINE USED TO DETERMINE FSD 0 to less than 2 Projections not permitted 2 to less than 3 24 inches 3 to less than 5 Two-thirds of FSD 5 or greater 40 inches For SI: 1 foot = 304.8 mm; 1 inch = 25.4 mm. | drafted:general.bc_table_705_2_projection_distances |
| 705.2.1 | Types I and II construction | flag | draft | Projections from walls of Type I or II construction are of noncombustible materials, or of combustible materials as 705.2.3.1 (balconies and similar projections) and 705.2.4 (bay and oriel windows) allow: a person's check (general.bc_705_2_1_type_i_ii_projections). | 705.2.1 Types I and II construction. Projections from walls of Type I or II construction shall be of noncombustible materials or combustible materials as allowed by Sections 705.2.3.1 and 705.2.4. | drafted:general.bc_705_2_1_type_i_ii_projections |
| 705.2.2 | Type III, IV or V construction | flag | draft | Projections from walls of Type III, IV or V construction are of any approved material: a person's check (general.bc_705_2_2_type_iii_iv_v_projections). | 705.2.2 Type III, IV or V construction. Projections from walls of Type III, IV or V construction shall be of any approved material. | drafted:general.bc_705_2_2_type_iii_iv_v_projections |
| 705.2.3 | Projection protection | flag | draft | Projections extending to 5 feet or less from the line used to determine the fire separation distance are of noncombustible materials, combustible materials of at least 1-hour fire-resistance-rated construction, heavy timber construction per 2304.11, fire-retardant-treated wood, or as 705.2.3.1 permits; but Type VB construction is allowed for combustible projections in Group R-3 and U occupancies with a fire separation distance of 5 feet or more: a person's check (general.bc_705_2_3_projection_protection). | 705.2.3 Projection protection. Projections extending to 5 feet (1524 mm) or less from the line used to determine the fire separa- tion distance shall be one of the following: 1. Noncombustible materials. 2. Combustible materials of not less than 1-hour fire-resistance-rated construction. 3. Heavy timber construction complying with Section 2304.11. 4. Fire-retardant-treated wood. 5. As permitted by Section 705.2.3.1. | drafted:general.bc_705_2_3_projection_protection |
| 705.2.3.1 | Balconies and similar projections | flag | draft | Balconies and similar projections of combustible construction other than fire-retardant-treated wood are fire-resistance rated where Table 601 requires it for floor construction, or of heavy timber construction per 2304.11, and their aggregate length is at most 50 percent of the building's perimeter on each floor; but on buildings of Types I and II construction, three stories or less above grade plane, fire-retardant-treated wood is permitted for balconies, porches, decks and exterior stairways not used as required exits; untreated wood and plastic composites complying with ASTM D7032 and Section 2612 are permitted for pickets, rails and similar guard components at most 42 inches high; balconies and similar projections on buildings of Types III, IV-HT and V construction may be of Type V construction with no fire-resistance rating where sprinkler protection is extended to these areas; and where sprinkler protection is extended to the balcony areas, the aggregate length of balconies on each floor is not limited: a person's check (general.bc_705_2_3_1_balconies). | 705.2.3.1 Balconies and similar projections. Balconies and similar projections of combustible construction other than fire- retardant-treated wood shall be fire-resistance rated where required by Table 601 for floor construction or shall be of heavy timber construction in accordance with Section 2304.11. The aggregate length of the projections shall not exceed 50 percent of the building’s perimeter on each floor. Exceptions: 1. On buildings of Types I and II construction, three stories or less above grade plane, fire-retardant-treated wood shall be permitted for balconies, porches, decks and exterior stairways not used as required exits. 2. Untreated wood and plastic composites that comply with ASTM D7032 and Section 2612 are permitted for pickets, rails and similar guard components that are limited to 42 inches (1067 mm) in height. 3. Balconies and similar projections on buildings of Types III, IV-HT and V construction shall be permitted to be of Type V construction and shall not be required to have a fire-resistance rating where sprinkler protection is extended to these areas. 4. Where sprinkler protection is extended to the balcony areas, the aggregate length of the balcony on each floor shall not be limited. | drafted:general.bc_705_2_3_1_balconies |
| 705.3 | Buildings on the same lot | flag | draft | Buildings on the same lot are assumed to have an imaginary line between them for their required wall and opening protection, projections and roof coverings; where a new building is erected on the same lot as an existing one, the line sits so that the existing building's exterior wall and opening protection meet 705.5 and 705.9; but two or more buildings on the same lot are either regulated as separate buildings or considered portions of one building where their aggregate area is within Chapter 5's limits for a single building (the most restrictive group's or type of construction's area where they differ); and where an S-2 parking garage of Type I or IIA construction is on the same lot as a Group R-2 building with no fire separation distance between them, the adjoining exterior walls may have occupant use openings per 706.8, opening protectives required only in the garage's exterior wall, rated at least 1 1/2 hours: a person's check (general.bc_705_3_buildings_on_the_same_lot). | 705.3 Buildings on the same lot. For the purposes of determining the required wall and opening protection, projections and roof- covering requirements, buildings on the same lot shall be assumed to have an imaginary line between them. Where a new building is to be erected on the same lot as an existing building, the location of the assumed imaginary line with rela- tion to the existing building shall be such that the exterior wall and opening protection of the existing building meet the criteria as set forth in Sections 705.5 and 705.9. Exceptions: 1. Two or more buildings on the same lot shall be either regulated as separate buildings or shall be considered as portions of one building if the aggregate area of such buildings is within the limits specified in Chapter 5 for a single building. Where the buildings contain different occupancy groups or are of different types of construction, the area shall be that allowed for the most restrictive occupancy or construction. 2. Where an S-2 parking garage of Construction Type I or IIA is erected on the same lot as a Group R-2 building, and there is no fire separation distance between these buildings, then the adjoining exterior walls between the buildings are permitted to have occupant use openings in accordance with Section 706.8. However, opening protectives in such openings shall only be required in the exterior wall of the S-2 parking garage, not in the exterior wall openings in the R-2 building, and these open- ing protectives in the exterior wall of the S-2 parking garage shall be not less than 1 1/2-hour fire protection rating. | drafted:general.bc_705_3_buildings_on_the_same_lot |
| 705.4 | Materials | flag | draft | Exterior walls, an exit stairway's and an exit passageway's among them (1023.7, 1024.8), are of materials the building's type of construction permits: a person's check (general.bc_705_4_exterior_wall_materials). | 705.4 Materials. Exterior walls shall be of materials permitted by the building’s type of construction. | drafted:general.bc_705_4_exterior_wall_materials |
| 705.5 | Fire-resistance ratings | flag | draft | Exterior walls are rated per Table 601 by the type of construction and per Table 705.5 by the fire separation distance; a wall with a fire separation distance of more than 10 feet is rated for exposure to fire from the inside, one of 10 feet or less for exposure to fire from both sides: a person's check (general.bc_705_5_exterior_wall_ratings). | 705.5 Fire-resistance ratings. Exterior walls shall be fire-resistance rated in accordance with Table 601, based on the type of construction, and Table 705.5, based on the fire separation distance. The required fire-resistance rating of exterior walls with a fire separation distance of greater than 10 feet (3048 mm) shall be rated for exposure to fire from the inside. The required fire-resistance rating of exterior walls with a fire separation distance of less than or equal to 10 feet (3048 mm) shall be rated for exposure to fire from both sides. | drafted:general.bc_705_5_exterior_wall_ratings |
| Table 705.5 | FIRE-RESISTANCE RATING REQUIREMENTS FOR EXTERIOR WALLS BASED ON FIRE SEPARATION DISTANCE | flag | draft | Table 705.5 gives an exterior wall's rating in hours by its fire separation distance, the type of construction and the group, with its notes a to i, typed row by row in its flag: a person's check (general.bc_table_705_5_exterior_wall_ratings). | TABLE 705.5—FIRE-RESISTANCE RATING REQUIREMENTS FOR EXTERIOR WALLS BASED ON FIRE SEPARATION DISTANCE [fna,] [fnd,] [fng] FIRE SEPARATION TYPE OF OCCUPANCY GROUP OCCUPANCY GROUP A, OCCUPANCY GROUP H [fne] DISTANCE = X (feet) CONSTRUCTION F-1, M, S-1 [fnf] B, E, F-2, I, R[fni], S-2, U [fnh] X < 5 [fnb] All 3 2 1 IA, IVA 3 2 1 5 ≤ X < 10 Others 2 1 1 IA, IB, IVA, IVB 2 1 1 [fnc] 10 ≤ X < 30 IIB, VB 1 0 0 Others 1 1 1 [fnc] X ≥ 30 All 0 0 0 For SI: 1 foot = 304.8 mm. a.Load-bearing exterior walls shall also comply with the fire-resistance rating requirements of Table 601. b.See Section 706.1.1 for party walls. c. Open parking garages complying with Section 406 shall not be required to have a fire-resistance rating. d.The fire-resistance rating of an exterior wall is determined based upon the fire separation distance of the exterior wall and the story in which the wall is located. e.For special requirements for Group H occupancies, see Section 415.6. f. For special requirements for Group S aircraft hangars, see Section 412.3.1. g.Where Section 705.9.1 permits nonbearing exterior walls with unlimited area of unprotected openings, the required fire-resistance rating for the exterior walls is 0 hours. h.For a building containing only a Group U occupancy private garage or carport, the exterior wall shall not be required to have a fire-resistance rating where the fire separation distance is 5 feet (1523 mm) or greater. i. For a Group R-3 building of Type II-B or Type V-B construction, the exterior wall shall not be required to have a fire-resistance rating where the fire separation distance is 5 feet (1523 mm) or greater. | drafted:general.bc_table_705_5_exterior_wall_ratings |
| 705.6 | Continuity | flag | draft | An exterior wall's fire-resistance rating extends from the top of the foundation or the floor/ceiling assembly below to the underside of the floor sheathing, roof sheathing, deck or slab above, or to the underside of a floor/ceiling or roof/ceiling assembly rated equal to or more than the exterior wall where the fire separation distance is more than 10 feet; parapets are provided as 705.12 requires: a person's check (general.bc_705_6_exterior_wall_continuity). | 705.6 Continuity. The fire-resistance rating of exterior walls shall extend from the top of the foundation or floor/ceiling assembly below to one of the following: 1. The underside of the floor sheathing, roof sheathing, deck or slab above. 2. The underside of a floor/ceiling or roof/ceiling assembly having a fire-resistance rating equal to or greater than the exterior wall and the fire separation distance is greater than 10 feet. Parapets shall be provided as required by Section 705.12. | drafted:general.bc_705_6_exterior_wall_continuity |
| 705.7 | Structural stability | flag | draft | Interior structural elements that brace an exterior wall but are not within its plane have the rating Table 601 requires for that element; structural elements that brace it from outside the wall or within its plane have the rating Table 601 and Table 705.5 require for the exterior wall: a person's check (general.bc_705_7_structural_stability). | 705.7 Structural stability. Interior structural elements that brace the exterior wall but that are not located within the plane of the exterior wall shall have the minimum fire-resistance rating required in Table 601 for that structural element. Structural elements that brace the exterior wall but are located outside of the exterior wall or within the plane of the exterior wall shall have the minimum fire- resistance rating required in Table 601 and Table 705.5 for the exterior wall. | drafted:general.bc_705_7_structural_stability |
| 705.7.1 | Floor assemblies in Type III construction | flag | draft | In Type III construction, where a floor assembly carries gravity loads from an exterior wall, the part of the floor assembly supporting the wall is rated at least as Table 601 requires for the exterior wall (the part supporting it and within its plane may count the ceiling membrane for exposure to fire from the inside), and the building elements of the floor within the wall's plane, rim joists, rim boards and blocking among them, meet the requirements for interior building elements of Type III construction: a person's check (general.bc_705_7_1_type_iii_floor_assemblies). | 705.7.1 Floor assemblies in Type III construction. In Type III construction where a floor assembly supports gravity loads from an exterior wall, the fire-resistance rating of the portion of the floor assembly that supports the exterior wall shall be not less than the fire-resistance rating required for the exterior wall in Table 601. The fire-resistance rating provided by the portion of the floor assembly supporting and within the plane of the exterior wall shall be permitted to include the contribution of the ceiling membrane when considering exposure to fire from the inside. Where a floor assembly supports gravity loads from an exterior wall, the building elements of the floor construction within the plane of the exterior wall, including but not limited to rim joists, rim boards and blocking, shall be in accordance with the requirements for interior building elements of Type III construction. | drafted:general.bc_705_7_1_type_iii_floor_assemblies |
| 705.8 | Unexposed surface temperature | flag | draft | Where protected openings are not limited by 705.9, the limit on the temperature rise of an exterior wall's unexposed surface required by ASTM E119 or UL 263 does not apply; where 705.9 limits them, it does not apply provided a correction is made for radiation from the unexposed wall surface by Equation 7-1, Ae = A + (Af x Feo): Ae the equivalent area of protected openings, A their actual area, Af the area of the wall surface in the story, openings excluded, on which ASTM E119's or UL 263's temperature limits for walls are exceeded, and Feo the equivalent opening factor from Figure 705.8 by the unexposed surface's average temperature and the wall's rating: a person's check (general.bc_705_8_unexposed_surface_temperature). | 705.8 Unexposed surface temperature. Where protected openings are not limited by Section 705.9, the limitation on the rise of temperature on the unexposed surface of exterior walls as required by ASTM E119 or UL 263 shall not apply. Where protected open- ings are limited by Section 705.9, the limitation on the rise of temperature on the unexposed surface of exterior walls as required by ASTM E119 or UL 263 shall not apply provided that a correction is made for radiation from the unexposed exterior wall surface in accordance with the following formula: Equation 7-1 A = A + (A × F ) e f eo where: A = Equivalent area of protected openings. e A = Actual area of protected openings. A = Area of exterior wall surface in the story under consideration exclusive of openings, on which the temperature limitations f of ASTM E119 or UL 263 for walls are exceeded. F = An “equivalent opening factor” derived from Figure 705.8 based on the average temperature of the unexposed wall eo surface and the fire-resistance rating of the wall. | drafted:general.bc_705_8_unexposed_surface_temperature |
| Table 705.9 | MAXIMUM AREA OF EXTERIOR WALL OPENINGS BASED ON FIRE SEPARATION DISTANCE AND DEGREE OF OPENING PROTECTION | flag | draft | Table 705.9 gives the most area of openings in an exterior wall, per story, by the fire separation distance and the openings' protection (UP, S only with a 903.3.1.1 system), with its notes a to k, typed row by row in its flag: a person's check (general.bc_table_705_9_exterior_wall_openings). | TABLE 705.9—MAXIMUM AREA OF EXTERIOR WALL OPENINGS BASED ON FIRE SEPARATION DISTANCE AND DEGREE OF OPENING PROTECTION FIRE SEPARATION DISTANCE (feet) DEGREE OF OPENING PROTECTION ALLOWABLE AREA[fna] Unprotected, Nonsprinklered (UP, NS) Not Permitted[fnk] 0 to less than 3[fnb,] [fnc,] [fnk] Unprotected, Sprinklered (UP, S)[fni] Not Permitted[fnk] Protected (P) Not Permitted[fnk] Unprotected, Nonsprinklered (UP, NS) Not Permitted 3 to less than 5[fnd,] [fne] Unprotected, Sprinklered (UP, S)[fni] 15% Protected (P) 15% Unprotected, Nonsprinklered (UP, NS) 10%[fnh] 5 to less than 10[fne,] [fnf,] [fnj] Unprotected, Sprinklered (UP, S)[fni] 25% Protected (P) 25% Unprotected, Nonsprinklered (UP, NS) 15%[fnh] 10 to less than 15[fne,] [fnf,] [fng,] [fnj] Unprotected, Sprinklered (UP, S)[fni] 45% Protected (P) 45% Unprotected, Nonsprinklered (UP, NS) 25% 15 to less than 20[fnf,] [fng,] [fnj] Unprotected, Sprinklered (UP, S)[fni] 75% Protected (P) 75% Unprotected, Nonsprinklered (UP, NS) 45% 20 to less than 25[fnf,] [fng,] [fnj] Unprotected, Sprinklered (UP, S)[fni] No Limit Protected (P) No Limit Unprotected, Nonsprinklered (UP, NS) 70% 25 to less than 30[fnf,] [fng,] [fnj] Unprotected, Sprinklered (UP, S)[fni] No Limit Protected (P) No Limit | drafted:general.bc_table_705_9_exterior_wall_openings |
| 705.9.1 | Allowable area of openings | flag | draft | The area of unprotected and protected openings in an exterior wall in any story is at most the percentage Table 705.9 gives for that story's own fire separation distance; but, in other than Group H occupancies, unlimited unprotected openings are permitted in the first story above grade plane where the wall faces a street and has a fire separation distance of more than 15 feet, or faces an unoccupied space on the same lot or dedicated for public use, at least 30 feet wide, with access from a street by a posted fire lane per the Fire Code of New York State; and buildings whose exterior bearing walls, exterior nonbearing walls and exterior primary structural frame need no rating may have unlimited unprotected openings: a person's check (general.bc_705_9_1_allowable_opening_area). | 705.9.1 Allowable area of openings. The maximum area of unprotected and protected openings permitted in an exterior wall in any story of a building shall not exceed the percentages specified in Table 705.9 based on the fire separation distance of each indi- vidual story. Exceptions: 1. In other than Group H occupancies, unlimited unprotected openings are permitted in the first story above grade plane where the wall faces one of the following: 1.1. A street and has a fire separation distance of more than 15 feet (4572 mm). 1.2. An unoccupied space. The unoccupied space shall be on the same lot or dedicated for public use, shall be not less than 30 feet (9144 mm) in width and shall have access from a street by a posted fire lane in accor- dance with the Fire Code of New York State. 2. Buildings whose exterior bearing walls, exterior nonbearing walls and exterior primary structural frame are not required to be fire-resistance rated shall be permitted to have unlimited unprotected openings. | drafted:general.bc_705_9_1_allowable_opening_area |
| 705.9.2 | Protected openings | flag | draft | Where openings in an exterior wall must be protected, their opening protectives meet Section 716; but none are required where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system per 903.3.1.1 (an NFPA 13R or 13D system is not one) and the exterior openings are protected by a water curtain of automatic sprinklers approved for that use: a person's check (general.bc_705_9_2_protected_openings). | 705.9.2 Protected openings. Where openings are required to be protected, opening protectives shall comply with Section 716. Exception: Opening protectives are not required where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 and the exterior openings are protected by a water curtain using automatic sprin- klers approved for that use. | drafted:general.bc_705_9_2_protected_openings |
| 705.9.3 | Unprotected openings | flag | draft | Where unprotected openings are permitted, their windows and doors are of any approved materials, the glazing per Chapters 24 and 26: a person's check (general.bc_705_9_3_unprotected_openings). | 705.9.3 Unprotected openings. Where unprotected openings are permitted, windows and doors shall be constructed of any approved materials. Glazing shall conform to the requirements of Chapters 24 and 26. | drafted:general.bc_705_9_3_unprotected_openings |
| 705.9.4 | Mixed openings | flag | draft | Where an exterior wall in any story has both unprotected and protected openings, their total area meets Equation 7-2, (Ap/ap) + (Au/au) at most 1: Ap the actual area of protected openings, or their equivalent area Ae (705.8), ap the allowable area of protected openings, Au the actual area of unprotected openings, au the allowable area of unprotected openings: a person's check (general.bc_705_9_4_mixed_openings). | 705.9.4 Mixed openings. Where both unprotected and protected openings are located in the exterior wall in any story of a build- ing, the total area of openings shall be determined in accordance with the following: Equation 7-2 (A /a ) + (A /a ) ≤ 1 p p u u A = Actual area of protected openings, or the equivalent area of protected openings, A (see Section 705.8). p e a = Allowable area of protected openings. p A = Actual area of unprotected openings. u a = allowable area of unprotected openings. u | drafted:general.bc_705_9_4_mixed_openings |
| 705.9.5 | Vertical separation of openings | flag | draft | Openings in exterior walls in adjacent stories, within 5 feet of each other horizontally where the opening in the lower story is not a protected opening rated at least 3/4 hour, are separated vertically at least 3 feet by spandrel girders, exterior walls or other similar assemblies rated at least 1 hour for exposure to fire from both sides, or by flame barriers extending at least 30 inches beyond the exterior wall, rated at least 1 hour (ASTM E119's or UL 263's unexposed surface temperature limits don't apply to flame barriers unless the code otherwise requires); but not in buildings of three stories or less above grade plane, buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (an NFPA 13R system counts only in Group R, where 903.3.1.2 allows it), or open parking garages: a person's check (general.bc_705_9_5_vertical_separation). | 705.9.5 Vertical separation of openings. Openings in exterior walls in adjacent stories shall be separated vertically to protect against fire spread on the exterior of the buildings where the openings are within 5 feet (1524 mm) of each other horizontally and the opening in the lower story is not a protected opening with a fire protection rating of not less than 3/4 hour. Such openings shall be separated vertically not less than 3 feet (914 mm) by spandrel girders, exterior walls or other similar assemblies that have a fire- resistance rating of not less than 1 hour, rated for exposure to fire from both sides, or by flame barriers that extend horizontally not less than 30 inches (762 mm) beyond the exterior wall. Flame barriers shall have a fire-resistance rating of not less than 1 hour. The unexposed surface temperature limitations specified in ASTM E119 or UL 263 shall not apply to the flame barriers unless otherwise required by the provisions of this code. Exceptions: 1. This section shall not apply to buildings that are three stories or less above grade plane. 2. This section shall not apply to buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. | drafted:general.bc_705_9_5_vertical_separation |
| 705.9.6 | Vertical exposure | flag | draft | For buildings on the same lot, every opening less than 15 feet vertically above the roof of an adjacent building or structure has an opening protective rated at least 3/4 hour, where the fire separation distances from the imaginary line between them to each building or structure are less than 15 feet; but none is required where the adjacent roof assembly is rated at least 1 hour for at least 10 feet from the exterior wall facing the imaginary line and the entire length and span of its supporting elements are rated at least 1 hour, nor for buildings on the same lot considered portions of one building per 705.3: a person's check (general.bc_705_9_6_vertical_exposure). | 705.9.6 Vertical exposure. For buildings on the same lot, opening protectives having a fire protection rating of not less than 3/4 hour shall be provided in every opening that is less than 15 feet (4572 mm) vertically above the roof of an adjacent building or structure based on assuming an imaginary line between them. The opening protectives are required where the fire separation distances from the imaginary line to each building or structure are less than 15 feet (4572 mm). Exceptions: 1. Opening protectives are not required where the roof assembly of the adjacent building or structure has a fire-resis- tance rating of not less than 1 hour for a minimum distance of 10 feet (3048 mm) from the exterior wall facing the imaginary line and the entire length and span of the supporting elements for the fire-resistance-rated roof assembly has a fire-resistance rating of not less than 1 hour. 2. Buildings on the same lot and considered as portions of one building in accordance with Section 705.3 are not required to comply with Section 705.9.6. | drafted:general.bc_705_9_6_vertical_exposure |
| 705.10 | Joints | flag | draft | Joints made in or between exterior walls this section requires to be rated meet Section 715, but not joints in exterior walls permitted to have unprotected openings: a person's check (general.bc_705_10_joints). | 705.10 Joints. Joints made in or between exterior walls required by this section to have a fire-resistance rating shall comply with Section 715. Exception: Joints in exterior walls that are permitted to have unprotected openings. | drafted:general.bc_705_10_joints |
| 705.10.1 | Voids | flag | draft | The void where a floor/ceiling assembly meets an exterior curtain wall assembly is protected per 715.4: a person's check (general.bc_705_10_1_voids). | 705.10.1 Voids. The void created at the intersection of a floor/ceiling assembly and an exterior curtain wall assembly shall be protected in accordance with Section 715.4. | drafted:general.bc_705_10_1_voids |
| 705.11 | Ducts and air transfer openings | flag | draft | Air ducts and air transfer openings penetrating rated exterior walls required to have protected openings meet Section 717; foundation vents installed per the code are permitted: a person's check (general.bc_705_11_ducts). | 705.11 Ducts and air transfer openings. Penetrations by air ducts and air transfer openings in fire-resistance-rated exterior walls required to have protected openings shall comply with Section 717. Exception: Foundation vents installed in accordance with this code are permitted. | drafted:general.bc_705_11_ducts |
| 705.12 | Parapets | flag | draft | Exterior walls of buildings have parapets, except where: the wall needs no rating by Table 705.5 because of its fire separation distance; the building has at most 1,000 square feet on any floor; the walls end at roofs of at least 2-hour rated construction, or at a roof (deck or slab and supporting construction included) entirely of noncombustible materials; 1-hour rated exterior walls end at the underside of the roof sheathing, deck or slab, provided roof/ceiling framing parallel to the wall, and what supports it, is at least 1-hour rated for a width of 4 feet (Groups R and U) or 10 feet (other occupancies) from the wall's interior side, framing not parallel to it, and what supports it, is at least 1-hour rated for its entire span, no roof openings lie within 5 feet (Groups R and U) or 10 feet (other occupancies) of the wall's interior side, and the entire building has at least a Class B roof covering; in Groups R-2 and R-3 with a Class C roof covering on the entire building, in Types III, IV and V, the wall ends at the underside of the roof sheathing or deck where the sheathing or deck is of approved noncombustible materials or fire-retardant-treated wood for 4 feet, or the roof is protected with 0.625-inch Type X gypsum board directly beneath the sheathing or deck, on ledgers of at least nominal 2 inches attached to the sides of the roof framing, for at least 4 feet (one or both); or the wall is permitted at least 25 percent of its area in unprotected openings by fire separation distance per 705.9: a person's check (general.bc_705_12_parapets). | 705.12 Parapets. Parapets shall be provided on exterior walls of buildings. Exceptions: A parapet need not be provided on an exterior wall where any of the following conditions exist: 1. The wall is not required to be fire-resistance rated in accordance with Table 705.5 because of fire separation distance. 2. The building has an area of not more than 1,000 square feet (93 m[fn2]) on any floor. 3. Walls that terminate at roofs of not less than 2-hour fire-resistance-rated construction or where the roof, including the deck or slab and supporting construction, is constructed entirely of noncombustible materials. 4. One-hour fire-resistance-rated exterior walls that terminate at the underside of the roof sheathing, deck or slab, provided that: 4.1. Where the roof/ceiling framing elements are parallel to the walls, such framing and elements supporting such framing shall not be of less than 1-hour fire-resistance-rated construction for a width of 4 feet (1220 mm) for Groups R and U and 10 feet (3048 mm) for other occupancies, measured from the interior side of the wall. 4.2. Where roof/ceiling framing elements are not parallel to the wall, the entire span of such framing and elements supporting such framing shall not be of less than 1-hour fire-resistance-rated construction. 4.3. Openings in the roof shall not be located within 5 feet (1524 mm) of the 1-hour fire-resistance-rated exterior wall for Groups R and U and 10 feet (3048 mm) for other occupancies, measured from the interior side of the wall. 4.4. The entire building shall be provided with not less than a Class B roof covering. 5. In Groups R-2 and R-3 where the entire building is provided with a Class C roof covering, the exterior wall shall be permit- ted to terminate at the underside of the roof sheathing or deck in Types III, IV and V construction, provided that one or both of the following criteria is met: 5.1. The roof sheathing or deck is constructed of approved noncombustible materials or of fire-retardant-treated wood for a distance of 4 feet (1220 mm). 5.2. The roof is protected with 0.625-inch (16 mm) Type X gypsum board directly beneath the underside of the roof sheathing or deck, supported by not less than nominal 2-inch (51 mm) ledgers attached to the sides of the roof framing members for a minimum distance of 4 feet (1220 mm). 6. Where the wall is permitted to have not less than 25 percent of the exterior wall areas containing unprotected openings based on fire separation distance as determined in accordance with Section 705.9. | drafted:general.bc_705_12_parapets |
| 705.12.1 | Parapet construction | flag | draft | A required parapet has the same rating as the wall supporting it, noncombustible faces for its uppermost 18 inches on any side next to a roof surface (counterflashing and coping materials included), and a height of at least 30 inches above where the roof surface and the wall meet; where the roof slopes toward it at more than 2 units vertical in 12 horizontal (16.7 percent), it extends to the same height as any part of the roof within a fire separation distance where wall openings must be protected, and at least 30 inches: a person's check (general.bc_705_12_1_parapet_construction). | 705.12.1 Parapet construction. Required parapets shall have the same fire-resistance rating as that required for the supporting wall, and on any side adjacent to a roof surface, shall have noncombustible faces for the uppermost 18 inches (457 mm), including counterflashing and coping materials. The height of the parapet shall be not less than 30 inches (762 mm) above the point where the roof surface and the wall intersect. Where the roof slopes toward a parapet at a slope greater than 2 units vertical in 12 units horizontal (16.7-percent slope), the parapet shall extend to the same height as any portion of the roof within a fire separation distance where protection of wall openings is required, but the height shall be not less than 30 inches (762 mm). | drafted:general.bc_705_12_1_parapet_construction |
| 707.3.2 | Interior exit stairway and ramp construction | flag | draft | The fire barrier around an interior exit stairway or ramp has the rating Section 1023 asks: its rating isn't in the model, a person's check (general.bc_707_3_2_exit_stairway_fire_barriers). | 707.3.2 Interior exit stairway and ramp construction. The fire-resistance rating of the fire barrier separating building areas from an interior exit stairway or ramp shall comply with Section 1023.1. | drafted:general.bc_707_3_2_exit_stairway_fire_barriers |
| 707.3.4 | Exit passageway | flag | draft | The fire barrier around an exit passageway has the rating 1024.3 asks: its rating isn't in the model, a person's check (general.bc_707_3_4_exit_passageway_fire_barriers). | 707.3.4 Exit passageway. The fire-resistance rating of the fire barrier separating building areas from an exit passageway shall comply with Section 1024.3. | drafted:general.bc_707_3_4_exit_passageway_fire_barriers |
| 707.3.5 | Horizontal exit | flag | draft | A horizontal exit's separation has the rating Section 1026 asks (1026.2: at least 2 hours; section group 9g's flag): not in the model, a person's check (general.bc_707_3_5_horizontal_exit_separation). | 707.3.5 Horizontal exit. The fire-resistance rating of the separation between building areas connected by a horizontal exit shall comply with Section 1026.1. | drafted:general.bc_707_3_5_horizontal_exit_separation |
| 707.4 | Exterior walls | flag | draft | An exterior wall that is part of a shaft, stairway, ramp or exit passageway enclosure follows Section 705 instead, with its exceptions: a person's check (general.bc_707_4_exterior_walls). | 707.4 Exterior walls. Where exterior walls serve as a part of a required fire-resistance-rated shaft, or separation or enclosure for a stairway, ramp or exit passageway, such walls shall comply with the requirements of Section 705 for exterior walls and the fire-resis- tance-rated enclosure or separation requirements shall not apply. Exceptions: 1.
