Covered mall and open mall buildings shall be equipped throughout with a stand- pipe system where required by Section 905.3.1. Mall buildings not required to be equipped with a standpipe system by Section 905.3.1 shall be equipped with Class I hose connections connected to the automatic sprinkler system sized to deliver water at 250 gallons per minute (946.4 L/min) at the hydraulically most remote hose connection while concurrently supplying the automatic sprinkler system demand. The standpipe system shall be designed to not exceed a 50 pounds per square inch (psi) (345 kPa) resid- ual pressure loss with a flow of 250 gallons per minute (946.4 L/min) from the fire department connection to the hydraulically most remote hose connection. Hose connections shall be provided at each of the following locations: 1. Within the mall at the entrance to each exit passageway or corridor. 2. At each floor-level landing within interior exit stairways opening directly on the mall. 3. At exterior public entrances to the mall of a covered mall building. 4. At public entrances at the perimeter line of an open mall building. 5. At other locations as necessary so that the distance to reach all portions of a tenant space does not exceed 200 feet (60 960 mm) from a hose connection. | drafted:general.bc_905_3_3_mall_standpipes |
| 905.3.4 | Underground buildings | flag | draft | An underground building has a Class I automatic wet or manual wet standpipe system throughout: a person's check (general.bc_905_3_4_underground_standpipes). | [F] 905.3.4 Underground buildings. Underground buildings shall be equipped throughout with a Class I automatic wet or manual wet standpipe system. | drafted:general.bc_905_3_4_underground_standpipes |
| 905.3.5 | Helistops and heliports | flag | draft | A building with a rooftop helistop or heliport has a Class I or III standpipe extended to that roof per Section 2007.5 of the Fire Code of New York State: a person's check (general.bc_905_3_5_helistop_standpipes). | [F] 905.3.5 Helistops and heliports. Buildings with a rooftop helistop or heliport shall be equipped with a Class I or III standpipe system extended to the roof level on which the helistop or heliport is located in accordance with Section 2007.5 of the Fire Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_905_3_5_helistop_standpipes |
| 905.3.6 | Marinas and boatyards | flag | draft | Any standpipes in marinas and boatyards, required or not (901.2), comply with Chapter 36 of the Fire Code of New York State: a person's check (general.bc_905_3_6_marina_standpipes). | [F] 905.3.6 Marinas and boatyards. Standpipes in marinas and boatyards shall comply with Chapter 36 of the Fire Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_905_3_6_marina_standpipes |
| 905.3.7 | Vegetative roof and landscaped roof standpipe systems | flag | draft | A building with a landscaped or vegetative roof and a standpipe system has it extended to that roof, any standpipe system provided (901.2): a person's check (general.bc_905_3_7_roof_garden_standpipes). | [F] 905.3.7 Vegetative roof and landscaped roof standpipe systems. Buildings or structures that have landscaped roofs or vegetative roofs and that are equipped with a standpipe system shall have the standpipe system extended to the roof level on which the landscaped roof or vegetative roof is located. | drafted:general.bc_905_3_7_roof_garden_standpipes |
| 905.4 | Location of Class I standpipe hose connections | flag | draft | Where a Class I or Class III standpipe system is installed (Class III through 905.6 and 905.6.1), required or not (901.2), Class I hose connections in every required interior or exterior exit stairway, one for each story above and below grade plane at the main floor landing unless the fire code official approves otherwise (one in the open corridor or breezeway between open stairs 75 feet or less apart), on each side of a horizontal exit's wall and in every exit passageway at its entrance from other areas (neither where a 30-foot hose stream on 100 feet of hose from a stairway's connection reaches the floor areas next to it), at a mall's public entrances and its exit passageway and corridor entrances, at the roof or the highest landing with roof access per 1011.12 where the roof's slope is under 4 in 12, and more where the fire code official asks (a floor's most remote point over 150 feet from one, 200 feet sprinklered): a person's check (general.bc_905_4_class_i_hose_connections). | [F] 905.4 Location of Class I standpipe hose connections. Class I standpipe hose connections shall be provided in all of the follow- ing locations: 1. In every required interior exit stairway or exterior exit stairway, a hose connection shall be provided for each story above and below grade plane. Hose connections shall be located at the main floor landing unless otherwise approved by the fire code official. Exception: A single hose connection shall be permitted to be installed in the open corridor or open breezeway between open stairs that are not greater than 75 feet (22 860 mm) apart. 2. On each side of the wall adjacent to the exit opening of a horizontal exit. Exception: Where floor areas adjacent to a horizontal exit are reachable from an interior exit stairway or exterior exit stairway hose connection by a 30-foot (9144 mm) hose stream from a nozzle attached to 100 feet (30 480 mm) of hose, a hose connection shall not be required at the horizontal exit. 3. In every exit passageway, at the entrance from the exit passageway to other areas of a building. Exception: Where floor areas adjacent to an exit passageway are reachable from an interior exit stairway or exterior exit stairway hose connection by a 30-foot (9144 mm) hose stream from a nozzle attached to 100 feet (30 480 mm) of hose, a hose connection shall not be required at the entrance from the exit passageway to other areas of the building. 4. In covered mall buildings, adjacent to each exterior public entrance to the mall and adjacent to each entrance from an exit passageway or exit corridor to the mall. In open mall buildings, adjacent to each public entrance to the mall at the perimeter line and adjacent to each entrance from an exit passageway or exit corridor to the mall. 5. Where the roof has a slope less than 4 units vertical in 12 units horizontal (33.3-percent slope), a hose connection shall be located to serve the roof or at the highest landing of an interior exit stairway with access to the roof provided in accordance with Section 1011.12. 6. Where the most remote portion of a nonsprinklered floor or story is more than 150 feet (45 720 mm) from a hose connection or the most remote portion of a sprinklered floor or story is more than 200 feet (60 960 mm) from a hose connection, the fire code official is authorized to require that additional hose connections be provided in approved locations. | drafted:general.bc_905_4_class_i_hose_connections |
| 905.4.1 | Protection | flag | draft | Where a Class I or Class III standpipe system is installed (Class III through 905.6 and 905.6.1), required or not (901.2), its risers and laterals not in an interior exit stairway have the fire resistance the building's vertical enclosures need (laterals left unenclosed in a building sprinklered throughout): a person's check (general.bc_905_4_1_standpipe_protection). | [F] 905.4.1 Protection. Risers and laterals of Class I standpipe systems not located within an interior exit stairway shall be protected by a degree of fire resistance equal to that required for vertical enclosures in the building in which they are located. Exception: In buildings equipped throughout with an approved automatic sprinkler system, laterals that are not located within an interior exit stairway are not required to be enclosed within fire-resistance-rated construction. | drafted:general.bc_905_4_1_standpipe_protection |
| 905.4.2 | Interconnection | flag | draft | Where a building has more than one standpipe, they are interconnected per NFPA 14, any standpipe system provided (901.2): a person's check (general.bc_905_4_2_standpipe_interconnection). | [F] 905.4.2 Interconnection. In buildings where more than one standpipe is provided, the standpipes shall be interconnected in accordance with NFPA 14. | drafted:general.bc_905_4_2_standpipe_interconnection |
| 907.1.3 | Equipment | flag | draft | Fire alarm systems and components listed and approved for their purpose: the equipment isn't in the model, a person's check (general.bc_907_1_3_listed_equipment). | [F] 907.1.3 Equipment. Systems and components shall be listed and approved for the purpose for which they are installed. | drafted:general.bc_907_1_3_listed_equipment |
| 907.2 | Where required—new buildings and structures | flag | draft | Where Sections 907.2.1 to 907.2.23 require a fire alarm system, it is approved, installed per this code and NFPA 72, and gives occupant notification per 907.5; one with automatic detectors or waterflow detection has at l...: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_where_required). | [F] 907.2 Where required—new buildings and structures. An approved fire alarm system installed in accordance with the provi- sions of this code and NFPA 72 shall be provided in new buildings and structures in accordance with Sections 907.2.1 through 907.2.23 and provide occupant notification in accordance with Section 907.5, unless other requirements are provided by another section of this code. Not fewer than one manual fire alarm box shall be provided in an approved location to initiate a fire alarm signal for fire alarm systems employing automatic fire detectors or waterflow detection devices. Where other sections of this code allow elimination of fire alarm boxes due to sprinklers, a single fire alarm box shall be installed. Exceptions: 1. The manual fire alarm box is not required for fire alarm systems dedicated to elevator recall control and supervisory service. 2. The manual fire alarm box is not required for Group R-2 occupancies unless required by the fire code official to provide a means for fire watch personnel to initiate an alarm during a sprinkler system impairment event. Where provided, the manual fire alarm box shall not be located in an area that is open to the public. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_where_required |
| 907.2.1 | Group A | flag | draft | A manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5 is installed where the assembly occupant load is 300 or more, or the Group A occupant load is more than 100 above or below the lowest level of exit di...: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_1_group_a_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.1 Group A. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group A occupancies where the occupant load due to the assembly occupancy is 300 or more, or where the Group A occupant load is more than 100 persons above or below the lowest level of exit discharge. Group A occupancies not sepa- rated from one another in accordance with Section 707.3.10 shall be considered as a single occupancy for the purposes of applying this section. Portions of Group E occupancies occupied for assembly purposes shall be provided with a fire alarm system as required for the Group E occupancy. Exceptions: 1. Manual fire alarm boxes are not required where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 and the occupant notification appliances will activate through- out the notification zones upon sprinkler water flow. 2. Manual fire alarm boxes and the associated occupant notification system or emergency voice/alarm communication system are not required for Group A-5 outdoor bleacher-type seating having an occupant load of greater than or equal to 300 and less than 15,000 occupants, provided that all of the following are met: 2.1. A public address system with standby power is provided. 2.2. Enclosed spaces attached to or within 5 feet (1524 mm) of the outdoor bleacher-type seating compose, in the aggregate, a maximum of 10 percent of the overall area of the outdoor bleacher-type seating or 1,000 square feet (92.9 m[fn2]), whichever is less. 2.3. Enclosed accessory spaces under or attached to the outdoor bleacher-type seating shall be separated from the bleacher-type seating in accordance with Section 1030.1.1.1. 2.4. All means of egress from the bleacher-type seating are open to the outside. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_1_group_a_fire_alarm, drafted:general.bc_907_2_1_1_group_a_voice_alarm |
| 907.2.1.1 | System initiation in Group A occupancies with an occupant load of 1,000 or more | rule | draft | An emergency voice/alarm communication system for a Group A occupancy of 1,000 or more: a rule per room on the stated group and system (general.bc_907_2_1_1_group_a_voice_alarm); in a building of two or more groups a person's check (general.bc_907_2_1_1_mixed_voice_alarm). | [F] 907.2.1.1 System initiation in Group A occupancies with an occupant load of 1,000 or more. Activation of the fire alarm in Group A occupancies with an occupant load of 1,000 or more shall initiate a signal using an emergency voice/alarm communi- cations system in accordance with Section 907.5.2.2. Exception: Where approved, the prerecorded announcement is allowed to be manually deactivated for a period of time, not to exceed 3 minutes, for the sole purpose of allowing a live voice announcement from an approved, constantly attended location. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_1_1_group_a_voice_alarm, drafted:general.bc_907_2_1_1_mixed_voice_alarm |
| 907.2.1.2 | Emergency voice/alarm communication captions | flag | draft | Stadiums, arenas and grandstands required to caption audible public announcements do so per 907.5.2.2.4: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_1_2_captions). | [F] 907.2.1.2 Emergency voice/alarm communication captions. Stadiums, arenas and grandstands required to caption audible public announcements shall be in accordance with Section 907.5.2.2.4. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_1_2_captions |
| 907.2.1.3 | Automatic smoke detection system | flag | draft | New York requires an automatic smoke detection system activating occupant notification per 907.5 in Group A, unless the fire area has a 903.3.1.1 sprinkler system that activates the occupant notification system: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_1_3_group_a_smoke_detection). | [NY] 907.2.1.3 Automatic smoke detection system. An automatic smoke detection system that activates the occupant notifi- cation system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group A occupancies unless the fire area is protected with an automatic sprinkler system, that activates the occupant notification system, installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_1_3_group_a_smoke_detection |
| 907.2.2 | Group B | flag | draft | A manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5 is installed in Group B where the Group B occupant load of all floors together is 500 or more, more than 100 are above or below the lowest level of ex...: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_2_group_b_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.2 Group B. A manual fire alarm system, which activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5, shall be installed in Group B occupancies where one of the following conditions exists: 1. The combined Group B occupant load of all floors is 500 or more. 2. The Group B occupant load is more than 100 persons above or below the lowest level of exit discharge. 3. The fire area contains an ambulatory care facility. Exception: Manual fire alarm boxes are not required where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 and the occupant notification appliances will activate throughout the notification zones upon sprinkler water flow. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_2_group_b_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.2.1 | Ambulatory care facilities | flag | draft | Fire areas holding an ambulatory care facility have an electronically supervised automatic smoke detection system in the facility and in public use areas outside tenant spaces (public corridors, elevator lobbies), unless...: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_2_1_ambulatory_care_detection). | [F] 907.2.2.1 Ambulatory care facilities. Fire areas containing ambulatory care facilities shall be provided with an electroni- cally supervised automatic smoke detection system installed within the ambulatory care facility and in public use areas outside of tenant spaces, including public corridors and elevator lobbies. Exception: Buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1, provided that the occupant notification appliances will activate throughout the notification zones upon sprinkler water flow. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_2_1_ambulatory_care_detection |
| 907.2.2.2 | Laboratories involving research and development or testing | flag | draft | A fire area used for research and development or testing of lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries has a fire alarm system activated by air-sampling smoke detection or radiant-energy-sensing detection throughout: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_2_2_battery_laboratory_detection). | [F] 907.2.2.2 Laboratories involving research and development or testing. A fire alarm system activated by an air- sampling-type smoke detection system or a radiant-energy-sensing detection system shall be installed throughout the entire fire area utilized for the research and development or testing of lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_2_2_battery_laboratory_detection |
| 907.2.3 | Group E | rule | draft | Group E has a manual fire alarm system initiating notification through an emergency voice/alarm communication system per 907.5.2.2: the voice/alarm system, a stated fact, is a rule per story by the occupancy's occupant load for a building stated Group E alone (general.bc_907_2_3_group_e_voice_alarm); the manual fire alarm system and its exceptions, and the voice/alarm system in a building of two or more groups, are a person's check (general.bc_907_2_3_group_e_manual_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.3 Group E. A manual fire alarm system that initiates the occupant notification signal utilizing an emergency voice/alarm communication system meeting the requirements of Section 907.5.2.2 and installed in accordance with Section 907.6 shall be installed in Group E occupancies. Where automatic sprinkler systems or smoke detectors are installed, such systems or detectors shall be connected to the building fire alarm system. Exceptions: 1. A manual fire alarm system shall not be required in Group E occupancies with an occupant load of 50 or less. 2. Emergency voice/alarm communication systems meeting the requirements of Section 907.5.2.2 and installed in accordance with Section 907.6 shall not be required in Group E occupancies with occupant loads of 100 or less, provided that activation of the manual fire alarm system initiates an approved occupant notification signal in accor- dance with Section 907.5. 3. Manual fire alarm boxes shall not be required in Group E occupancies where all of the following apply: 3.1. Interior corridors are protected by smoke detectors. 3.2. Auditoriums, cafeterias, gymnasiums and similar areas are protected by heat detectors or other approved detection devices. 3.3. Shops and laboratories involving dusts or vapors are protected by heat detectors or other approved detec- tion devices. 3.4. Manual activation is provided from a normally occupied location. 4. Manual fire alarm boxes shall not be required in Group E occupancies where all of the following apply: 4.1. The building is equipped throughout with an approved automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. 4.2. The emergency voice/alarm communication system will activate on sprinkler waterflow. 4.3. Manual activation is provided from a normally occupied location. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_3_group_e_manual_fire_alarm, drafted:general.bc_907_2_3_group_e_voice_alarm |
| 907.2.4 | Group F | flag | draft | A manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5 is installed in Group F of two or more stories with a combined occupant load of 500 or more above or below the lowest level of exit discharge; boxes m...: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_4_group_f_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.4 Group F. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group F occupancies where both of the following conditions exist: | drafted:general.bc_907_2_4_group_f_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.4.1 | Manufacturing involving lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries | flag | draft | A fire area where lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries are made, or vehicles, energy storage systems or equipment holding them, has a fire alarm system activated by air-sampling smoke detection or radiant-energy detect...: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_4_1_battery_manufacturing). | [F] 907.2.4.1 Manufacturing involving lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries. A fire alarm system activated by an air- sampling-type smoke detection system or a radiant-energy-sensing detection system shall be installed throughout the entire fire area where lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries are manufactured; and where the manufacturer of vehicles, energy stor- age systems or equipment containing lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries when the batteries are installed as part of the manufacturing process. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_4_1_battery_manufacturing |
| 907.2.5 | Group H | flag | draft | A manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5 in Group H-5 and where organic coatings are made, and automatic smoke detection for highly toxic gases, organic peroxides and oxidizers per Chapters 6...: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_5_group_h_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.5 Group H. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group H-5 occupancies and in occupancies used for the manufacture of organic coatings. An automatic smoke detection system shall be installed for highly toxic gases, organic peroxides and oxidizers in accordance with Chapters 60, 62 and 63, respectively, of the Fire Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_5_group_h_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.6 | Group I | flag | draft | Group I has a manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5, and automatic smoke detection per 907.2.6.1, 907.2.6.2 and 907.2.6.3.3 (exception 1: Group I-1 and I-2 sleeping units' boxes not required at exits where they are at all care providers' control stations or other constantly attended staff locations, visible, with ready access, within 907.4.2.1's travel distances; exception 2: occupant notification needn't be activated where the fire code official approves private mode signaling per NFPA 72 and the fire safety and evacuation plan per Section 404 of the Fire Code of New York State includes staff evacuation): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_group_i_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.6 Group I. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group I occupancies. An automatic smoke detection system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be provided in accordance with Sections 907.2.6.1, 907.2.6.2 and 907.2.6.3.3. Exceptions: 1. Manual fire alarm boxes in sleeping units of Group I-1 and I-2 occupancies shall not be required at exits if located at all care providers’ control stations or other constantly attended staff locations, provided that such manual fire alarm boxes are visible and provided with ready access, and the distances of travel required in Section 907.4.2.1 are not exceeded. 2. Occupant notification systems are not required to be activated where private mode signaling installed in accordance with NFPA 72 is approved by the fire code official and staff evacuation responsibilities are included in the fire safety and evacuation plan required by Section 404 of the Fire Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_group_i_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.6.1 | Group I-1 | flag | draft | Group I-1 has automatic smoke detection activated per 907.5 in corridors, waiting areas open to corridors and habitable spaces other than sleeping units and kitchens (not in habitable spaces of a Condition 1 facility with a 903.3.1.1 system; not on exterior balconies): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_1_group_i1_smoke_detection). | [F] 907.2.6.1 Group I-1. In Group I-1 occupancies, an automatic smoke detection system shall be installed in corridors, waiting areas open to corridors and habitable spaces other than sleeping units and kitchens. The system shall be activated in accor- dance with Section 907.5. Exceptions: 1. For Group I-1, Condition 1 occupancies, smoke detection in habitable spaces is not required where the facility is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. 2. Smoke detection is not required for exterior balconies. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_1_group_i1_smoke_detection |
| 907.2.6.1.1 | Smoke alarms | flag | draft | Group I-1 has single- and multiple-station smoke alarms per 907.2.11: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_1_1_group_i1_smoke_alarms). | [F] 907.2.6.1.1 Smoke alarms. Single- and multiple-station smoke alarms shall be installed in accordance with Section 907.2.11. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_1_1_group_i1_smoke_alarms |
| 907.2.6.2 | Group I-2 | flag | draft | Group I-2 Condition 1 has automatic smoke detection in corridors and the spaces 407.2 lets open to them, activated per 907.4; Condition 2 as Section 407 requires (corridor detection not needed in smoke compartments whose sleeping units have UL 268 detectors with a visual display on the corridor side and an audible and visual alarm at the care providers' station, or door-closing devices with integral smoke detectors on the unit sides installed per their listing that perform the required alerting function): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_2_group_i2_smoke_detection). | [F] 907.2.6.2 Group I-2. An automatic smoke detection system shall be installed in corridors in Group I-2, Condition 1 facilities and spaces permitted to be open to the corridors by Section 407.2. The system shall be activated in accordance with Section 907.4. Group I-2, Condition 2 occupancies shall be equipped with an automatic smoke detection system as required in Section 407. Exceptions: 1. Corridor smoke detection is not required in smoke compartments that contain sleeping units where such units are provided with smoke detectors that comply with UL 268. Such detectors shall provide a visual display on the corri- dor side of each sleeping unit and shall provide an audible and visual alarm at the care providers’ station attending each unit. 2. Corridor smoke detection is not required in smoke compartments that contain sleeping units where sleeping unit doors are equipped with automatic door-closing devices with integral smoke detectors on the unit sides installed in accordance with their listing, provided that the integral detectors perform the required alerting function. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_2_group_i2_smoke_detection |
| 907.2.6.3 | Group I-3 occupancies | flag | draft | Group I-3 has a manual fire alarm system and automatic smoke detection installed for alerting staff: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_3_group_i3_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.6.3 Group I-3 occupancies. Group I-3 occupancies shall be equipped with a manual fire alarm system and automatic smoke detection system installed for alerting staff. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_3_group_i3_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.6.3.1 | System initiation | flag | draft | In Group I-3, an extinguishing system, sprinkler system, manual fire alarm box or fire detector initiates an approved signal that automatically notifies staff: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_3_1_group_i3_initiation). | [F] 907.2.6.3.1 System initiation. Actuation of an automatic fire-extinguishing system, automatic sprinkler system, a manual fire alarm box or a fire detector shall initiate an approved fire alarm signal that automatically notifies staff. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_3_1_group_i3_initiation |
| 907.2.6.3.2 | Manual fire alarm boxes | flag | draft | In Group I-3, manual boxes needn't be located per 907.4.2 where they are at staff-attended locations directly supervising the areas left without them: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_3_2_group_i3_alarm_boxes). | [F] 907.2.6.3.2 Manual fire alarm boxes. Manual fire alarm boxes are not required to be located in accordance with Section 907.4.2 where the fire alarm boxes are provided at staff-attended locations having direct supervision over areas where manual fire alarm boxes have been omitted. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_3_2_group_i3_alarm_boxes |
| 907.2.6.3.2.1 | Manual fire alarm boxes in areas occupied by detained individuals | flag | draft | New York allows manual boxes locked in areas occupied by detained individuals, with staff present and keys readily available: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_3_2_1_group_i3_locked_boxes). | [NY] 907.2.6.3.2.1 Manual fire alarm boxes in areas occupied by detained individuals. Manual fire alarm boxes are allowed to be locked in areas occupied by detained individuals, provided that staff members are present within the subject area and have keys readily available to operate the manual fire alarm boxes. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_3_2_1_group_i3_locked_boxes |
| 907.2.6.3.3 | Automatic smoke detection system | flag | draft | Group I-3 has automatic smoke detection throughout resident housing areas, sleeping units and contiguous day rooms, group activity spaces and other common spaces normally open to residents (other approved arrangements giving equivalent protection where needed to prevent damage or tampering; not in Use Conditions 2 and 3 sleeping units per Section 308; not in sleeping units with four or fewer occupants in smoke compartments with a 903.3.1.1 system): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_6_3_3_group_i3_smoke_detection). | [F] 907.2.6.3.3 Automatic smoke detection system. An automatic smoke detection system shall be installed throughout resident housing areas, including sleeping units and contiguous day rooms, group activity spaces and other common spaces normally open to residents. Exceptions: 1. Other approved smoke detection arrangements providing equivalent protection, including, but not limited to, placing detectors in exhaust ducts from cells or behind protective guards listed for the purpose, are allowed where necessary to prevent damage or tampering. 2. Sleeping units in Use Conditions 2 and 3 as described in Section 308. 3. Smoke detectors are not required in sleeping units with four or fewer occupants in smoke compartments that are equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_6_3_3_group_i3_smoke_detection |
| 907.2.7.1 | Occupant load | flag | draft | Group M has a manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5 where the Group M occupant load of all floors together is 500 or more, or more than 100 are above or below the lowest level of exit discharge (not in covered or open mall buildings complying with Section 402; boxes left out with a 903.3.1.1 system that activates notification on water flow): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_7_1_group_m_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.7.1 Occupant load. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group M occupancies where one of the following conditions exists: 1. The combined Group M occupant load of all floors is 500 or more persons. 2. The Group M occupant load is more than 100 persons above or below the lowest level of exit discharge. Exceptions: 1. A manual fire alarm system is not required in covered or open mall buildings complying with Section 402. 2. Manual fire alarm boxes are not required where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 and the occupant notification appliances will automatically activate throughout the notification zones upon sprinkler water flow. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_7_1_group_m_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.7.1.1 | Occupant notification | flag | draft | In Group M, a box or waterflow signal needn't sound the notification appliances while the building is occupied where it alarms at a constantly attended location that starts evacuation over an emergency voice/alarm communication system per 907.5.2.2: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_7_1_1_group_m_notification). | [F] 907.2.7.1.1 Occupant notification. During times that the building is occupied, the initiation of a signal from a manual fire alarm box or from a waterflow switch shall not be required to activate the alarm notification appliances when an alarm signal is activated at a constantly attended location from which evacuation instructions shall be initiated over an emergency voice/alarm communication system installed in accordance with Section 907.5.2.2. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_7_1_1_group_m_notification |
| 907.2.7.2 | Storage of lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries | flag | draft | A room or space in Group M storing lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries where Section 320 of the Fire Code of New York State requires it has a fire alarm system activated by air-sampling or radiant-energy detection: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_7_2_group_m_battery_storage). | [F] 907.2.7.2 Storage of lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries. A fire alarm system activated by an air-sampling-type smoke detection system or a radiant-energy-sensing detection system shall be installed in a room or space within a Group M occu- pancy where required for the storage of lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries in accordance with Section 320 of the Fire Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_7_2_group_m_battery_storage |
| 907.2.8.1 | Manual fire alarm system | flag | draft | Group R-1 has a manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5 (exception 1: not in a building of two stories or less whose units and their contiguous attic and crawl spaces are separated from each other and from public or common areas by 1-hour fire partitions and each have an exit directly to a public way, egress court or yard; exception 2: boxes left out with a 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 system, notification on water flow and at least one box at an approved location): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_8_1_group_r1_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.8.1 Manual fire alarm system. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accor- dance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group R- 1 occupancies. Exceptions: 1. A manual fire alarm system is not required in buildings not more than two stories in height where all individual dwelling units, sleeping units, and contiguous attic and crawl spaces to those units are separated from each other and public or common areas by not less than 1-hour fire partitions and each individual dwelling unit and sleeping unit has an exit directly to a public way, egress court or yard. 2. Manual fire alarm boxes are not required throughout the building where all of the following conditions are met: 2.1. The building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. 2.2. The notification appliances will activate upon sprinkler water flow. 2.3. Not fewer than one manual fire alarm box is installed at an approved location. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_8_1_group_r1_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.8.2 | Automatic smoke detection system | flag | draft | Group R-1 has automatic smoke detection activating notification per 907.5 throughout interior corridors serving dwelling or sleeping units (not where there are no such corridors and each unit's egress door opens directly to an exit or an exterior exit access leading directly to one): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_8_2_group_r1_corridor_detection). | [F] 907.2.8.2 Automatic smoke detection system. An automatic smoke detection system that activates the occupant notifica- tion system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed throughout all interior corridors serving dwelling units or sleeping units. Exception: An automatic smoke detection system is not required in buildings that do not have interior corridors serving dwell- ing units or sleeping units and where each dwelling unit or sleeping unit has a means of egress door opening directly to an exit or to an exterior exit access that leads directly to an exit. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_8_2_group_r1_corridor_detection |
| 907.2.8.3 | Smoke alarms | flag | draft | Group R-1 has single- and multiple-station smoke alarms per 907.2.11: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_8_3_group_r1_smoke_alarms). | [F] 907.2.8.3 Smoke alarms. Single- and multiple-station smoke alarms shall be installed in accordance with Section 907.2.11. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_8_3_group_r1_smoke_alarms |
| 907.2.9.1 | Manual fire alarm system | flag | draft | Group R-2 has a manual fire alarm system activating occupant notification per 907.5 where any unit is three or more stories above the lowest level of exit discharge, more than one story below the highest level of exit discharge of the exits serving it, or the building has more than 16 dwelling or sleeping units (exception 1: not in a building of two stories or less whose units and their contiguous attic and crawl spaces are separated from each other and from public or common areas by 1-hour fire partitions and each have an exit directly to a public way, egress court or yard; exception 2: boxes left out with a 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 system and notification on water flow; exception 3: none with a 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 system where there are no interior corridors serving units and each unit's egress door opens to an exterior exit access leading directly to the exits, or open-ended corridors per 1027.6 exception 3 serve them): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_9_1_group_r2_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.9.1 Manual fire alarm system. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accor- dance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group R-2 occupancies where any of the following conditions apply: 1. Any dwelling unit or sleeping unit is located three or more stories above the lowest level of exit discharge. 2. Any dwelling unit or sleeping unit is located more than one story below the highest level of exit discharge of exits serv- ing the dwelling unit or sleeping unit. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_9_1_group_r2_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.9.2 | Smoke alarms | flag | draft | Group R-2 has single- and multiple-station smoke alarms per 907.2.11: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_9_2_group_r2_smoke_alarms). | [F] 907.2.9.2 Smoke alarms. Single- and multiple-station smoke alarms shall be installed in accordance with Section 907.2.11. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_9_2_group_r2_smoke_alarms |
| 907.2.9.3 | Group R-2 college and university buildings | flag | draft | Group R-2 a college or university runs for student or staff housing has automatic smoke detection activating notification per 907.5 in common spaces outside units, laundry, mechanical equipment and storage rooms, and interior corridors serving units (not where there are no interior corridors and each unit's egress door opens to an exterior exit access leading directly to an exit, or directly to an exit), its units' smoke alarms interconnected with the fire alarm system per NFPA 72: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_9_3_group_r2_college_detection). | [F] 907.2.9.3 Group R-2 college and university buildings. An automatic smoke detection system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group R-2 occupancies operated by a college or university for student or staff housing in all of the following locations: 1. Common spaces outside of dwelling units and sleeping units. 2. Laundry rooms, mechanical equipment rooms and storage rooms. 3. All interior corridors serving sleeping units or dwelling units. Exception: An automatic smoke detection system is not required in buildings that do not have interior corridors serving sleep- ing units or dwelling units and where each sleeping unit or dwelling unit either has a means of egress door opening directly to an exterior exit access that leads directly to an exit or a means of egress door opening directly to an exit. Required smoke alarms in dwelling units and sleeping units in Group R-2 occupancies operated by a college or university for student or staff housing shall be interconnected with the fire alarm system in accordance with NFPA 72. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_9_3_group_r2_college_detection |
| 907.2.10.1 | Public- and self-storage occupancies | flag | draft | Group S public and self-storage three stories or more in height has a manual fire alarm system activating notification per 907.5 for interior corridors and common areas (no visible appliances needed in storage units; boxes left out with a 903.3.1.1 system that activates notification on water flow): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_10_1_storage_fire_alarm). | [F] 907.2.10.1 Public- and self-storage occupancies. A manual fire alarm system that activates the occupant notification system in accordance with Section 907.5 shall be installed in Group S public-and self-storage occupancies three stories or greater in height for interior corridors and interior common areas. Visible notification appliances are not required within stor- age units. Exception: Manual fire alarm boxes are not required where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1, and the occupant notification appliances will activate throughout the notification zones upon sprinkler water flow. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_10_1_storage_fire_alarm |
| 907.2.10.2 | Storage of lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries | flag | draft | Where Section 320 of the Fire Code of New York State requires it for stored lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries, the fire area has a fire alarm system activated by air-sampling or radiant-energy detection throughout, in a building of any group (storage accessory to another occupancy is part of it, 311.1.1): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_10_2_battery_storage_detection). | [F] 907.2.10.2 Storage of lithium-ion or lithium metal batteries. A fire alarm system activated by an air-sampling-type smoke detection system or a radiant-energy-sensing detection system shall be installed throughout the entire fire area where required for the storage of lithium-ion batteries or lithium metal batteries in accordance with Section 320 of the Fire Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_10_2_battery_storage_detection |
| 907.2.11 | Single- and multiple-station smoke alarms | flag | draft | Listed single- and multiple-station smoke alarms complying with UL 217 are installed per 907.2.11.1 to 907.2.11.7, NFPA 72 and the maker's instructions: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_smoke_alarms). | [F] 907.2.11 Single- and multiple-station smoke alarms. Listed single- and multiple-station smoke alarms complying with UL 217 shall be installed in accordance with Sections 907.2.11.1 through 907.2.11.7, NFPA 72 and the manufacturer’s instructions. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_smoke_alarms |
| 907.2.11.1 | Group R-1 | flag | draft | New York places Group R-1's smoke alarms in sleeping areas, in every room on the path of egress from them to the unit's door, on each story within a unit, including basements (a split level's upper alarm serving a lower level less than a full story below with no door between), at least 3 feet horizontally from the door or opening of a bathroom with a tub or shower unless that would prevent a required alarm, in a hallway serving bedrooms and the room open to it where that room's ceiling is 24 inches or more above the hallway's, in the room a sleeping loft opens to, near the loft, in each common space shared by three or more units, clearly audible there, and, on a level of a unit over 1000 square feet of interior floor area, garages excluded, within 30 feet of travel of every ceiling point or one per 500 square feet (upper-floor alarms may serve great rooms and vaulted ceilings): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_1_group_r1_smoke_alarm_locations). | [NY] 907.2.11.1 Group R-1. Single- or multiple-station smoke alarms shall be installed in all of the following locations in Group R- 1: 1. In sleeping areas. 2. In every room in the path of the means of egress from the sleeping area to the door leading from the dwelling unit or sleeping unit. 3. In each story within the dwelling unit or sleeping unit, including basements. For dwelling units or sleeping units with split levels and without an intervening door between the adjacent levels, a smoke alarm installed on the upper level shall suffice for the adjacent lower level provided that the lower level is less than one full story below the upper level. 4. Not less than 3 feet (914 mm) horizontally from the door or opening of a bathroom that contains a bathtub or shower unless this would prevent placement of a smoke alarm required by this section. 5. In the hallway and in the room open to the hallway in dwelling units where the ceiling height of a room open to a hall- way serving bedrooms exceeds that of the hallway by 24 inches (610 mm) or more. 6. Within the room to which a sleeping loft is open, in the immediate vicinity of the sleeping loft. 7. In each common space shared by three or more dwelling units such that an alarm notification is clearly audible in each common space. 8. Where the interior floor area for a given level of a dwelling unit, excluding garage areas, is greater than 1000 ft2 (93 m2), smoke alarms shall be installed per both of the following: | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_1_group_r1_smoke_alarm_locations |
| 907.2.11.2 | Groups R-2, R-3, R-4 and I-1 | flag | draft | New York places smoke alarms in Groups R-2, R-3, R-4 and I-1 outside each separate sleeping area near the bedrooms, in each room used for sleeping, on each story within a unit including basements (not crawl spaces or uninhabitable attics) (a split level's upper alarm serving a lower level less than a full story below with no door between), at least 3 feet horizontally from the door or opening of a bathroom with a tub or shower unless that would prevent a required alarm, in a hallway serving bedrooms and the room open to it where that room's ceiling is 24 inches or more above the hallway's, in the room a sleeping loft opens to, near the loft, in each common space shared by three or more units, clearly audible there, and, on a level of a unit over 1000 square feet of interior floor area, garages excluded, within 30 feet of travel of every ceiling point or one per 500 square feet (upper-floor alarms may serve great rooms and vaulted ceilings): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_2_group_r_smoke_alarm_locations). | [NY] 907.2.11.2 Groups R-2, R-3, R-4 and I-1. Single- or multiple-station smoke alarms shall be installed and maintained in Groups R-2, R-3, R-4 and I-1 regardless of occupant load at all of the following locations: 1. On the ceiling or wall outside of each separate sleeping area in the immediate vicinity of bedrooms. 2. In each room used for sleeping purposes. 3. In each story within a dwelling unit, including basements but not including crawl spaces and uninhabitable attics. In dwellings or dwelling units with split levels and without an intervening door between the adjacent levels, a smoke alarm installed on the upper level shall suffice for the adjacent lower level provided that the lower level is less than one full story below the upper level. 4. Not less than 3 feet (914 mm) horizontally from the door or opening of a bathroom that contains a bathtub or shower unless this would prevent placement of a smoke alarm required by this section. 5. In the hallway and in the room open to the hallway in dwelling units where the ceiling height of a room open to a hall- way serving bedrooms exceeds that of the hallway by 24 inches (610 mm) or more. 6. Within the room to which a sleeping loft is open, in the immediate vicinity of the sleeping loft. 7. In each common space shared by three or more dwelling units such that an alarm notification is clearly audible in each common space. 8. Where the interior floor area for a given level of a dwelling unit, excluding garage areas, is greater than 1000 ft2 (93 m2), smoke alarms shall be installed per both of the following: 8.1 All points on the ceiling shall have a smoke alarm within a distance of 30 ft (9.1 m) travel distance or shall have an equivalent of one smoke alarm per 500 ft2 (46 m2) of floor area. 8.2 Where dwelling units include great rooms or vaulted/cathedral ceilings extending over multiple floors, smoke alarms located on the upper floor that are intended to protect the aforementioned area shall be permitted to be considered as part of the lower floor’s protection scheme. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_1_group_r1_smoke_alarm_locations, drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_2_group_r_smoke_alarm_locations |
| 907.2.11.3 | Installation near cooking appliances | flag | draft | Smoke alarms are at least 10 feet horizontally from a permanently installed cooking appliance, 6 feet where needed to comply with 907.2.11.1 or 907.2.11.2: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_3_smoke_alarms_near_cooking). | [F] 907.2.11.3 Installation near cooking appliances. Smoke alarms shall be installed not less than 10 feet (3048 mm) hori- zontally from a permanently installed cooking appliance. Exception: Smoke alarms shall be permitted to be installed not less than 6 feet (1829 mm) horizontally from a permanently installed cooking appliance where necessary to comply with Section 907.2.11.1 or 907.2.11.2. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_3_smoke_alarms_near_cooking |
| 907.2.11.4 | Installation near bathrooms | flag | draft | Smoke alarms are at least 3 feet horizontally from the door or opening of a bathroom with a tub or shower, unless that would prevent an alarm 907.2.11.1 or 907.2.11.2 requires: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_4_smoke_alarms_near_bathrooms). | [F] 907.2.11.4 Installation near bathrooms. Smoke alarms shall be installed not less than 3 feet (914 mm) horizontally from the door or opening of a bathroom that contains a bathtub or shower unless this would prevent placement of a smoke alarm required by Section 907.2.11.1 or 907.2.11.2. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_4_smoke_alarms_near_bathrooms |
| 907.2.11.5 | Interconnection | flag | draft | Where a unit in Group R or I-1 needs more than one smoke alarm, they are interconnected so one sets off all (listed wireless alarms may do it), clearly audible in all bedrooms over background noise with the doors closed: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_5_smoke_alarm_interconnection). | [F] 907.2.11.5 Interconnection. Where more than one smoke alarm is required to be installed within an individual dwelling unit or sleeping unit in Group R or I-1 occupancies, the smoke alarms shall be interconnected in such a manner that the activa- tion of one alarm will activate all of the alarms in the individual unit. Physical interconnection of smoke alarms shall not be required where listed wireless alarms are installed and all alarms sound upon activation of one alarm. The alarm shall be clearly audible in all bedrooms over background noise levels with all intervening doors closed. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_5_smoke_alarm_interconnection |
| 907.2.11.6 | Power source | flag | draft | In new construction, smoke alarms take their primary power from building wiring where it is served from a commercial source, with battery backup, those with strobes and no battery on an emergency electrical system per 2702 (no battery where on one), signal low batteries, and are wired permanently without a disconnecting switch other than for overcurrent protection: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_6_smoke_alarm_power). | [F] 907.2.11.6 Power source. In new construction, required smoke alarms shall receive their primary power from the building wiring where such wiring is served from a commercial source and shall be equipped with a battery backup. Smoke alarms with integral strobes that are not equipped with battery backup shall be connected to an emergency electrical system in accor- dance with Section 2702. Smoke alarms shall emit a signal when the batteries are low. Wiring shall be permanent and without a disconnecting switch other than as required for overcurrent protection. Exception: Smoke alarms are not required to be equipped with battery backup where they are connected to an emergency electrical system that complies with Section 2702. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_6_smoke_alarm_power |
| 907.2.11.7 | Smoke detection system | flag | draft | Smoke detectors listed per UL 268 in the building fire alarm system may stand in for smoke alarms where the system meets Section 907, a detector in a unit sounds notification in the unit per 907.5.2, and not outside it where a supervisory signal is monitored per 907.6.6: a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_7_smoke_detection_instead). | [F] 907.2.11.7 Smoke detection system. Smoke detectors listed in accordance with UL 268 and provided as part of the build- ing fire alarm system shall be an acceptable alternative to single- and multiple-station smoke alarms and shall comply with the following: 1. The fire alarm system shall comply with all applicable requirements in Section 907. 2. Activation of a smoke detector in a dwelling unit or sleeping unit shall initiate alarm notification in the dwelling unit or sleeping unit in accordance with Section 907.5.2. 3. Activation of a smoke detector in a dwelling unit or sleeping unit shall not activate alarm notification appliances outside of the dwelling unit or sleeping unit, provided that a supervisory signal is generated and monitored in accor- dance with Section 907.6.6. | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_7_smoke_detection_instead |
| 907.2.11.8 | Portable smoke alarms in hotels, motels, and lodging houses | flag | draft | New York asks hotels, motels and lodging houses for portable audible and visual smoke alarms, 3 percent of the sleeping units (at least one per building) per NFPA 72, with a sign posted conspicuously at the main desk or a similar station, its letters at least 3 inches high, saying they are available (not where audible/visual alarms are hard wired, or part of a fire alarm system that activates visual notification): a person's check (general.bc_907_2_11_8_portable_smoke_alarms). | [NY] 907.2.11.8 Portable smoke alarms in hotels, motels, and lodging houses. In addition to any other requirement of this code, portable smoke alarms of both audible and visual design shall be provided in all hotels, motels, and lodging houses. The number of smoke alarms available shall be 3 percent of the number of sleeping units with a minimum of one operational smoke | drafted:general.bc_907_2_11_8_portable_smoke_alarms |
| 907.5.2.2 | Emergency voice/alarm communication systems | flag | draft | An emergency voice/alarm communication system is designed and installed per NFPA 72: a detector, waterflow device or manual box sounds an alert tone and voice instructions for general or staged evacuation per the plans Section 404 of the Fire Code of New York State requires; in high-rise buildings on at least the alarming floor and those above and below; speakers throughout by paging zones, at least for elevator groups, interior exit stairways, each floor and areas of refuge as Chapter 2 defines them (in Groups I-1 and I-2 the alarm in a constantly attended area and a general notification over the overhead page): a person's check (general.bc_907_5_2_2_voice_alarm_design). | [F] 907.5.2.2 Emergency voice/alarm communication systems. Emergency voice/alarm communication systems required by this code shall be designed and installed in accordance with NFPA 72. The operation of any automatic fire detector, sprinkler waterflow device or manual fire alarm box shall automatically sound an alert tone followed by voice instructions giving approved information and directions for a general or staged evacuation in accordance with the building’s fire safety and evacu- ation plans required by Section 404 of the Fire Code of New York State. In high-rise buildings, the system shall operate on at least the alarming floor, the floor above and the floor below. Speakers shall be provided throughout the building by paging zones. At a minimum, paging zones shall be provided as follows: 1. Elevator groups. 2. Interior exit stairways. 3. Each floor. 4. Areas of refuge as defined in Chapter 2. Exception: In Group I-1 and I-2 occupancies, the alarm shall sound in a constantly attended area and a general occupant notification shall be broadcast over the overhead page. | drafted:general.bc_907_5_2_2_voice_alarm_design |
| 907.5.2.2.1 | Manual override | flag | draft | A manual override for voice communication on a selective and all-call basis for all paging zones: a person's check (general.bc_907_5_2_2_1_voice_alarm_override). | [F] 907.5.2.2.1 Manual override. A manual override for emergency voice communication shall be provided on a selective and all-call basis for all paging zones. | drafted:general.bc_907_5_2_2_1_voice_alarm_override |
| 907.5.2.2.2 | Live voice messages | flag | draft | The system can broadcast live voice messages by paging zones, selective and all-call: a person's check (general.bc_907_5_2_2_2_voice_alarm_live_messages). | [F] 907.5.2.2.2 Live voice messages. The emergency voice/alarm communication system shall have the capability to broadcast live voice messages by paging zones on a selective and all-call basis. | drafted:general.bc_907_5_2_2_2_voice_alarm_live_messages |
| 907.5.2.2.3 | Alternative uses | flag | draft | The system may carry other announcements where the manual fire alarm use takes precedence: a person's check (general.bc_907_5_2_2_3_voice_alarm_other_uses). | [F] 907.5.2.2.3 Alternative uses. The emergency voice/alarm communication system shall be allowed to be used for other announcements, provided that the manual fire alarm use takes precedence over any other use. | drafted:general.bc_907_5_2_2_3_voice_alarm_other_uses |
| 907.5.2.2.4 | Emergency voice/alarm communication captions | flag | draft | Where stadiums, arenas and grandstands have 15,000 fixed seats or more and audible public announcements, the system gives prerecorded or real-time captions from an approved location constantly attended by trained personnel: a person's check (general.bc_907_5_2_2_4_voice_alarm_captions). | [F] 907.5.2.2.4 Emergency voice/alarm communication captions. Where stadiums, arenas and grandstands have 15,000 fixed seats or more and provide audible public announcements, the emergency/voice alarm communication system shall provide prerecorded or real-time captions. Prerecorded or live emergency captions shall be from an approved location constantly attended by personnel trained to respond to an emergency. | drafted:general.bc_907_5_2_2_4_voice_alarm_captions |
| 907.5.2.2.5 | Standby power | flag | draft | The system has standby power per Section 2702: a person's check (general.bc_907_5_2_2_5_voice_alarm_standby_power). | [F] 907.5.2.2.5 Standby power. Emergency voice/alarm communications systems shall be provided with standby power in accordance with Section 2702. | drafted:general.bc_907_5_2_2_5_voice_alarm_standby_power |
| 909.1 | Scope and purpose | flag | draft | Where another section requires a mechanical or passive smoke control system (an atrium, smoke-protected assembly seating per 1030.6.2.1, an underground building, a high-rise's smokeproof enclosures among them), it is designed, installed and acceptance-tested per Section 909 for a tenable environment while occupants evacuate or relocate (not 910's smoke and heat removal; not an exhaust system under Chapter 5 of the Mechanical Code of New York State): a person's check (general.bc_909_1_smoke_control_scope). | [F] 909.1 Scope and purpose. This section applies to mechanical or passive smoke control systems where they are required by other provisions of this code. The purpose of this section is to establish minimum requirements for the design, installation and acceptance testing of smoke control systems that are intended to provide a tenable environment for the evacuation or relocation of occupants. These provisions are not intended for the preservation of contents, the timely restoration of operations or for assistance in fire suppression or overhaul activities. Smoke control systems regulated by this section serve a different purpose than the smoke- and heat-removal provisions found in Section 910. Mechanical smoke control systems shall not be considered exhaust systems under Chapter 5 of the Mechanical Code of New York State. | drafted:general.bc_909_1_smoke_control_scope |
| 909.20 | Smokeproof enclosures | flag | draft | Where 1023.12 requires a smokeproof enclosure, it is an interior exit stairway or ramp enclosed per Section 1023 with an open exterior balcony, or a pressurized stair and entrance vestibule, per 909.20, and roof access the Fire Code of New York State requires is from it: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_smokeproof_enclosures). | 909.20 Smokeproof enclosures. Where required by Section 1023.12, a smokeproof enclosure shall be constructed in accordance with this section. A smokeproof enclosure shall consist of an interior exit stairway or ramp that is enclosed in accordance with the applicable provisions of Section 1023 and an open exterior balcony or pressurized stair and pressurized entrance vestibule meeting the requirements of this section. Where access to the roof is required by the Fire Code of New York State, such access shall be from the smokeproof enclosure where a smokeproof enclosure is required. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_smokeproof_enclosures |
| 909.20.1 | Access | flag | draft | The stairway or ramp is reached through a vestibule or open exterior balcony; the vestibule at least the corridor's required clear width, not under 44 inches wide nor 72 inches long in the direction of travel, measured straight between the doorways' centerlines: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_1_smokeproof_access). | 909.20.1 Access. Access to the stairway or ramp shall be by way of a vestibule or an open exterior balcony. The minimum dimen- sion of the vestibule shall be not less than the required clear width of the corridor leading to the vestibule but shall not have a width of less than 44 inches (1118 mm) and shall not have a length of less than 72 inches (1829 mm) in the direction of egress travel into the stairway, measured in a straight line between the centerline of the doorways into the vestibule and stairway. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_1_smokeproof_access |
| 909.20.2 | Construction | flag | draft | The enclosure is separated from the rest of the building, and the vestibule from the stairway or ramp, by 2-hour fire barriers per 707 or horizontal assemblies per 711, no openings but the required egress doors, an open exterior balcony built to the floor assemblies' rating: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_2_smokeproof_construction). | 909.20.2 Construction. The smokeproof enclosure shall be separated from the remainder of the building by not less than 2-hour fire barriers constructed in accordance with Section 707 or horizontal assemblies constructed in accordance with Section 711, or | drafted:general.bc_909_20_2_smokeproof_construction |
| 909.20.2.1 | Door closers | flag | draft | Its doors are self-closing or close automatically on a smoke detector per 716.2.6.6 at the floor-side entrance, any one detector closing every door of the enclosure at all levels, detectors per 907.3: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_2_1_smokeproof_door_closers). | 909.20.2.1 Door closers. Doors in a smokeproof enclosure shall be self- or automatic closing by actuation of a smoke detector in accordance with Section 716.2.6.6 and shall be installed at the floor-side entrance to the smokeproof enclosure. The actua- tion of the smoke detector on any door shall activate the closing devices on all doors in the smokeproof enclosure at all levels. Smoke detectors shall be installed in accordance with Section 907.3. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_2_1_smokeproof_door_closers |
| 909.20.3.1 | Balcony doors | flag | draft | In a smokeproof enclosure ventilated by natural means (909.20.3), where an open exterior balcony leads to the stairway or ramp, the door into the enclosure is a fire door assembly per 716: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_3_1_smokeproof_balcony_doors). | 909.20.3.1 Balcony doors. Where access to the stairway or ramp is by way of an open exterior balcony, the door assembly into the enclosure shall be a fire door assembly in accordance with Section 716. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_3_1_smokeproof_balcony_doors |
| 909.20.3.2 | Vestibule doors | flag | draft | In a smokeproof enclosure ventilated by natural means (909.20.3), where a vestibule leads to the stairway or ramp, the door into the vestibule is a fire door assembly per 716, the door from the vestibule to the stairway at least 20 minutes per 716: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_3_2_smokeproof_vestibule_doors). | 909.20.3.2 Vestibule doors. Where access to the stairway or ramp is by way of a vestibule, the door assembly into the vesti- bule shall be a fire door assembly complying with Section 716. The door assembly from the vestibule to the stairway shall have not less than a 20-minute fire protection rating complying with Section 716. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_3_2_smokeproof_vestibule_doors |
| 909.20.3.3 | Vestibule ventilation | flag | draft | In a smokeproof enclosure ventilated by natural means (909.20.3), each vestibule has at least 16 square feet of net opening in a wall facing an outer court, yard or public way at least 20 feet wide: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_3_3_smokeproof_vestibule_ventilation). | 909.20.3.3 Vestibule ventilation. Each vestibule shall have a minimum net area of 16 square feet (1.5 m[fn2]) of opening in a wall facing an outer court, yard or public way that is not less than 20 feet (6096 mm) in width. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_3_3_smokeproof_vestibule_ventilation |
| 909.20.4 | Stairway and ramp pressurization alternative | flag | draft | With a 903.3.1.1 system throughout, the vestibule may be left out where each interior exit stairway or ramp is pressurized to between 0.10 and 0.35 inch of water relative to the building with its doors closed under the worst stack and wind effect: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_4_stair_pressurization). | 909.20.4 Stairway and ramp pressurization alternative. Where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprin- kler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1, the vestibule is not required, provided that each interior exit stairway or ramp is pressurized to not less than 0.10 inch of water (25 Pa) and not more than 0.35 inches of water (87 Pa) in the shaft relative to the building measured with all interior exit stairway and ramp doors closed under maximum anticipated conditions of stack effect and wind effect. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_4_stair_pressurization |
| 909.20.5.1 | Vestibule doors | flag | draft | In a pressurized stair and vestibule (909.20.5), the door into the vestibule is a fire door assembly per 716.2.2.1; the door from the vestibule to the stairway at least 20 minutes and a smoke door assembly per 716.2.2.1, installed per NFPA 105: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_5_1_pressurized_vestibule_doors). | 909.20.5.1 Vestibule doors. The door assembly from the building into the vestibule shall be a fire door assembly complying with Section 716.2.2.1. The door assembly from the vestibule to the stairway shall have not less than a 20-minute fire protection rating and meet the requirements for a smoke door assembly in accordance with Section 716.2.2.1. The door shall be installed in accordance with NFPA 105. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_5_1_pressurized_vestibule_doors |
| 909.20.5.2 | Pressure difference | flag | draft | In a pressurized stair and vestibule (909.20.5), the stair is at least 0.05 inch of water above the vestibule with all stairway doors closed under the maximum anticipated stack pressures, and the vestibule at least 0.05 inch above the fire floor with all doors closed, at most 30 pounds of force to begin opening a door: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_5_2_pressure_difference). | 909.20.5.2 Pressure difference. The stair enclosure shall be pressurized to not less than 0.05 inch of water gage (12.44 Pa) positive pressure relative to the vestibule with all stairway doors closed under the maximum anticipated stack pressures. The vestibule, with doors closed, shall have not less than 0.05 inch of water gage (12.44 Pa) positive pressure relative to the fire floor. The pressure difference across doors shall not exceed 30 pounds (133-N) maximum force to begin opening the door. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_5_2_pressure_difference |
| 909.20.5.3 | Dampered relief opening | flag | draft | A controlled relief vent discharging at least 2,500 cubic feet per minute at the design pressure difference in the upper part of the pressurized exit enclosure: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_5_3_relief_opening). | 909.20.5.3 Dampered relief opening. A controlled relief vent having the capacity to discharge not less than 2,500 cubic feet per minute (1180 L/s) of air at the design pressure difference shall be located in the upper portion of the pressurized exit enclosure. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_5_3_relief_opening |
| 909.20.5.4 | Smoke detection | flag | draft | The fan system has a smoke detector that shuts it down when smoke is detected within it: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_5_4_fan_smoke_detection). | 909.20.5.4 Smoke detection. The fan system shall be equipped with a smoke detector that will automatically shut down the fan system when smoke is detected within the system. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_5_4_fan_smoke_detection |
| 909.20.6 | Ventilating equipment | flag | draft | Smoke detectors at each floor, at an approved location at the enclosure's entrance, per 907.3, start the ventilating equipment 909.20.4 and 909.20.5 require, which runs at its required performance when the doors' closers act on smoke or power failure: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_6_ventilating_equipment). | 909.20.6 Ventilating equipment. The activation of ventilating equipment required by the alternatives in Sections 909.20.4 and 909.20.5 shall be by smoke detectors installed at each floor level at an approved location at the entrance to the smokeproof enclo- sure. When the closing device for the stairway and ramp shaft and vestibule doors is activated by smoke detection or power failure, the mechanical equipment shall activate and operate at the required performance levels. Smoke detectors shall be installed in accordance with Section 907.3. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_6_ventilating_equipment |
| 909.20.6.1 | Ventilation systems | flag | draft | The enclosure's ventilation is independent of other systems, its equipment, control and power wiring and ductwork outside the building and connected to the enclosure directly or by ductwork enclosed by 2-hour fire barriers per 707 or horizontal assemblies per 711; or within the enclosure, its intake or exhaust directly from and to the outside or through ductwork so enclosed; or within the building, separated from the rest of it, other mechanical equipment included, by 2-hour fire barriers or horizontal assemblies (wiring outside them protected by 2-hour UL 2196 cables, 2 inches of concrete, or 2-hour electrical circuit protective systems installed per their listing): a person's check (general.bc_909_20_6_1_ventilation_systems). | 909.20.6.1 Ventilation systems. Smokeproof enclosure ventilation systems shall be independent of other building ventilation systems. The equipment, control wiring, power wiring and ductwork shall comply with one of the following: 1. Equipment, control wiring, power wiring and ductwork shall be located exterior to the building and directly connected to the smokeproof enclosure or connected to the smokeproof enclosure by ductwork enclosed by not less than 2-hour fire barriers constructed in accordance with Section 707 or horizontal assemblies constructed in accor- dance with Section 711, or both. 2. Equipment, control wiring, power wiring and ductwork shall be located within the smokeproof enclosure with intake or exhaust directly from and to the outside or through ductwork enclosed by not less than 2-hour fire barriers constructed in accordance with Section 707 or horizontal assemblies constructed in accordance with Section 711, or both. 3. Equipment, control wiring, power wiring and ductwork shall be located within the building if separated from the remainder of the building, including other mechanical equipment, by not less than 2-hour fire barriers constructed in accordance with Section 707 or horizontal assemblies constructed in accordance with Section 711, or both. Exception: 1. Control wiring and power wiring located outside of a 2-hour fire barrier construction shall be protected using any one of the following methods: | drafted:general.bc_909_20_6_1_ventilation_systems |
| 909.20.6.2 | Standby power | flag | draft | The vestibule and shaft ventilation and automatic fire detection have standby power per Section 2702: a person's check (general.bc_909_20_6_2_smokeproof_standby_power). | 909.20.6.2 Standby power. Mechanical vestibule and stairway and ramp shaft ventilation systems and automatic fire detec- tion systems shall be provided with standby power in accordance with Section 2702. | drafted:general.bc_909_20_6_2_smokeproof_standby_power |
| 911.1 | General | flag | draft | Where another section requires one, in a high-rise building, and in Group F-1 and S-1 occupancies with a building footprint over 500,000 square feet, a fire command center for fire department operations is provided per 911.1.1 to 911.1.7 (approved location and access, a 1-hour separation, its size, an approved layout, no other storage, the features NFPA 72 and 911.1.6 list, from the voice/alarm control unit and stair door unlocking to the building information card, and its sign): a person's check (general.bc_911_1_fire_command_center). | [F] 911.1 General. Where required by other sections of this code, in buildings classified as high-rise buildings by this code and in all F- 1 and S-1 occupancies with a building footprint of over 500,000 square feet (46 452 m[fn2]), a fire command center for fire department operations shall be provided and shall comply with Sections 911.1.1 through 911.1.7. | drafted:general.bc_911_1_fire_command_center |
| 1003.2 | Ceiling height | flag | draft | The means of egress has a ceiling at least 7 feet 6 inches above the finished floor, except sloped ceilings and the ceilings of dwelling and sleeping units in residential occupancies (each per 1208.2), projections 1003.3 allows, stair headroom (1011.3), door height (1010.1.1), ramp headroom (1012.5.2), the clear height of parking garages' traffic areas (406.2.2) and areas above and below mezzanines (505.2): ceiling heights along the means of egress aren't measured, so a person's check. | 1003.2 Ceiling height. The means of egress shall have a ceiling height of not less than 7 feet 6 inches (2286 mm) above the finished floor. Exceptions: 1. Sloped ceilings in accordance with Section 1208.2. 2. Ceilings of dwelling units and sleeping units within residential occupancies in accordance with Section 1208.2. 3. Allowable projections in accordance with Section 1003.3. 4. Stair headroom in accordance with Section 1011.3. 5. Door height in accordance with Section 1010.1.1. 6. Ramp headroom in accordance with Section 1012.5.2. 7. The clear height of floor levels in vehicular and pedestrian traffic areas of public and private parking garages in accor- dance with Section 406.2.2. 8. Areas above and below mezzanine floors in accordance with Section 505.2. | drafted:general.bc_1003_2_ceiling_height |
| 1003.3 | Protruding objects | reference | draft | A pointer: protruding objects comply with 1003.3.1 to 1003.3.4, each labeled on its own; quoted by 1003.3.1's flag. | 1003.3 Protruding objects. Protruding objects on circulation paths shall comply with the requirements of Sections 1003.3.1 through 1003.3.4. | drafted:general.bc_1003_3_1_headroom |
| 1003.3.1 | Headroom | flag | draft | Protruding objects on circulation paths may hang below 1003.2's ceiling height only where at least 80 inches of headroom is kept over every circulation path (walks, corridors, aisles and passageways), and they lower not more than half the ceiling area of a means of egress; door closers, overhead doorstops, frame stops, power door operators and electromagnetic locks may reach down to 78 inches in a door opening; where the clearance over a circulation path is less than 80 inches, a barrier is provided with its leading edge at most 27 inches above the floor: protruding objects aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1003.3.1 Headroom. Protruding objects are permitted to extend below the minimum ceiling height required by Section 1003.2 where a minimum headroom of 80 inches (2032 mm) is provided over any circulation paths, including walks, corridors, aisles and passageways. Not more than 50 percent of the ceiling area of a means of egress shall be reduced in height by protruding objects. Exception: Door closers, overhead doorstops, frame stops, power door operators and electromagnetic door locks shall be permitted to project into the door opening height not lower than 78 inches (1980 mm) above the floor. | drafted:general.bc_1003_3_1_headroom |
| 1003.3.2 | Post-mounted objects | flag | draft | An object on a post or pylon overhangs it at most 4 inches where its leading edge's lowest point is more than 27 and less than 80 inches above the floor; a sign or other obstruction between posts or pylons more than 12 inches apart has its lowest edge at most 27 or at least 80 inches above the floor or ground (the sloping handrails of stairs and ramps aside): such objects aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1003.3.2 Post-mounted objects. A free-standing object mounted on a post or pylon shall not overhang that post or pylon more than 4 inches (102 mm) where the lowest point of the leading edge is more than 27 inches (686 mm) and less than 80 inches (2032 mm) above the finished floor. Where a sign or other obstruction is mounted between posts or pylons and the clear distance between the posts or pylons is greater than 12 inches (305 mm), the lowest edge of such sign or obstruction shall be 27 inches (686 mm) maximum or 80 inches (2032 mm) minimum above the finished floor or ground. Exception: These requirements shall not apply to sloping portions of handrails between the top and bottom riser of stairs and above the ramp run. | drafted:general.bc_1003_3_2_post_mounted_objects |
