| 1003.3.3 | Horizontal projections | flag | draft | Objects with leading edges more than 27 and not more than 80 inches above the floor project at most 4 inches into the circulation path (handrails up to 4 1/2 inches from the wall or guard): wall-mounted objects aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1003.3.3 Horizontal projections. Objects with leading edges more than 27 inches (685 mm) and not more than 80 inches (2030 mm) above the finished floor shall not project horizontally more than 4 inches (102 mm) into the circulation path. Exception: Handrails are permitted to protrude 4 1/2 inches (114 mm) from the wall or guard. | drafted:general.bc_1003_3_3_horizontal_projections |
| 1003.3.4 | Clear width | flag | draft | Protruding objects don't reduce the minimum clear width of an accessible route: protruding objects and accessible routes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1003.3.4 Clear width. Protruding objects shall not reduce the minimum clear width of accessible routes. | drafted:general.bc_1003_3_4_clear_width |
| 1003.4 | Slip-resistant surface | flag | draft | The circulation paths of the means of egress have a slip-resistant surface, securely attached: floor finishes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1003.4 Slip-resistant surface. Circulation paths of the means of egress shall have a slip-resistant surface and be securely attached. | drafted:general.bc_1003_4_slip_resistant_surface |
| 1003.5 | Elevation change | flag | draft | A change in elevation of less than 12 inches in the means of egress is made by a sloped surface, a ramp complying with 1012 where steeper than 1 in 20 (5 percent), with handrails or a contrasting floor finish where 6 inches or less; the exceptions are steps at exterior doors per 1010.1.4, a stair of one riser or of two risers and a tread where Chapter 11 doesn't require access (risers and treads per 1011.5, treads at least 13 inches deep, a handrail per 1014 within 30 inches of the centerline of the path), and a step under 12 inches in aisles serving seating where Chapter 11 doesn't require access (risers and treads per 1030.14, a handrail per 1030.16); throughout a story in Group I-2, any change in elevation in the means of egress serving nonambulatory persons is a ramp or sloped walkway: the routes read each story as one level, so changes in floor level within it aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1003.5 Elevation change. Where changes in elevation of less than 12 inches (305 mm) exist in the means of egress, sloped surfaces shall be used. Where the slope is greater than one unit vertical in 20 units horizontal (5-percent slope), ramps complying with Section 1012 shall be used. Where the difference in elevation is 6 inches (152 mm) or less, the ramp shall be equipped with either handrails or floor finish materials that contrast with adjacent floor finish materials. Exceptions: 1. Steps at exterior doors complying with Section 1010.1.4. 2. A stair with a single riser or with two risers and a tread is permitted at locations not required to be accessible by Chapter 11 where the risers and treads comply with Section 1011.5, the minimum depth of the tread is 13 inches (330 mm) and not less than one handrail complying with Section 1014 is provided within 30 inches (762 mm) of the centerline of the normal path of egress travel on the stair. 3. A step is permitted in aisles serving seating that has a difference in elevation less than 12 inches (305 mm) at locations not required to be accessible by Chapter 11, provided that the risers and treads comply with Section 1030.14 and the aisle is provided with a handrail complying with Section 1030.16. Throughout a story in a Group I-2 occupancy, any change in elevation in portions of the means of egress that serve nonambulatory persons shall be by means of a ramp or sloped walkway. | drafted:general.bc_1003_5_elevation_change |
| 1003.6 | Means of egress continuity | flag | draft | The path of egress travel isn't interrupted by any building element other than a means of egress component; nothing but the projections this chapter permits obstructs a component's minimum width or required capacity; and the minimum width or required capacity of the means of egress system isn't diminished along the path of egress travel: the routes measure the corridors' clear width between the walls and what is drawn (Table 1020.3's rule), not every component's width or capacity along the path, so a person's check. | 1003.6 Means of egress continuity. The path of egress travel along a means of egress shall not be interrupted by a building element other than a means of egress component as specified in this chapter. Obstructions shall not be placed in the minimum width or required capacity of a means of egress component except projections permitted by this chapter. The minimum width or required capacity of a means of egress system shall not be diminished along the path of egress travel. | drafted:general.bc_1003_6_means_of_egress_continuity |
| 1003.7 | Elevators, escalators and moving walks | flag | draft | Elevators, escalators and moving walks are not used as a component of a required means of egress from any other part of the building, except elevators used as an accessible means of egress per 1009.4: which ways out the architect counts on beyond the doors and stairs the routes read isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1003.7 Elevators, escalators and moving walks. Elevators, escalators and moving walks shall not be used as a component of a required means of egress from any other part of the building. Exception: Elevators used as an accessible means of egress in accordance with Section 1009.4. | drafted:general.bc_1003_7_elevators_escalators_moving_walks |
| 1004.7 | Outdoor areas | flag | draft | Outdoor areas (yards, patios, occupiable roofs, courts) need means of egress and an occupant load the building official assigns: not drawn, a person's check (general.bc_1004_7_outdoor_areas). | 1004.7 Outdoor areas. Yards, patios, occupiable roofs, courts and similar outdoor areas accessible to and usable by the building occupants shall be provided with means of egress as required by this chapter. The occupant load of such outdoor areas shall be assigned by the building official in accordance with the anticipated use. Where outdoor areas are to be used by persons in addition to | drafted:general.bc_1004_7_outdoor_areas |
| 1004.9 | Posting of occupant load | flag | draft | Posting an assembly room's occupant load: a sign in the building, not in the model: a person's check (general.bc_1004_9_posting). | 1004.9 Posting of occupant load. Every room or space that is an assembly occupancy shall have the occupant load of the room or space posted in a conspicuous place, near the main exit or exit access doorway from the room or space, for the intended configurations. Posted signs shall be of an approved legible permanent design and shall be maintained by the owner or the owner’s authorized agent. | drafted:general.bc_1004_9_posting |
| 1005.3.1 | Stairways | flag | draft | A stairway's capacity: 0.3 inch per occupant of the story it serves (0.2 with a sprinkler system per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 and an emergency voice/alarm system, other than Groups H and I-2; assembly seating's own factors): the stairways' widths aren't measured, so a person's check. | 1005.3.1 Stairways. The capacity, in inches, of means of egress stairways shall be calculated by multiplying the occupant load served by such stairways by a means of egress capacity factor of 0.3 inch (7.6 mm) per occupant. Where stairways serve more than one story, only the occupant load of each story considered individually shall be used in calculating the required capacity of the stairways serving that story. | drafted:general.bc_1005_3_1_stairway_capacity |
| 1005.3.2 | Other egress components | rule | draft | The capacity of an egress component other than a stairway: 0.2 inch per occupant served (0.15 with a sprinkler system per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 and an emergency voice/alarm system, other than Groups H and I-2; assembly seating's own factors): measurable for a corridor (its width and the occupants it serves, the rule "Corridor capacity, 0.2 or 0.15 in per occupant"; a 903.3.1.2 system counts where 903.3.1.2 allows it, in Group R); doors, ramps and exit passageways aren't measured, a flag. | 1005.3.2 Other egress components. The capacity, in inches, of means of egress components other than stairways shall be calcu- lated by multiplying the occupant load served by such component by a means of egress capacity factor of 0.2 inch (5.1 mm) per occupant. | drafted:general.bc_1020_3_corridor_capacity, drafted:general.bc_1005_3_2_other_components |
| 1005.4 | Continuity | flag | draft | The width or capacity required from a story never narrows along the path of egress travel to the public way: the routes measure each story's spaces and corridors, not the exits and discharge beyond them, so a person's check. | 1005.4 Continuity. The minimum width or required capacity of the means of egress required from any story of a building shall not be reduced along the path of egress travel until arrival at the public way. | drafted:general.bc_1005_4_continuity |
| 1005.5 | Distribution of minimum width and required capacity | flag | draft | Where more than one exit is required, losing any one exit (or access to it) leaves at least half the required capacity or width: the exits' widths aren't measured, so a person's check. | 1005.5 Distribution of minimum width and required capacity. Where more than one exit, or access to more than one exit, is required, the means of egress shall be configured such that the loss of any one exit, or access to one exit, shall not reduce the avail- able capacity or width to less than 50 percent of the required capacity or width. | drafted:general.bc_1005_5_distribution |
| 1005.6 | Egress convergence | flag | draft | Where egress from the stories above and below meets at an intermediate level, the capacity from there on is the larger of the widest minimum or the two stories' stairways' or ramps' required capacities added: the stairways aren't measured, so a person's check. | 1005.6 Egress convergence. Where the means of egress from stories above and below converge at an intermediate level, the capac- ity of the means of egress from the point of convergence shall be not less than the largest minimum width or the sum of the required capacities for the stairways or ramps serving the two adjacent stories, whichever is larger. | drafted:general.bc_1005_6_convergence |
| 1005.7 | Encroachment | reference | draft | A pointer: encroachments into the required width follow 1005.7.1 to 1005.7.3, each labeled on its own. | 1005.7 Encroachment. Encroachments into the required means of egress width shall be in accordance with the provisions of this section. | drafted:general.bc_1005_7_1_door_encroachment, drafted:general.bc_1005_7_2_projections, drafted:general.bc_1005_7_3_protruding_objects |
| 1005.7.1 | Doors | flag | draft | A door fully open takes at most 7 inches of the required width, in any position at most half (latch hardware 34 to 48 inches up aside; not inside an R-2 dwelling or sleeping unit or an R-3 dwelling unit): the doors' swing into a corridor isn't drawn, so a person's check. | 1005.7.1 Doors. Doors, when fully opened, shall not reduce the required width by more than 7 inches (178 mm). Doors in any position shall not reduce the required width by more than one-half. | drafted:general.bc_1005_7_1_door_encroachment |
| 1005.7.2 | Other projections | flag | draft | Handrails project as 1014.9 allows, trim and similar at most 1 1/2 inches each side (Group I-2 Condition 1 corridors per 407.4.3): projections aren't drawn, so a person's check. | 1005.7.2 Other projections. Handrail projections shall be in accordance with the provisions of Section 1014.9. Other nonstruc- tural projections such as trim and similar decorative features shall be permitted to project into the required width not more than 1 1/2 inches (38 mm) on each side. Exception: Projections are permitted in corridors within Group I-2, Condition 1 in accordance with Section 407.4.3. | drafted:general.bc_1005_7_2_projections |
| 1005.7.3 | Protruding objects | flag | draft | Protruding objects meet 1003.3 (brought in by section group 9a, each of 1003.3.1 to 1003.3.4 its own flag): a person's check. | 1005.7.3 Protruding objects. Protruding objects shall comply with the applicable requirements of Section 1003.3. | drafted:general.bc_1005_7_3_protruding_objects |
| 1006.2.1 | Egress based on occupant load and common path of egress travel distance | rule | draft | Two exits or exit access doorways from any space whose design occupant load or common path of egress travel distance exceeds Table 1006.2.1's values: measurable per space. | 1006.2.1 Egress based on occupant load and common path of egress travel distance. Two exits or exit access doorways from any space shall be provided where the design occupant load or the common path of egress travel distance exceeds the values listed in Table 1006.2.1. The cumulative occupant load from adjacent rooms, areas or spaces shall be determined in accordance | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_1_two_exits, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_a_e_m, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_f, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h1_h2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h4_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i1_i2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r1, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r3_r4, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_s, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_u, drafted:general.bc_1007_1_1_space_remoteness |
| Table 1006.2.1 | SPACES WITH ONE EXIT OR EXIT ACCESS DOORWAY | rule | draft | The values 1006.2.1 uses, row by row: the most occupants and the longest common path a space with one exit may have in each group, without an automatic sprinkler system (by occupant load up to 30 and over 30, or not permitted) and with one (note a: 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2; note b: Group H's 903.2.5). | I-1, I-2[fnd], I-4 10 NP NP 75[fna] | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_1_two_exits, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_other_occupancies, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_a_e_m, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_f, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h1_h2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h4_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i1_i2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r1, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r3_r4, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_s, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_u, drafted:general.bc_1007_1_1_space_remoteness, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_note_d_group_h |
| 1006.2.1.1 | Three or more exits or exit access doorways | rule | draft | Three exits or exit access doorways from a space of 501 to 1,000 occupants, four above 1,000: measurable per space. | 1006.2.1.1 Three or more exits or exit access doorways. Three exits or exit access doorways shall be provided from any space with an occupant load of 501 to 1,000. Four exits or exit access doorways shall be provided from any space with an occu- pant load greater than 1,000. | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_1_1_three_or_more_exits, drafted:general.bc_1007_1_2_space_three_or_more |
| 1006.2.2 | Egress based on use | reference | draft | Points to 1006.2.2.1 to 1006.2.2.6, the uses whose exits are set by their use: a pointer, no requirement of its own. | 1006.2.2 Egress based on use. The numbers, configuration and types of components of exits or access to exits shall be provided in the uses described in Sections 1006.2.2.1 through 1006.2.2.6. | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_1_two_exits, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_a_e_m, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_f, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h1_h2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h4_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i1_i2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r1, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r3_r4, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_s, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_u |
| 1006.2.2.1 | Boiler, incinerator and furnace rooms | flag | draft | Two exit access doorways for a boiler, incinerator or furnace room over 500 square feet with fuel-fired equipment over 400,000 Btu input, one-half the diagonal apart: the room's use isn't marked, so a person's check (general.bc_1006_2_2_1_boiler_rooms). | 1006.2.2.1 Boiler, incinerator and furnace rooms. Two exit access doorways are required in boiler, incinerator and furnace rooms where the area is over 500 square feet (46 m[fn2]) and any fuel-fired equipment exceeds 400,000 British thermal units (Btu) (422 000 KJ) input capacity. Where two exit access doorways are required, one is permitted to be a fixed ladder or an alternating | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_1_two_exits, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_a_e_m, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_f, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h1_h2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h4_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i1_i2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r1, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r3_r4, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_s, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_u, drafted:general.bc_1006_2_2_1_boiler_rooms |

| 1006.3.4 | Single exits | reference | draft | Says when a story may have a single exit (item 1: Table 1006.3.4(2)'s values; items 2 to 5: rooms that exit directly outside, mechanical parking, Groups R-3 and R-4, dwelling units): a permission the story rules read (general.bc_1006_3_3_story_exits, general.bc_1007_1_1_remoteness); where an item a rule doesn't measure may apply, they need review, never fail. | 1006.3.4 Single exits. Where | drafted:general.bc_1006_3_4_1_mixed_occupancies |
| 1007.1 | General | reference | draft | Says exits, exit access doorways and exit access stairways and ramps are separated as this section says: no requirement of its own. | 1007.1 General. Exits, exit access doorways, and exit access stairways and ramps serving spaces, including individual building stories, shall be separated in accordance with the provisions of this section. | drafted:general.bc_1007_1_1_space_remoteness |
| 1007.1.1 | Two exits or exit access doorways | rule | draft | Two required exits or exit access doorways at least one-half of the maximum overall diagonal apart, one-third in a building sprinklered throughout (903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2): measurable per story. | 1007.1.1 Two exits or exit access doorways. Where two exits, exit access doorways, exit access stairways or ramps, or any combi- nation thereof, are required from any portion of the exit access, they shall be placed a distance apart equal to not less than one- half of the length of the maximum overall diagonal dimension of the building or area to be served measured in a straight line | drafted:general.bc_1007_1_1_remoteness, drafted:general.bc_1007_1_1_space_remoteness, drafted:general.bc_1007_1_2_space_three_or_more |
| 1007.1.1.1 | Measurement point | reference | draft | Says where the separation distance is measured to (any point of a doorway's width, an exit access stairway's closest riser, a ramp's start): how to measure, no limit. | 1007.1.1.1 Measurement point. The separation distance required in Section 1007.1.1 shall be measured in accordance with the following: 1. The separation distance to exit or exit access doorways shall be measured to any point along the width of the doorway. | drafted:general.bc_1007_1_1_space_remoteness |
| 1007.1.2 | Three or more exits or exit access doorways | rule | draft | Where three or more exits are required, two of them are arranged as 1007.1.1 says (measurable per story); the others a reasonable distance apart (a person's judgment). | 1007.1.2 Three or more exits or exit access doorways. Where access to three or more exits is required, not less than two exit or exit access doorways shall be arranged in accordance with the provisions of Section 1007.1.1. Additional required exit or exit access doorways shall be arranged a reasonable distance apart so that if one becomes blocked, the others will be available. | drafted:general.bc_1007_1_2_three_or_more_exits, drafted:general.bc_1007_1_2_space_three_or_more |
| 1007.1.3 | Remoteness of exit access stairways or ramps | flag | draft | Two exit access stairways or ramps to another story keep the required separation for all their length: not measured from the drawing yet, a person checks. | 1007.1.3 Remoteness of exit access stairways or ramps. Where two exit access stairways or ramps provide the required means of egress to exits at another story, the required separation distance shall be maintained for all portions of such exit access stair- ways or ramps. | drafted:general.bc_1007_1_3_exit_access_stairways |
| 1007.1.3.1 | Three or more exit access stairways or ramps | flag | draft | Of more than two exit access stairways or ramps, two are arranged as 1007.1.3 says: a person checks. | 1007.1.3.1 Three or more exit access stairways or ramps. Where more than two exit access stairways or ramps provide the required means of egress, not less than two shall be arranged in accordance with Section 1007.1.3. | drafted:general.bc_1007_1_3_1_three_or_more_stairways |
| 1008.1 | Means of egress illumination | reference | draft | A pointer: means of egress lighting follows 1008.2 and, on a power failure, 1008.3, each labeled on its own; quoted by 1008.2's flag. | 1008.1 Means of egress illumination. Illumination shall be provided in the means of egress in accordance with Section 1008.2. In the event of power supply failure, means of egress illumination shall comply with Section 1008.3. | drafted:general.bc_1008_2_illumination_required |
| 1008.2 | Illumination required | flag | draft | The means of egress serving a room or space is lit at all times the room or space is occupied (1008.3's emergency lighting on a power failure); not required in Group U, in self-service storage units of 400 sq ft or less entered directly from outside, in Group A's aisle accessways, within dwelling and sleeping units in Groups R-1, R-2 and R-3, or in Group I sleeping units: lighting isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.2 Illumination required. The means of egress serving a room or space shall be illuminated at all times that the room or space is occupied. Exceptions: 1. Occupancies in Group U. 2. Self-service storage units 400 square feet (37.2 m[fn2]) or less in area and accessed directly from the exterior of the building. 3. Aisle accessways in Group A. 4. Dwelling units and sleeping units in Groups R-1, R-2 and R-3. 5. Sleeping units of Group I occupancies. | drafted:general.bc_1008_2_illumination_required |
| 1008.2.1 | Illumination level under normal power | flag | draft | The means of egress is lit to at least 1 footcandle at the walking surface, and exit access stairways, exit stairways and their required landings to at least 10 footcandles while the stairway is in use; auditoriums, theaters, concert or opera halls and similar assembly spaces may lower it during performances, restored automatically by the fire alarm system, to 0.2 footcandle on externally lit walking surfaces, or by marking steps, landings and ramp sides with self-luminous materials (1025.2.1, 1025.2.2 and 1025.2.4, systems listed to UL 1994): lighting isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.2.1 Illumination level under normal power. The means of egress illumination level shall be not less than 1 footcandle (11 lux) at the walking surface. Along exit access stairways, exit stairways and at their required landings, the illumination level shall not be less than 10 footcandles (108 lux) at the walking surface when the stairway is in use. Exception: For auditoriums, theaters, concert or opera halls and similar assembly occupancies, the illumination at the walking surface is permitted to be reduced during performances by one of the following methods provided that the required illumina- tion is automatically restored upon activation of a premises’ fire alarm system: 1. Externally illuminated walking surfaces shall be permitted to be illuminated to not less than 0.2 footcandle (2.15 lux). 2. Steps, landings and the sides of ramps shall be permitted to be marked with self-luminous materials in accordance with Sections 1025.2.1, 1025.2.2 and 1025.2.4 by systems listed in accordance with UL 1994. | drafted:general.bc_1008_2_1_illumination_level |
| 1008.2.2 | Group I-2 | flag | draft | In Group I-2 where two or more exits are required, the exterior landings 1010.1.5 requires are lit for the exit discharge so that one lamp failing in a luminaire leaves at least 1 footcandle on the landing: lighting isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.2.2 Group I-2. In Group I-2 occupancies where two or more exits are required, on the exterior landings required by Section 1010.1.5, means of egress illumination levels for the exit discharge shall be provided such that failure of a single lamp in a lumi- naire shall not reduce the illumination level on that landing to less than 1 footcandle (11 lux). | drafted:general.bc_1008_2_2_group_i2_exterior_landings |
| 1008.2.3 | Exit discharge | flag | draft | The path of the exit discharge is lit from each exit to the public way, or from the exit to a safe dispersal area complying with 1028.5 that is itself lit to at least 1 footcandle at the walking surface: site lighting isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.2.3 Exit discharge. Illumination shall be provided along the path of travel for the exit discharge from each exit to the public way. Exception: Illumination shall not be required where the path of the exit discharge meets both of the following requirements: 1. The path of exit discharge is illuminated from the exit to a safe dispersal area complying with Section 1028.5. 2. A dispersal area shall be illuminated to a level not less than 1 footcandle (11 lux) at the walking surface. | drafted:general.bc_1008_2_3_exit_discharge_illumination |
| 1008.2.4 | Power for illumination | flag | draft | Means of egress lighting is normally powered by the premises' electrical supply: the electrical design isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.2.4 Power for illumination. The power supply for means of egress illumination shall normally be provided by the premises’ electrical supply. | drafted:general.bc_1008_2_4_power_for_illumination |
| 1008.3 | Illumination required by an emergency electrical system | flag | draft | An emergency electrical system lights, on a power failure: in rooms or spaces requiring two or more exits or access to exits, the aisles, corridors and exit access stairways and ramps; in buildings requiring two or more exits or access to exits, the interior exit access stairways and ramps, interior and exterior exit stairways and ramps, exit passageways, vestibules and discharge areas on the level of discharge (1028.2), and the exterior landings 1010.1.5 requires at exit doorways leading directly to the exit discharge; and electrical equipment rooms, fire command centers, fire pump rooms, generator rooms and public restrooms over 300 sq ft: the electrical design isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.3 Illumination required by an emergency electrical system. An emergency electrical system shall be provided to automati- cally illuminate the following areas in the event of a power supply failure: 1. In rooms or spaces that require two or more exits or access to exits: 1.1. Aisles. 1.2. Corridors. 1.3. Exit access stairways and ramps. 2. In buildings that require two or more exits or access to exits: 2.1. Interior exit access stairways and ramps. 2.2. Interior and exterior exit stairways and ramps. 2.3. Exit passageways. 2.4. Vestibules and areas on the level of discharge used for exit discharge in accordance with Section 1028.2. 2.5. Exterior landings as required by Section 1010.1.5 for exit doorways that lead directly to the exit discharge. 3. In other rooms and spaces: 3.1. Electrical equipment rooms. 3.2. Fire command centers. 3.3. Fire pump rooms. 3.4. Generator rooms. 3.5. Public restrooms with an area greater than 300 square feet (27.87 m[fn2]). | drafted:general.bc_1008_3_emergency_illumination |
| 1008.3.1 | Duration | flag | draft | The emergency power system powers the lighting for at least 90 minutes, from storage batteries, unit equipment or an on-site generator, installed per 2702: the electrical design isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.3.1 Duration. The emergency power system shall provide power for a duration of not less than 90 minutes and shall consist of storage batteries, unit equipment or an on-site generator. The installation of the emergency power system shall be in accor- dance with Section 2702. | drafted:general.bc_1008_3_1_emergency_power_duration |
| 1008.3.2 | Illumination level under emergency power | flag | draft | Emergency lighting gives at first an average of at least 1 footcandle and at least 0.1 footcandle at any point along the path of egress at floor level, declining to no less than 0.6 average and 0.06 at any point by the end of its duration, with a maximum-to-minimum ratio no more than 40 to 1; in Group I-2 one lamp failing in a luminaire leaves at least 0.2 footcandle: lighting isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1008.3.2 Illumination level under emergency power. Emergency lighting facilities shall be arranged to provide initial illumina- tion that is not less than an average of 1 footcandle (11 lux) and a minimum at any point of 0.1 footcandle (1 lux) measured along the path of egress at floor level. Illumination levels shall be permitted to decline to 0.6 footcandle (6 lux) average and a minimum at any point of 0.06 footcandle (0.6 lux) at the end of the emergency lighting time duration. A maximum-to-minimum illumination uniformity ratio of 40 to 1 shall not be exceeded. In Group I-2 occupancies, failure of a single lamp in a luminaire shall not reduce the illumination level to less than 0.2 footcandle (2.2 lux). | drafted:general.bc_1008_3_2_emergency_illumination_level |
| 1009.1 | Accessible means of egress required | flag | draft | Every accessible space has at least one accessible means of egress, and where 1006.2 or 1006.3 requires more than one means of egress from it, each accessible portion of it is served by at least two; one will do from an accessible mezzanine (1009.3, 1009.4 or 1009.5) and in assembly areas with ramped or stepped aisles where the common path is accessible and meets 1030.8: which spaces must be accessible (Chapter 11) and their accessible means of egress aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.1 Accessible means of egress required. Accessible means of egress shall comply with this section. Accessible spaces shall be provided with not less than one accessible means of egress. Where more than one means of egress is required by Section 1006.2 or 1006.3 from any accessible space, each accessible portion of the space shall be served by not less than two accessible means of egress. Exceptions: 1. One accessible means of egress is required from an accessible mezzanine level in accordance with Section 1009.3, 1009.4 or 1009.5. 2. In assembly areas with ramped aisles or stepped aisles, one accessible means of egress is permitted where the common path of egress travel is accessible and meets the requirements in Section 1030.8. | drafted:general.bc_1009_1_accessible_means_of_egress_required |
| 1009.2 | Continuity and components | flag | draft | Each required accessible means of egress is continuous to a public way and made of accessible routes (1104), interior exit stairways (1009.3 and 1023), exit access stairways (1009.3 and 1019.3 or 1019.4), exterior exit stairways (1009.3 and 1027) serving levels other than the level of exit discharge, elevators (1009.4), platform lifts (1009.5), horizontal exits (1026), ramps (1012), areas of refuge (1009.6) or exterior areas for assisted rescue (1009.7) serving exits at the level of exit discharge: accessible means of egress aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.2 Continuity and components. Each required accessible means of egress shall be continuous to a public way and shall consist of one or more of the following components: 1. Accessible routes complying with Section 1104. 2. Interior exit stairways complying with Sections 1009.3 and 1023. 3. Exit access stairways complying with Sections 1009.3 and 1019.3 or 1019.4. 4. Exterior exit stairways complying with Sections 1009.3 and 1027 and serving levels other than the level of exit discharge. 5. Elevators complying with Section 1009.4. 6. Platform lifts complying with Section 1009.5. 7. Horizontal exits complying with Section 1026. 8. Ramps complying with Section 1012. 9. Areas of refuge complying with Section 1009.6. 10. Exterior areas for assisted rescue complying with Section 1009.7 serving exits at the level of exit discharge. | drafted:general.bc_1009_2_continuity_and_components |
| 1009.2.1 | Elevators required | flag | draft | Where a required accessible floor is four or more stories above or below a level of exit discharge, or an accessible occupiable roof is above a story three or more stories above it, at least one required accessible means of egress includes an elevator complying with 1009.4; in a building sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 the elevator isn't required on floors with a horizontal exit at or above the levels of exit discharge, or on floors or occupiable roofs with a ramp complying with 1012: which floors must be accessible isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.2.1 Elevators required. In buildings where a required accessible floor is four or more stories above or below a level of exit discharge or where an accessible occupiable roof is above a story that is three or more stories above the level of exit discharge, not less than one required accessible means of egress shall include an elevator complying with Section 1009.4. Exceptions: | drafted:general.bc_1009_2_1_elevators_required |
| 1009.2.2 | Doors | flag | draft | Doors on an accessible route to an exit, area of refuge or exterior area for assisted rescue have ICC A117.1's maneuvering clearances in the direction of egress, on both sides where a door leads to an area of refuge or exterior area for assisted rescue and reentry to the floor is possible; not required at doors to exit stairways above and below the level of exit discharge whose enclosure has no area of refuge: maneuvering clearances aren't measured, so a person's check. | 1009.2.2 Doors. Where doors are part of an accessible route to provide access to an exit, area of refuge or exterior area of assisted rescue, maneuvering clearances shall be provided at such doors as required by ICC A117.1 in the direction of egress. Where doors lead to an area of refuge or exterior area for assisted rescue and reentry to the floor is possible, door maneuvering clearances shall be provided on both sides of the door. Exception: Maneuvering clearances are not required at doors to exit stairways for levels above and below the level of exit discharge where the exit enclosure does not include an area of refuge. | drafted:general.bc_1009_2_2_doors |
| 1009.3 | Stairways | reference | draft | A pointer: a stairway counts toward an accessible means of egress only as 1009.3.1 to 1009.3.3 allow, each labeled on its own; quoted by 1009.3.1's flag. | 1009.3 Stairways. In order to be considered part of an accessible means of egress, a stairway between stories shall comply with Sections 1009.3.1 through 1009.3.3. | drafted:general.bc_1009_3_1_exit_access_stairways |
| 1009.3.1 | Exit access stairways | flag | draft | A stairway between stories counts toward an accessible means of egress only as 1009.3.1 to 1009.3.3 allow (1009.3); exit access stairways connecting levels in the same story don't count (stairways from mezzanines do): which stairways the accessible means of egress relies on isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.3.1 Exit access stairways. Exit access stairways that connect levels in the same story are not permitted as part of an acces- sible means of egress. Exception: Exit access stairways providing means of egress from mezzanines are permitted as part of an accessible means of egress. | drafted:general.bc_1009_3_1_exit_access_stairways |
| 1009.3.2 | Stairway width | flag | draft | A stairway in an accessible means of egress has at least 48 inches clear between handrails; not required in a building sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1), or for stairways reached from a refuge area with a horizontal exit: which stairways serve as an accessible means of egress, and their width between handrails, aren't measured, so a person's check. | 1009.3.2 Stairway width. Stairways shall have a clear width of 48 inches (1219 mm) minimum between handrails. Exceptions: 1. The clear width of 48 inches (1219 mm) between handrails is not required in buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. 2. The clear width of 48 inches (1219 mm) between handrails is not required for stairways accessed from a refuge area in conjunction with a horizontal exit. | drafted:general.bc_1009_3_2_stairway_width |
| 1009.3.3 | Area of refuge | flag | draft | A stairway in an accessible means of egress has an area of refuge within an enlarged floor-level landing or is reached from one complying with 1009.6; not required at exit access stairways with two-way communication at the elevator landing (1009.8), in a building sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1), at open parking garages' stairways, for smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating (1030.6.2 and 1030.6.3), in Group R-2, or for stairways reached from a refuge area with a horizontal exit: areas of refuge aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.3.3 Area of refuge. Stairways shall either incorporate an area of refuge within an enlarged floor-level landing or shall be accessed from an area of refuge complying with Section 1009.6. Exceptions: 1. Areas of refuge are not required at exit access stairways where two-way communication is provided at the elevator landing in accordance with Section 1009.8. 2. Areas of refuge are not required at stairways in buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. 3. Areas of refuge are not required at stairways serving open parking garages. 4. Areas of refuge are not required for smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating areas complying with Sections 1030.6.2 and 1030.6.3. 5. Areas of refuge are not required at stairways in Group R-2 occupancies. 6. Areas of refuge are not required for stairways accessed from a refuge area in conjunction with a horizontal exit. | drafted:general.bc_1009_3_3_area_of_refuge_at_stairways |
| 1009.4 | Elevators | reference | draft | A pointer: an elevator counts toward an accessible means of egress only as 1009.4.1 and 1009.4.2 allow, each labeled on its own; quoted by 1009.4.1's flag. | 1009.4 Elevators. In order to be considered part of an accessible means of egress, an elevator shall comply with Sections 1009.4.1 and 1009.4.2. | drafted:general.bc_1009_4_1_elevator_standby_power |
| 1009.4.1 | Standby power | flag | draft | An elevator counts toward an accessible means of egress only as 1009.4.1 and 1009.4.2 allow (1009.4); it meets the emergency operation and signaling requirements of ASME A17.1/CSA B44 Section 2.27, with standby power per Chapter 27 and 3003: elevators aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.4.1 Standby power. The elevator shall meet the emergency operation and signaling device requirements of Section 2.27 of ASME A17.1/CSA B44. Standby power shall be provided in accordance with Chapter 27 and Section 3003. | drafted:general.bc_1009_4_1_elevator_standby_power |
| 1009.4.2 | Area of refuge | flag | draft | An elevator in an accessible means of egress is reached from an area of refuge complying with 1009.6; not required in open parking garages, in buildings and facilities sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1), at elevators 712 doesn't require in a shaft, for smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating (1030.6.2 and 1030.6.3), or for elevators reached from a refuge area with a horizontal exit: elevators and areas of refuge aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.4.2 Area of refuge. The elevator shall be accessed from an area of refuge complying with Section 1009.6. Exceptions: 1. Areas of refuge are not required at the elevator in open parking garages. 2. Areas of refuge are not required in buildings and facilities equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. 3. Areas of refuge are not required at elevators not required to be located in a shaft in accordance with Section 712. 4. Areas of refuge are not required at elevators serving smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating areas complying with Sections 1030.6.2 and 1030.6.3. 5. Areas of refuge are not required for elevators accessed from a refuge area in conjunction with a horizontal exit. | drafted:general.bc_1009_4_2_elevator_area_of_refuge |
| 1009.5 | Platform lifts | flag | draft | A platform lift serves as part of an accessible means of egress only where 1110.11 allows it in a required accessible route (not under its item 10), with standby power per Chapter 27: platform lifts aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.5 Platform lifts. Platform lifts shall be permitted to serve as part of an accessible means of egress where allowed as part of a required accessible route in Section 1110.11 except for Item 10. Standby power for the platform lift shall be provided in accordance with Chapter 27. | drafted:general.bc_1009_5_platform_lifts |
| 1009.6 | Areas of refuge | flag | draft | Every required area of refuge is reached from the space it serves by an accessible means of egress: areas of refuge aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.6 Areas of refuge. Every required area of refuge shall be accessible from the space it serves by an accessible means of egress. | drafted:general.bc_1009_6_areas_of_refuge |
| 1009.6.1 | Travel distance | flag | draft | The travel distance from any accessible space to an area of refuge is no more than the exit access travel distance the occupancy is allowed (1017.1): areas of refuge aren't marked in the model, so the routes don't measure travel to one, a person's check. | 1009.6.1 Travel distance. The maximum travel distance from any accessible space to an area of refuge shall not exceed the exit access travel distance permitted for the occupancy in accordance with Section 1017.1. | drafted:general.bc_1009_6_1_area_of_refuge_travel_distance |
| 1009.6.2 | Stairway or elevator access | flag | draft | Every required area of refuge has direct access to a stairway complying with 1009.3 and 1023 or an elevator complying with 1009.4 (or, at the level of exit discharge, an interior area of refuge to an exterior exit door): areas of refuge aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.6.2 Stairway or elevator access. Every required area of refuge shall have direct access to a stairway complying with Sections 1009.3 and 1023 or an elevator complying with Section 1009.4. Exception: An interior area of refuge at the level of exit discharge that provides direct access to an exterior exit door. | drafted:general.bc_1009_6_2_area_of_refuge_stairway_or_elevator_access |
| 1009.6.3 | Size | flag | draft | Each area of refuge holds one 30 by 52 inch wheelchair space for each 200 occupants or part of 200 it and the areas it serves hold, the spaces not reducing the means of egress minimum width or required capacity, and none blocked by more than one adjoining space: areas of refuge aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.6.3 Size. Each area of refuge shall be sized to accommodate one wheelchair space of 30 inches by 52 inches (762 mm by 1320 mm) for each 200 occupants or portion thereof, based on the occupant load of the area of refuge and areas served by the area of refuge. Such wheelchair spaces shall not reduce the means of egress minimum width or required capacity. Access to any of the required wheelchair spaces in an area of refuge shall not be obstructed by more than one adjoining wheelchair space. | drafted:general.bc_1009_6_3_area_of_refuge_size |
| 1009.6.4 | Separation | flag | draft | Each area of refuge is separated from the rest of the story by a smoke barrier (709) or a horizontal exit (1026) and designed to keep smoke out; not required within an interior exit stairway enclosure (1023), or in outdoor facilities whose exit access is essentially open to the outside: areas of refuge aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.6.4 Separation. Each area of refuge shall be separated from the remainder of the story by a smoke barrier complying with Section 709 or a horizontal exit complying with Section 1026. Each area of refuge shall be designed to minimize the intrusion of smoke. Exceptions: 1. Areas of refuge located within an enclosure for interior exit stairways complying with Section 1023. 2. Areas of refuge in outdoor facilities where exit access is essentially open to the outside. | drafted:general.bc_1009_6_4_area_of_refuge_separation |
| 1009.6.5 | Two-way communication | flag | draft | Areas of refuge have a two-way communication system complying with 1009.8.1 and 1009.8.2: areas of refuge aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.6.5 Two-way communication. Areas of refuge shall be provided with a two-way communication system complying with Sections 1009.8.1 and 1009.8.2. | drafted:general.bc_1009_6_5_area_of_refuge_two_way_communication |
| 1009.7 | Exterior areas for assisted rescue | flag | draft | An exterior area for assisted rescue is reached by an accessible route from the area it serves, and one is provided on the exterior landing (1009.7.1 to 1009.7.4) where the exit discharge has no accessible route from an exit on the level of exit discharge to a public way: the site's accessible routes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.7 Exterior areas for assisted rescue. Exterior areas for assisted rescue shall be accessed by an accessible route from the area served. Where the exit discharge does not include an accessible route from an exit located on the level of exit discharge to a public way, an exterior area of assisted rescue shall be provided on the exterior landing in accordance with Sections 1009.7.1 through 1009.7.4. | drafted:general.bc_1009_7_exterior_areas_for_assisted_rescue |
| 1009.7.1 | Size | flag | draft | Each exterior area for assisted rescue holds wheelchair spaces as 1009.6.3 sizes them: such areas aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.7.1 Size. Each exterior area for assisted rescue shall be sized to accommodate wheelchair spaces in accordance with Section 1009.6.3. | drafted:general.bc_1009_7_1_assisted_rescue_size |
| 1009.7.2 | Separation | flag | draft | The exterior walls between an exterior area for assisted rescue and the building's interior are rated at least 1 hour from the inside, extending at least 10 feet beyond the landing on either side (or equivalent rated construction at least 4 feet out from the wall beside the landing) and from the ground to at least 10 feet above the area's floor or the roof line, whichever is lower, openings protected per 716; neither the rating nor the opening protectives is required in a building sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1): such areas and wall ratings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.7.2 Separation. Exterior walls separating the exterior area of assisted rescue from the interior of the building shall have a minimum fire-resistance rating of 1 hour, rated for exposure to fire from the inside. The fire-resistance-rated exterior wall construction shall extend horizontally not less than 10 feet (3048 mm) beyond the landing on either side of the landing or equiva- lent fire-resistance-rated construction is permitted to extend out perpendicular to the exterior wall not less than 4 feet (1219 mm) on the side of the landing. The fire-resistance-rated construction shall extend vertically from the ground to a point not less than 10 feet (3048 mm) above the floor level of the area for assisted rescue or to the roof line, whichever is lower. Openings within such fire-resistance-rated exterior walls shall be protected in accordance with Section 716. Exception: The fire-resistance rating and opening protectives are not required in the exterior wall where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. | drafted:general.bc_1009_7_2_assisted_rescue_separation |
| 1009.7.3 | Openness | flag | draft | An exterior area for assisted rescue is open to the outside air, its sides other than the separation walls at least 50 percent open, the openings spread to keep smoke and toxic gases from gathering: such areas aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.7.3 Openness. The exterior area for assisted rescue shall be open to the outside air. The sides other than the separation walls shall be not less than 50 percent open, and the open area shall be distributed so as to minimize the accumulation of smoke or toxic gases. | drafted:general.bc_1009_7_3_assisted_rescue_openness |
| 1009.7.4 | Stairways | flag | draft | Stairways in the means of egress from an exterior area for assisted rescue have at least 48 inches clear between handrails; not required in a building sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1): such areas aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.7.4 Stairways. Stairways that are part of the means of egress for the exterior area for assisted rescue shall provide a mini- mum clear width of 48 inches (1219 mm) between handrails. Exception: The minimum clear width of 48 inches (1219 mm) between handrails is not required at stairways serving buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. | drafted:general.bc_1009_7_4_assisted_rescue_stairways |
| 1009.8 | Two-way communication | flag | draft | A two-way communication system complying with 1009.8.1 and 1009.8.2 is at the landing serving each elevator or bank of elevators on each accessible floor one or more stories above or below the level of exit discharge; not required where the system is in areas of refuge (1009.6.5), on floors with ramps complying with 1012, at landings serving only service elevators outside the accessible means of egress and the required accessible route, only freight elevators, or a private residence elevator, or in Group I-2 or I-3 facilities: elevators aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.8 Two-way communication. A two-way communication system complying with Sections 1009.8.1 and 1009.8.2 shall be provided at the landing serving each elevator or bank of elevators on each accessible floor that is one or more stories above or below the level of exit discharge. Exceptions: 1. Two-way communication systems are not required at the landing serving each elevator or bank of elevators where the two-way communication system is provided within areas of refuge in accordance with Section 1009.6.5. 2. Two-way communication systems are not required on floors provided with ramps conforming to the provisions of Section 1012. 3. Two-way communication systems are not required at the landings serving only service elevators that are not designated as part of the accessible means of egress or serve as part of the required accessible route into a facility. 4. Two-way communication systems are not required at the landings serving only freight elevators. 5. Two-way communication systems are not required at the landing serving a private residence elevator. 6. Two-way communication systems are not required in Group I-2 or I-3 facilities. | drafted:general.bc_1009_8_two_way_communication |
| 1009.8.1 | System requirements | flag | draft | A two-way communication system connects each required location with the fire command center or a central control point the fire department approves, with timed automatic telephone dial-out to an approved supervising station or emergency services where that point isn't constantly attended, audible and visible signals, listed to UL 2525 and installed per NFPA 72: such systems aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.8.1 System requirements. Two-way communication systems shall provide communication between each required loca- tion and the fire command center or a central control point location approved by the fire department. Where the central control point is not a constantly attended location, the two-way communication system shall have timed, automatic telephone dial-out capability that provides two-way communication with an approved supervising station or emergency services. The two-way communication system shall include both audible and visible signals. Systems shall be listed in accordance with UL 2525 and installed in accordance with NFPA 72. | drafted:general.bc_1009_8_1_two_way_communication_system |
| 1009.8.2 | Directions | flag | draft | Directions for using the two-way communication system, instructions for summoning help through it and written identification of the location are posted beside it, in ICC A117.1's visual characters: such systems aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.8.2 Directions. Directions for the use of the two-way communication system, instructions for summoning assistance via the two-way communication system and written identification of the location shall be posted adjacent to the two-way communica- tion system. Signage shall comply with the ICC A117.1 requirements for visual characters. | drafted:general.bc_1009_8_2_two_way_communication_directions |
| 1009.9 | Signage | flag | draft | Each door to an area of refuge from an adjacent floor area is signed "AREA OF REFUGE", and each door to an exterior area for assisted rescue "EXTERIOR AREA FOR ASSISTED RESCUE", in ICC A117.1's visual characters with the International Symbol of Accessibility, lit where 1013.3 requires exit sign illumination, with visual, raised character and braille signs per ICC A117.1 at each such door (1013.4): signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.9 Signage. Signage indicating special accessibility provisions shall be provided as shown: 1. Each door providing access to an area of refuge from an adjacent floor area shall be identified by a sign stating, "AREA OF REFUGE." 2. Each door providing access to an exterior area for assisted rescue shall be identified by a sign stating, "EXTERIOR AREA FOR ASSISTED RESCUE." Signage shall comply with the ICC A117.1 requirements for visual characters and include the International Symbol of Accessibility. Where exit sign illumination is required by Section 1013.3, the signs shall be illuminated. Additionally, visual characters, raised char- acter and braille signage complying with ICC A117.1 shall be located at each door to an area of refuge and exterior area for assisted rescue in accordance with Section 1013.4. | drafted:general.bc_1009_9_accessibility_signage |
| 1009.10 | Directional signage | flag | draft | Directional signs showing every other means of egress, and which are accessible, are at exits serving a required accessible space that don't give an approved accessible means of egress, at elevator landings and within areas of refuge: signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.10 Directional signage. Directional signage indicating the location of all other means of egress and which of those are accessi- ble means of egress shall be provided at the following: 1. At exits serving a required accessible space but not providing an approved accessible means of egress. 2. At elevator landings. 3. Within areas of refuge. | drafted:general.bc_1009_10_directional_signage |
| 1009.11 | Instructions | flag | draft | Areas of refuge, exterior areas for assisted rescue and the places 1009.8 requires two-way communication have posted instructions for emergencies in ICC A117.1's visual characters: that those able to use the exit stairway do so at once unless assisting others, the planned help with stairs or supervised elevators and how to summon it, and how to use the two-way communication system where provided: signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1009.11 Instructions. In areas of refuge, exterior areas for assisted rescue and locations required to provide two-way communica- tions systems complying with Section 1009.8, instructions on the use of the area under emergency conditions shall be posted. Signage shall comply with the ICC A117.1 requirements for visual characters. The instructions shall include all of the following: 1. Persons able to use the exit stairway do so as soon as possible, unless they are assisting others. 2. Information on planned availability of assistance in the use of stairs or supervised operation of elevators and how to summon such assistance. 3. Directions for use of the two-way communication system where provided. | drafted:general.bc_1009_11_instructions |
| 1010.1 | General | flag | draft | Doors in the means of egress are readily distinguishable as doors, carry no mirrors and aren't concealed by curtains or decorations; exterior exit doors also meet 1022.2 (its own flag), gates 1010.4 and turnstiles 1010.5 (neither brought in): none of it is in the model, so a person's check. | 1010.1 General. Doors in the means of egress shall comply with the requirements of Sections 1010.1.1 through 1010.3.4. Exterior exit doors shall also comply with the requirements of Section 1022.2. Gates in the means of egress shall comply with the requirements of Sections 1010.4 and 1010.4.1. Turnstiles in the means of egress shall comply with the requirements of Sections 1010.5 through 1010.5.4. Doors, gates and turnstiles provided for egress purposes in numbers greater than required by this code shall comply with the requirements of this section. Doors in the means of egress shall be readily distinguishable from the adjacent construction and finishes such that the doors are easily recognizable as doors. Mirrors or similar reflecting materials shall not be used on means of egress doors. Means of egress doors shall not be concealed by curtains, drapes, decorations or similar materials. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_doors_general |
| 1010.1.1 | Size of doors | rule | draft | Each door opening's clear width (32 inches, 41 1/2 inches for Group I-2 bed movement) and clear height (80 inches), with exceptions that loosen them within dwelling and sleeping units, for small storage closets and for compartments: measurable per door from its maker's stated clear opening. Its capacity for the occupant load is a person's check. | 1010.1.1 Size of doors. The required capacity of each door opening shall be sufficient for the occupant load thereof and shall provide a minimum clear opening width of 32 inches (813 mm). The clear opening width of doorways with swinging doors shall be measured between the face of the door and the frame stop, with the door open 90 degrees (1.57 rad). Where this section requires a minimum clear opening width of 32 inches (813 mm) and a door opening includes two door leaves without a mullion, one leaf shall provide a minimum clear opening width of 32 inches (813 mm). In Group I-2, doors serving as means of egress doors where used for the movement of beds shall provide a minimum clear opening width of 41 1/2 inches (1054 mm). The minimum clear open- ing height of doors shall be not less than 80 inches (2032 mm). Exceptions: 1. In Group R-2 and R-3 dwelling and sleeping units that are not required to be an Accessible unit, Type A unit or Type B unit, the minimum width shall not apply to door openings that are not part of the required means of egress. 2. In Group I-3, door openings to resident sleeping units that are not required to be an Accessible unit shall have a mini- mum clear opening width of 28 inches (711 mm). 3. Door openings to storage closets less than 10 square feet (0.93 m[fn2]) in area shall not be limited by the minimum clear opening width. 4. Door openings within a dwelling unit or sleeping unit shall have a minimum clear opening height of 78 inches (1981 mm). 5. In dwelling and sleeping units that are not required to be Accessible, Type A or Type B units, exterior door openings other than the required exit door shall have a minimum clear opening height of 76 inches (1930 mm). 6. In Groups I-1, R-2, R-3 and R-4, in dwelling and sleeping units that are not required to be Accessible, Type A or Type B units, the minimum clear opening widths shall not apply to interior egress doors. 7. Door openings required to be accessible within Type B units intended for user passage shall have a minimum clear opening width of 31.75 inches (806 mm). | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_1_door_width, drafted:general.bc_1010_1_1_door_height, drafted:general.bc_1010_1_1_door_capacity |
| 1010.1.1.1 | Projections into clear opening | flag | draft | Nothing projects into a door's required clear width below 34 inches, and at most 4 inches between 34 and 80 inches (closers, stops and the like down to 78 inches): door hardware isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1010.1.1.1 Projections into clear opening. There shall not be projections into the required clear opening width lower than 34 inches (864 mm) above the floor or ground. Projections into the clear opening width between 34 inches (864 mm) and 80 inches (2032 mm) above the floor or ground shall not exceed 4 inches (102 mm). Exception: Door closers, overhead doorstops, frame stops, power door operators, and electromagnetic door locks shall proj- ect into the door opening height not lower than 78 inches (1980 mm) above the floor. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_1_1_projections |
| 1010.1.2 | Egress door types | flag | draft | Egress doors are side-hinged swinging, pivoted or balanced, with exceptions the model doesn't tell apart (a power-operated door in any group among them): a door of another kind, or whose kind isn't stated, is a person's check per door. | 1010.1.2 Egress door types. Egress doors shall be of the side-hinged swinging door, pivoted door, or balanced door types. Exceptions: 1. Private garages, office areas, factory and storage areas with an occupant load of 10 or less. 2. Group I-3 occupancies used as a place of detention. 3. Critical or intensive care patient rooms within suites of health care facilities. 4. Doors within or serving a single dwelling unit in Groups R-2 and R-3. 5. In other than Group H occupancies, revolving doors complying with Section 1010.3.1. 6. In other than Group H occupancies, special purpose horizontal sliding, accordion or folding door assemblies comply- ing with Section 1010.3.3. 7. Power-operated doors in accordance with Section 1010.3.2. 8. Doors serving a bathroom within an individual dwelling unit or sleeping unit in Group R-1. 9. In other than Group H occupancies, manually operated horizontal sliding doors are permitted in a means of egress from spaces with an occupant load of 10 or less. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_2_door_types |
| 1010.1.2.1 | Direction of swing | rule | draft | A swinging door swings in the direction of egress travel where it serves a room or area of 50 or more occupants or a Group H occupancy: checkable per door from the side it swings into, the direction the routes find and the occupant loads. | 1010.1.2.1 Direction of swing. Side-hinged swinging doors, pivoted doors and balanced doors shall swing in the direction of egress travel where serving a room or area containing an occupant load of 50 or more persons or a Group H occupancy. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_2_1_direction_of_swing |
| 1010.1.3 | Forces to unlatch and open doors | flag | draft | The forces to unlatch a door (15 pounds push or pull, 28 inch-pounds rotation) and to open it (5 pounds for an interior manual swinging door not required to be fire rated; else 30 pounds to set in motion, 15 to open fully): door hardware and forces aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.1.3 Forces to unlatch and open doors. The forces to unlatch doors shall comply with the following: 1. Where door hardware operates by push or pull, the operational force to unlatch the door shall not exceed 15 pounds (67 N). 2. Where door hardware operates by rotation, the operational force to unlatch the door shall not exceed 28 inch-pounds (315 N-cm). The force to open doors shall comply with the following: 1. For interior swinging egress doors that are manually operated, other than doors required to be fire rated, the force for pushing or pulling open the door shall not exceed 5 pounds (22 N). 2. For other swinging doors, sliding doors or folding doors, and doors required to be fire rated, the door shall require not more than a 30-pound (133 N) force to be set in motion and shall move to a full-open position when subjected to not more than a 15-pound (67 N) force. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_3_door_forces |
| 1010.1.3.1 | Location of applied forces | reference | draft | Says where 1010.1.3's forces are applied (the latch side of the door): how to measure them, read with 1010.1.3's flag. | 1010.1.3.1 Location of applied forces. Forces shall be applied to the latch side of the door. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_3_door_forces |
| 1010.1.3.2 | Manual horizontal sliding doors | flag | draft | A manual horizontal sliding door required to latch doesn't rebound into a partly open position when closed: latches aren't in the model, a person's check for each door that isn't stated side-hinged. | 1010.1.3.2 Manual horizontal sliding doors. Where a manual horizontal sliding door is required to latch, the latch or other mechanism shall prevent the door from rebounding into a partially open position when the door is closed. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_3_2_sliding_door_latch |
| 1010.1.4 | Floor elevation | flag | draft | A floor or landing at the same elevation on each side of a door, level (an exterior landing at most 2 percent), with six exceptions: floor levels at doors and landings aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.1.4 Floor elevation. There shall be a floor or landing on each side of a door. Such floor or landing shall be at the same eleva- tion on each side of the door. Landings shall be level except for exterior landings, which are permitted to have a slope not to exceed 0.25 unit vertical in 12 units horizontal (2-percent slope). Exceptions: 1. At doors serving individual dwelling units or sleeping units in Groups R-2 and R-3, a door is permitted to open at the top step of an interior flight of stairs, provided that the door does not swing over the top step. 2. At exterior doors serving Groups F, H, R-2 and S and where such doors are not part of an accessible route, the landing at an exterior door shall not be more than 7 inches (178 mm) below the landing on the egress side of the door, provided that the door, other than an exterior storm or screen door, does not swing over the landing. 3. At exterior doors serving Group U and individual dwelling units and sleeping units in Groups R-2 and R-3, and where such units are not required to be Accessible units, Type A units or Type B units, the landing at an exterior doorway shall be not more than 7 3/4 inches (197 mm) below the landing on the egress side of the door. Such doors, including storm or screen doors, shall be permitted to swing over either landing. 4. Variations in elevation due to differences in finish materials, but not more than 1/2 inch (12.7 mm). 5. Exterior decks, patios or balconies that are part of Type B dwelling units or sleeping units, that have impervious surfaces and that are not more than 4 inches (102 mm) below the finished floor level of the adjacent interior space of the dwelling unit or sleeping unit. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_4_floor_elevation |
| 1010.1.5 | Landings at doors | flag | draft | A landing at a door as wide as the stairway or door, whichever is greater, at least 44 inches long (36 in Groups R-3 and U and within Group R-2 units), a required dimension not reduced by a fully open door by more than 7 inches and, serving 50 or more, the landing not reduced by a door in any position to less than half its required width: landings aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.1.5 Landings at doors. Landings shall have a width not less than the width of the stairway or the door, whichever is greater. Doors in the fully open position shall not reduce a required dimension by more than 7 inches (178 mm). Where a landing serves an occupant load of 50 or more, doors in any position shall not reduce the landing to less than one-half its required width. Landings shall have a length measured in the direction of travel of not less than 44 inches (1118 mm). Exception: Landing length in the direction of travel in Groups R-3 and U and within individual units of Group R-2 need not exceed 36 inches (914 mm). | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_5_landings |
| 1010.1.6 | Thresholds | flag | draft | Threshold heights (3/4 inch for a dwelling unit's sliding door, 1/2 inch for others, beveled above 1/4 inch), with exceptions for Group R-2 and R-3 exterior doors and Type B units: thresholds aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.1.6 Thresholds. Thresholds at doorways shall not exceed 3/4 inch (19.1 mm) in height above the finished floor or landing for sliding doors serving dwelling units or 1/2 inch (12.7 mm) above the finished floor or landing for other doors. Raised thresholds and floor level changes greater than 1/4 inch (6.4 mm) at doorways shall be beveled with a slope not greater than one unit vertical in two units horizontal (50-percent slope). Exceptions: 1. In occupancy Group R-2 or R-3, threshold heights for sliding and side-hinged exterior doors shall be permitted to be up to 7 3/4 inches (197 mm) in height if all of the following apply: 1.1. The door is not part of the required means of egress. 1.2. The door is not part of an accessible route as required by Chapter 11. 1.3. The door is not part of an Accessible unit, Type A unit or Type B unit. 2. In Type B units, where Exception 5 to Section 1010.1.4 permits a 4-inch (102 mm) elevation change at the door, the threshold height on the exterior side of the door shall not exceed 4 3/4 inches (120 mm) in height above the exterior deck, patio or balcony for sliding doors or 4 1/2 inches (114 mm) above the exterior deck, patio or balcony for other doors. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_6_thresholds |
| 1010.1.7 | Door arrangement | flag | draft | Two doors in a series at least 48 inches apart plus a door swinging into the space, swinging the same way or away from it, with exceptions for horizontal sliding power-operated doors, Group R-2 and R-3 storm and screen doors, and doors within those units other than Type A units: doors in a series aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.1.7 Door arrangement. Space between two doors in a series shall be 48 inches (1219 mm) minimum plus the width of a door swinging into the space. Doors in a series shall swing either in the same direction or away from the space between the doors. Exceptions: 1. The minimum distance between horizontal sliding power-operated doors in a series shall be 48 inches (1219 mm). 2. Storm and screen doors serving individual dwelling units in Groups R-2 and R-3 need not be spaced 48 inches (1219 mm) from the other door. 3. Doors within individual dwelling units in Groups R-2 and R-3 other than within Type A dwelling units. | drafted:general.bc_1010_1_7_door_arrangement |
| 1010.2 | Door operations | flag | draft | Egress doors readily openable from the egress side without a key or special knowledge or effort, except as 1010.2 permits: how each door locks isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2 Door operations. Except as specifically permitted by this section, egress doors shall be readily openable from the egress side without the use of a key or special knowledge or effort. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_door_operations |
| 1010.2.1 | Unlatching | flag | draft | One motion in one direction releases every latch and lock, no manual bolts, with three exceptions: hardware isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.1 Unlatching. The unlatching of any door or leaf for egress shall require not more than one motion in a single linear or rotational direction to release all latching and all locking devices. locking devices. Manual bolts are not permitted. Exceptions: 1. Places of detention or restraint. 2. Doors with manual bolts, automatic flush bolts and constant latching bolts as permitted by Section 1010.2.4, Item 4. 3. Doors from individual dwelling units and sleeping units of Group R occupancies as permitted by Section 1010.2.4, Item 5. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_1_unlatching |
| 1010.2.2 | Hardware | flag | draft | Operating devices of doors Chapter 11 requires to be accessible need no tight grasping, pinching or twisting: hardware isn't in the model, a person's check. | [NY] 1010.2.2 Hardware. Door handles, pulls, latches, locks and other operating devices of doors required to be accessible by Chapter 11 shall not require tight grasping, tight pinching or twisting of the wrist to operate. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_2_hardware |
| 1010.2.3 | Hardware height | flag | draft | Door handles, pulls, latches, locks and other operating devices 34 to 48 inches above the finished floor, with two exceptions: hardware isn't in the model, a person's check. | [NY] 1010.2.3 Hardware height. Door handles, pulls, latches, locks and other operating devices of doors shall be installed 34 inches (864 mm) minimum and 48 inches (1219 mm) maximum above the finished floor. Exceptions: 1. Locks used only for security purposes and not used for normal operation are permitted at any height. 2. The height of operating devices of locks and latches of doors and gates in barriers required to restrict access to a swimming pool, spa, or hot tub shall comply with Sections 3109.2.7 and 3109.2.8. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_3_hardware_height |
| 1010.2.4 | Locks and latches | flag | draft | Where locks and latches may keep a door from opening (ten items, each with its conditions): how each door locks isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.4 Locks and latches. Locks and latches shall be permitted to prevent operation of doors where any of the following exist: 1. Places of detention or restraint. 2. In Group I-1, Condition 2 and Group I-2 occupancies where the clinical needs of persons receiving care require contain- ment or where persons receiving care pose a security threat, provided that all clinical staff can readily unlock doors at all times, and all such locks are keyed to keys carried by all clinical staff at all times or all clinical staff have the codes or other means necessary to operate the locks at all times. 3. In buildings in occupancy Group A having an occupant load of 300 or less, Groups B, F, M and S, and in places of religious worship, the main door or doors are permitted to be equipped with key-operated locking devices from the egress side provided that: 3.1. The doors are the main exterior doors to the building, or the doors are the main doors to the tenant space. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_4_locks_and_latches |
| Table 1010.2.4 | MANUAL BOLTS, AUTOMATIC FLUSH BOLTS AND CONSTANT LATCHING BOLTS ON THE INACTIVE LEAF OF A PAIR OF DOORS | flag | draft | Where manual bolts, automatic flush bolts and constant latching bolts are permitted on a pair's inactive leaf, by use and fire-door rating: bolts aren't in the model, a person's check. | TABLE 1010.2.4—MANUAL BOLTS, AUTOMATIC FLUSH BOLTS AND CONSTANT LATCHING BOLTS ON THE INACTIVE LEAF OF A PAIR OF DOORS PERMITTED USES OF MANUAL BOLTS, THE PAIR OF AUTOMATIC FLUSH BOLTS AND CONSTANT LATCHING APPLICATION WITH A PAIR OF DOORS WITH AN ACTIVE DOORS IS BOLTS ON THE INACTIVE LEAF OF A PAIR OF DOORS. REQUIRED TO LEAF AND AN INACTIVE LEAF COMPLY WITH Surface- or Automatic Constant SECTION 716 flush-mounted flush bolts latching bolts manual bolts No P P P Group B, F or S occupancies with occupant load less than 50. Yes NP NP[fnb] P Group B, F or S occupancies where the building is equipped No P P P with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 and the inactive leaf is not needed to meet Yes NP NP[fnb] P egress capacity requirements. Group I-2 patient care and sleeping rooms where inactive leaf No NP NP[fnb] P is not needed to meet egress capacity requirements. Yes NP NP[fnb] P Any occupancy where panic hardware is not required, egress No NP P NP doors are used in pairs, and where both leaves are required to meet egress capacity requirements. Yes NP NP[fnb] NP Storage or equipment rooms where the inactive leaf is not No P[fna] P P needed to meet egress capacity requirements. Yes P[fna] P P P = Permitted. NP = Not permitted. a.Not permitted on corridor doors in Group I-2 occupancies where corridor doors are required to be positive latching. b.Permitted where both doors are self closing or automatic closing, and are provided with a coordinator that causes the inactive leaf to be closed prior to the active leaf. | drafted:general.bc_table_1010_2_4_inactive_leaf_bolts |
| 1010.2.5 | Closet doors | flag | draft | A closet door that latches closed opens from inside the closet: hardware isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.5 Closet doors. Closet doors that latch in the closed position shall be openable from inside the closet. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_5_closet_doors |
| 1010.2.6 | Stairway doors | flag | draft | Interior stairway doors openable from both sides, with five exceptions: how each door locks isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.6 Stairway doors. Interior stairway means of egress doors shall be openable from both sides without the use of a key or special knowledge or effort. Exceptions: 1. Stairway discharge doors shall be openable from the egress side and shall only be locked from the opposite side. 2. This section shall not apply to doors arranged in accordance with Section 403.5.3. 3. Stairway exit doors shall not be locked from the side opposite the egress side, unless they are openable from the egress side and capable of being unlocked simultaneously without unlatching by all of the following methods: 3.1. Shall be capable of being unlocked individually or simultaneously upon a signal from the fire command center, where present, or a signal by emergency personnel from a single location inside the main entrance to the building. 3.2. Shall unlock simultaneously upon activation of a fire alarm signal when a fire alarm system is present in an area served by the stairway. 3.3.
