Shall unlock upon failure of the power supply to the electric lock or the locking system. 4. Stairway exit doors shall be openable from the egress side and shall only be locked from the opposite side in Group B, F, M and S occupancies where the only interior access to the tenant space is from a single exit stairway where permit- ted in Section 1006.3.4. 5. Stairway exit doors shall be openable from the egress side and shall only be locked from the opposite side in Group R- 2 occupancies where the only interior access to the dwelling unit is from a single exit stairway where permitted in Section 1006.3.4. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_6_stairway_doors |
| 1010.2.7 | Locking arrangements in educational occupancies | flag | draft | Intruder locks on classroom, office and other occupied rooms' doors in Groups E, B (educational) and I-4: locks aren't in the model, a person's check where the building may be one of those groups. | 1010.2.7 Locking arrangements in educational occupancies. In Group E occupancies, Group B educational occupancies and Group I-4 occupancies, egress doors from classrooms, offices and other occupied rooms with locking arrangements designed to keep intruders from entering the room shall comply with all of the following conditions: 1. The door shall be capable of being unlocked from outside the room with a key or other approved means. 2. The door shall be openable from within the room in accordance with Section 1010.2. 3. Modifications shall not be made to listed panic hardware, fire door hardware or door closers. 4. Modifications to fire door assemblies shall be in accordance with NFPA 80. Remote locking or unlocking of doors from an approved location shall be permitted in addition to the unlocking operation in Item 1. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_7_educational_locking |
| 1010.2.8 | Panic and fire exit hardware | rule | draft | A latched swinging door serving a Group H occupancy, or a Group A or E room or space of 50 or more, has panic or fire exit hardware as its only latch or lock: checkable per door from the hardware the description states, its operation and the building's group (the exceptions a review where the model can't rule them out). | 1010.2.8 Panic and fire exit hardware. Swinging doors serving a Group H occupancy and swinging doors serving rooms or spaces with an occupant load of 50 or more in a Group A or E occupancy shall not be provided with a latch or lock other than panic hardware or fire exit hardware. Exceptions: 1. A main exit of a Group A occupancy shall be permitted to have locking devices in accordance with Section 1010.2.4, Item 3. 2. Doors provided with panic hardware or fire exit hardware and serving a Group A or E occupancy shall be permitted to be electrically locked in accordance with Section 1010.2.10. 3. Exit access doors serving occupied exterior areas shall be permitted to be locked in accordance with Section 1010.2.4, Item 8. 4. Courtrooms shall be permitted to be locked in accordance with Section 1010.2.12, Item 3. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_8_panic_hardware |
| 1010.2.8.1 | Refrigeration machinery room | flag | draft | Refrigeration machinery rooms over 1,000 sq ft: two doorways swinging with egress travel, with panic or fire exit hardware: which rooms are refrigeration machinery rooms isn't in the model, a person's check where the building may have special egress spaces. | 1010.2.8.1 Refrigeration machinery room. Refrigeration machinery rooms larger than 1,000 square feet (93 m[fn2]) shall have not less than two exit or exit access doorways that swing in the direction of egress travel and shall be equipped with panic hard- ware or fire exit hardware. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_8_1_refrigeration_machinery_rooms |
| 1010.2.8.2 | Rooms with electrical equipment | flag | draft | Panic or fire exit hardware on the doors of transformer vaults, battery and energy storage rooms, modular data centers and rooms with large electrical equipment: which rooms these are isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.8.2 Rooms with electrical equipment. Exit or exit access doors serving transformer vaults, rooms designated for batteries or energy storage systems, or modular data centers shall be equipped with panic hardware or fire exit hardware. Rooms containing electrical equipment rated 800 amperes or more that contain overcurrent devices, switching devices or control devices and where the exit or exit access door is less than 25 feet (7620 mm) from the equipment working space as required by NFPA 70, such doors shall not be provided with a latch or lock other than panic hardware or fire exit hardware. The doors shall swing in the direction of egress travel. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_8_2_electrical_rooms |
| 1010.2.8.3 | Installation | flag | draft | Panic and fire exit hardware listed, at least half the leaf wide, at most 15 lb to unlatch: the hardware's listing and size aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.8.3 Installation. Where panic or fire exit hardware is installed, it shall comply with the following: 1. Panic hardware shall be listed in accordance with UL 305. 2. Fire exit hardware shall be listed in accordance with UL 10C and UL 305. 3. The actuating portion of the releasing device shall extend not less than one-half of the door leaf width. 4. The maximum unlatching force shall not exceed 15 pounds (67 N). | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_8_3_panic_hardware_installation |
| 1010.2.8.4 | Balanced doors | flag | draft | Push-pad panic hardware on balanced doors: which doors are balanced isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.8.4 Balanced doors. If balanced doors are used and panic hardware is required, the panic hardware shall be the push- pad type and the pad shall not extend more than one-half the width of the door measured from the latch side. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_8_4_balanced_doors |
| 1010.2.9 | Monitored or recorded egress, and access control systems | flag | draft | Doors with egress monitoring or access control lock only per 1010.2.10 to 1010.2.15 or open readily: access control isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.9 Monitored or recorded egress, and access control systems. Where electrical systems that monitor or record egress activity are incorporated, or where the door has an access control system, the locking system on the egress side of the door shall | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_9_access_control |
| 1010.2.10 | Door hardware release of electrically locked egress doors | flag | draft | Electric locks released by the door's hardware, with their conditions: electric locks aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.10 Door hardware release of electrically locked egress doors. Door hardware release of electrical locking systems shall be permitted on doors in the means of egress in any occupancy except Group H where installed and operated in accordance with all of the following: 1. The door hardware that is affixed to the door leaf has an obvious method of operation that is readily operated under all lighting conditions. 2. The door hardware is capable of being operated with one hand and shall comply with Section 1010.2.1. 3. Operation of the door hardware directly interrupts the power to the electric lock and unlocks the door immediately. 4. Loss of power to the electrical locking system automatically unlocks the electric lock. 5. Where panic or fire exit hardware is required by Section 1010.2.8, operation of the panic or fire exit hardware also releases the electric lock. 6. The electromechanical or electromagnetic locking device shall be listed in accordance with either UL 294 or UL1034. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_10_door_hardware_release |
| 1010.2.11 | Sensor release of electrically locked egress doors | flag | draft | Electric locks released by a sensor, with their conditions: electric locks aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.11 Sensor release of electrically locked egress doors. Sensor release of electrical locking systems shall be permitted on doors located in the means of egress in any occupancy except Group H where installed and operated in accordance with all of the following criteria: 1. The sensor shall be installed on the egress side, arranged to detect an occupant approaching the doors, and shall cause the electrical locking system to unlock the electric lock. 2. Upon a signal from a sensor or loss of power to the sensor, the electrical locking system shall unlock the electric lock. 3. Loss of power to the electric lock or the electrical locking system shall automatically unlock the electric locks. 4. The doors shall be arranged to unlock the electric lock from a manual unlocking device located 40 inches to 48 inches (1016 mm to 1219 mm) vertically above the floor and within 5 feet (1524 mm) of the secured doors. Ready access shall be provided to the manual unlocking device and the device shall be clearly identified by a sign that reads “PUSH TO EXIT.” When operated, the manual unlocking device shall result in direct interruption of power to the electric lock—indepen- dent of other electronics—and the electric lock shall remain unlocked for not less than 30 seconds. 5. Activation of the building fire alarm system, where provided, shall automatically unlock the electric lock, and the electric lock shall remain unlocked until the fire alarm system has been reset. 6. Activation of the building automatic sprinkler system or fire detection system, where provided, shall automatically unlock the electric lock. The electric lock shall remain unlocked until the fire alarm system has been reset. 7. Emergency lighting shall be provided on the egress side of the door. 8. The electromechanical or electromagnetic locking device shall be listed in accordance with either UL 294 or UL 1034. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_11_sensor_release |
| 1010.2.12 | Delayed egress | flag | draft | Where delayed egress locks are permitted: delayed egress locks aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.12 Delayed egress. Delayed egress electrical locking systems shall be permitted on doors in the means of egress serving the following occupancies in buildings that are equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or an approved automatic smoke or heat detection system installed in accordance with Section 907. 1. Group B, F, I, M, R, S and U occupancies. 2. Group E classrooms with an occupant load of less than 50. 3. In courtrooms in Group A-3 and B occupancies, delayed egress electrical locking systems shall be permitted to be installed on exit or exit access doors, other than the main exit or exit access door, in buildings that are equipped through- out with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_12_delayed_egress |
| 1010.2.12.1 | Delayed egress locking system | flag | draft | How a delayed egress lock works, how many a path may pass, and its sign: delayed egress locks aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.12.1 Delayed egress locking system. The delayed egress electrical locking system shall be installed and operated in accordance with all of the following: 1. The delay of the delayed egress electrical locking system shall deactivate upon actuation of the automatic sprinkler system or automatic fire detection system, allowing immediate free egress. 2. The delay of the delayed egress electrical locking system shall deactivate upon loss of power to the electrical locking system or electrical lock, allowing immediate free egress. 3. The delay of the delayed egress locking electrical system shall have the capability of being deactivated at the fire command center and other approved locations. 4. An attempt to egress shall initiate an irreversible process that shall allow such egress in not more than 15 seconds when a physical effort to exit is applied to the egress side door hardware for not more than 3 seconds. Initiation of the irreversible process shall activate an audible signal in the vicinity of the door. Once the delay has been deactivated, rearming the delay electronics shall be by manual means only. Exception: Where approved, a delay of not more than 30 seconds is permitted on a delayed egress door. 5. The egress path from any point shall not pass through more than one delayed egress locking system. Exceptions: 1. In Group I-1, Condition 2, Group I-2 or I-3 occupancies, the egress path from any point in the building shall pass through not more than two delayed egress locking systems provided that the combined delay does not exceed 30 seconds. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_12_1_delayed_egress_locking_system |
| 1010.2.13 | Controlled egress doors in Groups I-1 and I-2 | flag | draft | Controlled egress locks in Groups I-1 and I-2, with their conditions: locks aren't in the model, a person's check where the building may be one of those groups. | 1010.2.13 Controlled egress doors in Groups I-1 and I-2. Controlled egress electrical locking systems where egress is controlled by authorized personnel shall be permitted on doors in the means of egress in Group I-1 or I-2 occupancies where the clinical needs of persons receiving care require their containment. Controlled egress doors shall be permitted in such occupancies where the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or an approved auto- matic smoke detection system installed in accordance with Section 907, provided that the doors are installed and operate in accordance with all of the following: 1. The door’s electric locks shall unlock on actuation of the automatic sprinkler system or automatic smoke detection system allowing immediate free egress. 2. The door’s electric locks shall unlock on loss of power to the electrical locking system or to the electric lock mechanism allowing immediate free egress. 3. The electrical locking system shall be installed to have the capability of unlocking the electric locks by a switch located at the fire command center, a nursing station or other approved location. The switch shall directly break power to the electric lock. 4. A building occupant shall not be required to pass through more than one door equipped with a controlled egress locking system before entering an exit. 5. The procedures for unlocking the doors shall be described and approved as part of the emergency planning and preparedness required by Chapter 4 of the Fire Code of New York State. 6. All clinical staff shall have the keys, codes or other means necessary to operate the controlled egress electrical locking systems. 7. Emergency lighting shall be provided at the door. 8. The electromechanical or electromagnetic locking device shall be listed in accordance with either UL 294 or UL 1034. Exceptions: 1. Items 1 through 4 shall not apply to doors to areas occupied by persons who, because of clinical needs, require restraint or containment as part of the function of a psychiatric or cognitive treatment area. 2. Items 1 through 4 shall not apply to doors to areas where a listed egress control system is utilized to reduce the risk of child abduction from nursery and obstetric areas of a Group I-2 hospital. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_13_controlled_egress |
| 1010.2.14 | Elevator lobby exit access doors | flag | draft | Electrically locked exit access doors from elevator lobbies, with their conditions: electric locks aren't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.14 Elevator lobby exit access doors. Electrically locked exit access doors providing egress from elevator lobbies shall meet the following conditions: 1. For all occupants of the floor, the path of exit access travel to not less than two exits is not required to pass through the elevator lobby. 2. The building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1, and a fire alarm system in accordance with Section 907. Elevator lobbies shall be provided with an automatic smoke detection system in accordance with Section 907. 3. Upon activation of the building fire alarm system by means other than a manual fire alarm box shall automatically unlock the electric locks providing exit access from the elevator lobbies, and the electric locks shall remain unlocked until the fire alarm system is reset. 4. The electric locks shall unlock on loss of power to the electric locks or electrical locking system. 5. The electric locks shall have the capability of being unlocked by a switch located at the fire command center, security station or other approved location. 6. A two-way communication system complying with Sections 1009.8.1 and 1009.8.2, shall be located in the elevator lobby adjacent to the electrically locked exit access door and connected to an approved constantly attended station. This constantly attended station shall have the capability of unlocking the electric locks of the elevator lobby exit access doors. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_14_elevator_lobby_doors |
| 1010.2.15 | Locking arrangements in buildings within correctional facilities | flag | draft | Locked egress doors in correctional and detention facilities: whether the building is in one isn't in the model, a person's check. | 1010.2.15 Locking arrangements in buildings within correctional facilities. In buildings within correctional and detention facilities, doors in means of egress serving rooms or spaces occupied by persons whose movements are controlled for security reasons shall be permitted to be locked where equipped with egress control devices that shall unlock manually and by not less than one of the following means: 1. Activation of an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. 2. Activation of an approved manual fire alarm box. 3. A signal from a constantly attended location. | drafted:general.bc_1010_2_15_correctional_facilities |
| 1011.1 | General | flag | draft | Stairways serving occupied portions comply with 1011.2 to 1011.13, alternating tread devices, ship's ladders and ladders with 1011.14 to 1011.16, stepped aisles in assembly rooms with 1030: what isn't brought in (walkline, winders, nosings and risers' profile, construction, vertical rise, curved and spiral stairways, stair to roof, guards) is a person's check. | 1011.1 General. Stairways serving occupied portions of a building shall comply with the requirements of Sections 1011.2 through 1011.13. Alternating tread devices shall comply with Section 1011.14. Ship’s ladders shall comply with Section 1011.15. Ladders shall comply with Section 1011.16. Exception: Within rooms or spaces used for assembly purposes, stepped aisles shall comply with Section 1030. | drafted:general.bc_1011_1_stairways_general |
| 1011.2 | Width and capacity | rule | draft | Stairway width at least 44 inches (36 inches under 50 occupants), and the capacity 1005.1 asks: a rule on the measured clear width. | 1011.2 Width and capacity. The required capacity of stairways shall be determined as specified in Section 1005.1, but the minimum width shall be not less than 44 inches (1118 mm). The minimum width for stairways that serve as part of the accessible means of egress shall comply with Section 1009.3. | drafted:general.bc_1011_2_stair_width |
| 1011.3 | Headroom | rule | draft | Headroom at least 80 inches above the line connecting the nosings: a rule on the measured headroom. | 1011.3 Headroom. Stairways shall have a headroom clearance of not less than 80 inches (2032 mm) measured vertically from a line connecting the nosings. Such headroom shall be continuous above the stairway to the point where the line intersects the landing below, one tread depth beyond the bottom riser. The minimum clearance shall be maintained the full width of the stairway and landing. | drafted:general.bc_1011_3_stair_headroom |
| 1011.5.1 | Dimension reference surfaces | reference | draft | How the riser and tread dimensions are taken (without carpets, rugs or runners): the measuring reads the model's own treads and nosings, which carry no carpet, so this is no requirement of its own. | 1011.5.1 Dimension reference surfaces. For the purpose of this section, all dimensions are exclusive of carpets, rugs or runners. | drafted:general.bc_1011_1_stairways_general |
| 1011.5.2 | Riser height and tread depth | rule | draft | Riser height 4 to 7 inches and tread depth at least 11 inches, with their exceptions: rules on the measured riser heights and tread depth. | 1011.5.2 Riser height and tread depth. Stair riser heights shall be 7 inches (178 mm) maximum and 4 inches (102 mm) mini- mum. The riser height shall be measured vertically between the nosings of adjacent treads or between the stairway landing and the adjacent tread. Rectangular tread depths shall be 11 inches (279 mm) minimum measured horizontally between the vertical planes of the foremost projection of adjacent treads and at a right angle to the tread’s nosing. Winder treads shall have a minimum tread depth of 11 inches (279 mm) between the vertical planes of the foremost projection of adjacent treads at the intersections with the walkline and a minimum tread depth of 10 inches (254 mm) within the clear width of the stair. | drafted:general.bc_1011_5_2_riser_height, drafted:general.bc_1011_5_2_tread_depth |
| 1011.5.4 | Dimensional uniformity | rule | draft | Risers and treads uniform within 3/8 inch in a flight: rules on the measured variation (the treads' isn't given to the rules yet). | 1011.5.4 Dimensional uniformity. Stair treads and risers shall be of uniform size and shape. The tolerance between the largest and smallest riser height or between the largest and smallest tread depth shall not exceed 3/8 inch (9.5 mm) in any flight of stairs. The greatest winder tread depth at the walkline within any flight of stairs shall not exceed the smallest by more than 3/8 inch (9.5 mm). | drafted:general.bc_1011_5_4_riser_uniformity, drafted:general.bc_1011_5_4_tread_uniformity |
| 1011.6 | Stairway landings | flag | draft | A floor or landing at the top and bottom of each stairway, as wide as the stairway and as deep as it is wide or 48 inches; doors onto it keep half its width: landings aren't measured, so a person's check per flight. | 1011.6 Stairway landings. There shall be a floor or landing at the top and bottom of each stairway. The width of landings, measured perpendicularly to the direction of travel, shall be not less than the width of stairways served. Every landing shall have a minimum depth, measured parallel to the direction of travel, equal to the width of the stairway or 48 inches (1219 mm), whichever is less. Doors opening onto a landing shall not reduce the landing to less than one-half the required width. When fully open, the door shall not proj- ect more than 7 inches (178 mm) into the required width of a landing. Where wheelchair spaces are required on the stairway landing in accordance with Section 1009.6.3, the wheelchair space shall not be located in the required width of the landing and doors shall not swing over the wheelchair spaces. | drafted:general.bc_1011_6_stair_landings |
| 1011.11 | Handrails | rule | draft | Handrails on each side of a flight, with their exceptions: a rule on the measured handrail sides. | 1011.11 Handrails. Flights of stairways shall have handrails on each side and shall comply with Section 1014. Where glass is used to provide the handrail, the handrail shall comply with Section 2407. | drafted:general.bc_1011_1_stairways_general, drafted:general.bc_1011_11_stair_handrails |
| 1012.1 | Scope | reference | draft | Scope: Section 1012 applies to ramps in a means of egress; its exceptions (ramped aisles to 1030, curb ramps to ICC A117.1, some parking-garage vehicle ramps spared 1012.3 to 1012.10) only loosen, and every ramp flag needs review. | 1012.1 Scope. The provisions of this section shall apply to ramps used as a component of a means of egress. Exceptions: 1. Ramped aisles within assembly rooms or spaces shall comply with the provisions in Section 1030. 2. Curb ramps shall comply with ICC A117.1. 3. Vehicle ramps in parking garages for pedestrian exit access shall not be required to comply with Sections 1012.3 through 1012.10 where they are not an accessible route serving accessible parking spaces, other required accessible elements or part of an accessible means of egress. | drafted:general.bc_1012_2_slope |
| 1012.2 | Slope | flag | draft | A ramp used as part of a means of egress has a running slope no steeper than 1 in 12 (8.3 percent), any other pedestrian ramp no steeper than 1 in 8 (12.5 percent): ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.2 Slope. Ramps used as part of a means of egress shall have a running slope not steeper than 1 unit vertical in 12 units horizon- tal (8.3-percent slope). The slope of other pedestrian ramps shall not be steeper than 1 unit vertical in 8 units horizontal (12.5- percent slope). | drafted:general.bc_1012_2_slope |
| 1012.3 | Cross slope | flag | draft | A ramp's slope measured perpendicular to the direction of travel is no steeper than 1 in 48 (2 percent): ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.3 Cross slope. The slope measured perpendicular to the direction of travel of a ramp shall not be steeper than one unit vertical in 48 units horizontal (2-percent slope). | drafted:general.bc_1012_3_cross_slope |
| 1012.4 | Vertical rise | flag | draft | The rise of any ramp run is 30 inches at most: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.4 Vertical rise. The rise for any ramp run shall be 30 inches (762 mm) maximum. | drafted:general.bc_1012_4_vertical_rise |
| 1012.5 | Minimum dimensions | reference | draft | A pointer: a means of egress ramp's dimensions are 1012.5.1 to 1012.5.3's, each labeled on its own; quoted by 1012.5.1's flag. | 1012.5 Minimum dimensions. The minimum dimensions of means of egress ramps shall comply with Sections 1012.5.1 through 1012.5.3. | drafted:general.bc_1012_5_1_width_and_capacity |
| 1012.5.1 | Width and capacity | flag | draft | A means of egress ramp is at least as wide, and of at least the capacity, as 1020.3 requires of corridors, and at least 36 inches clear between handrails, if provided, or other permitted projections: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.5.1 Width and capacity. The minimum width and required capacity of a means of egress ramp shall be not less than that required for corridors by Section 1020.3. The clear width of a ramp between handrails, if provided, or other permissible projections shall be 36 inches (914 mm) minimum. | drafted:general.bc_1012_5_1_width_and_capacity |
| 1012.5.2 | Headroom | flag | draft | At least 80 inches of headroom above the finished floor of the ramp run and any intermediate landings, over the full width of the ramp and landing: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.5.2 Headroom. The minimum headroom in all parts of the means of egress ramp shall be not less than 80 inches (2032 mm) above the finished floor of the ramp run and any intermediate landings. The minimum clearance shall be maintained for the full width of the ramp and landing. | drafted:general.bc_1012_5_2_headroom |
| 1012.5.3 | Restrictions | flag | draft | A means of egress ramp doesn't narrow in the direction of egress travel, nothing projects into the required ramp and landing width, and a door opening onto a landing leaves at least 42 inches clear: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.5.3 Restrictions. Means of egress ramps shall not reduce in width in the direction of egress travel. Projections into the required ramp and landing width are prohibited. Doors opening onto a landing shall not reduce the clear width to less than 42 inches (1067 mm). | drafted:general.bc_1012_5_3_restrictions |
| 1012.6 | Landings | flag | draft | A ramp has landings at the bottom and top of each ramp, at points of turning, at entrances, at exits and at doors (ICC A117.1's maneuvering clearances at an adjoining doorway may overlap the landing, 1012.6.5): ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.6 Landings. Ramps shall have landings at the bottom and top of each ramp, points of turning, entrance, exits and at doors. Landings shall comply with Sections 1012.6.1 through 1012.6.5. | drafted:general.bc_1012_6_landings |
| 1012.6.1 | Slope | flag | draft | A ramp landing is no steeper than 1 in 48 (2 percent) in any direction, with no changes in level: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.6.1 Slope. Landings shall have a slope not steeper than one unit vertical in 48 units horizontal (2-percent slope) in any direc- tion. Changes in level are not permitted. | drafted:general.bc_1012_6_1_landing_slope |
| 1012.6.2 | Width | flag | draft | A ramp landing is at least as wide as the widest ramp run adjoining it: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.6.2 Width. The landing width shall be not less than the width of the widest ramp run adjoining the landing. | drafted:general.bc_1012_6_2_landing_width |
| 1012.6.3 | Length | flag | draft | A ramp landing is at least 60 inches long; 36 inches within Group R-2 and R-3 dwelling and sleeping units not required to be Accessible, Type A or Type B units (1108), and not more than 48 inches is required in the direction of travel where the ramp isn't part of an accessible route: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.6.3 Length. The landing length shall be 60 inches (1525 mm) minimum. Exceptions: 1. In Group R-2 and R-3 individual dwelling and sleeping units that are not required to be Accessible units, Type A units or Type B units in accordance with Section 1108, landings are permitted to be 36 inches (914 mm) minimum. 2. Where the ramp is not a part of an accessible route, the length of the landing shall not be required to be more than 48 inches (1219 mm) in the direction of travel. | drafted:general.bc_1012_6_3_landing_length |
| 1012.6.4 | Change in direction | flag | draft | Where the direction of travel changes at a landing between ramp runs, the landing is at least 60 by 60 inches (36 by 36 inches within Group R-2 and R-3 dwelling or sleeping units not required to be Accessible, Type A or Type B units, 1108): ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.6.4 Change in direction. Where changes in direction of travel occur at landings provided between ramp runs, the landing shall be 60 inches by 60 inches (1524 mm by 1524 mm) minimum. Exception: In Group R-2 and R-3 individual dwelling or sleeping units that are not required to be Accessible units, Type A units or Type B units in accordance with Section 1108, landings are permitted to be 36 inches by 36 inches (914 mm by 914 mm) minimum. | drafted:general.bc_1012_6_4_change_in_direction |
| 1012.6.5 | Doorways | reference | draft | A permission: ICC A117.1's maneuvering clearances at a doorway may overlap a ramp landing; read with 1012.6's flag, which quotes it. | 1012.6.5 Doorways. Where doorways are located adjacent to a ramp landing, maneuvering clearances required by ICC A117.1 are permitted to overlap the required landing area. | drafted:general.bc_1012_6_landings |
| 1012.7 | Ramp construction | flag | draft | A ramp is built of materials the building's type of construction permits (wood handrails allowed in every type): construction types and materials aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1012.7 Ramp construction. Ramps shall be built of materials consistent with the types permitted for the type of construction of the building, except that wood handrails shall be permitted for all types of construction. | drafted:general.bc_1012_7_ramp_construction |
| 1012.7.1 | Ramp surface | flag | draft | A ramp's surface is of slip-resistant materials, securely attached: finishes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1012.7.1 Ramp surface. The surface of ramps shall be of slip-resistant materials that are securely attached. | drafted:general.bc_1012_7_1_ramp_surface |
| 1012.7.2 | Outdoor conditions | flag | draft | Outdoor ramps and their outdoor approaches are designed so water doesn't accumulate on walking surfaces: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.7.2 Outdoor conditions. Outdoor ramps and outdoor approaches to ramps shall be designed so that water will not accu- mulate on walking surfaces. | drafted:general.bc_1012_7_2_outdoor_conditions |
| 1012.8 | Handrails | flag | draft | A ramp rising more than 6 inches has handrails on both sides, complying with 1014: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.8 Handrails. Ramps with a rise greater than 6 inches (152 mm) shall have handrails on both sides. Handrails shall comply with Section 1014. | drafted:general.bc_1012_8_handrails |
| 1012.9 | Guards | flag | draft | Guards where 1015 requires them on a ramp, built to 1015 (not brought in yet: section group 7's): ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.9 Guards. Guards shall be provided where required by Section 1015 and shall be constructed in accordance with Section 1015. | drafted:general.bc_1012_9_guards |
| 1012.10 | Edge protection | flag | draft | Each side of a ramp run and of a ramp landing has edge protection: a curb at least 4 inches high, or a rail, wall or barrier (a barrier stopping a 4-inch sphere anywhere within 4 inches of the floor or ground) (1012.10.1), or the floor or ground extending at least 12 inches beyond the inside face of a handrail complying with 1014 (1012.10.2); not required on a ramp that needs no handrails and has flared sides per ICC A117.1's curb ramp provisions, on a landing's sides serving an adjoining ramp run or stairway, or on a landing's sides with a dropoff of at most 1/2 inch within 10 inches of the required landing area: ramps aren't measured for the Building Code yet, so a person's check. | 1012.10 Edge protection. Edge protection complying with Section 1012.10.1 or 1012.10.2 shall be provided on each side of ramp runs and at each side of ramp landings. Exceptions: 1. Edge protection is not required on ramps that are not required to have handrails, provided they have flared sides that comply with the ICC A117.1 curb ramp provisions. 2. Edge protection is not required on the sides of ramp landings serving an adjoining ramp run or stairway. 3. Edge protection is not required on the sides of ramp landings having a vertical dropoff of not more than 1/2 inch (12.7 mm) within 10 inches (254 mm) horizontally of the required landing area. | drafted:general.bc_1012_10_edge_protection |
| 1012.10.1 | Curb, rail, wall or barrier | reference | draft | One of 1012.10's two ways to give edge protection (a curb at least 4 inches high, or a rail, wall or barrier, a barrier stopping a 4-inch sphere): read with 1012.10's flag, which quotes it. | 1012.10.1 Curb, rail, wall or barrier. A curb, rail, wall or barrier shall be provided to serve as edge protection. A curb shall be not less than 4 inches (102 mm) in height. Barriers shall be constructed so that the barrier prevents the passage of a 4-inch-diameter (102 mm) sphere, where any portion of the sphere is within 4 inches (102 mm) of the floor or ground surface. | drafted:general.bc_1012_10_edge_protection |
| 1012.10.2 | Extended floor or ground surface | reference | draft | The other of 1012.10's two ways (the floor or ground 12 inches past a handrail's inside face): read with 1012.10's flag, which quotes it. | 1012.10.2 Extended floor or ground surface. The floor or ground surface of the ramp run or landing shall extend 12 inches (305 mm) minimum beyond the inside face of a handrail complying with Section 1014. | drafted:general.bc_1012_10_edge_protection |
| 1013.1 | Where required | flag | draft | Exits and exit access doors are marked by an approved exit sign readily visible from any direction of egress travel; signs show the direction of travel where an exit or the path isn't immediately visible; intervening doors within exits are marked; and no point in an exit access corridor or exit passageway is farther than 100 feet, or the sign's listed viewing distance if less, from the nearest visible sign; not required in rooms or areas needing only one exit or exit access, at main exterior exit doors or gates obviously identifiable as exits where the building official approves, in Group U, within Group R-1, R-2 or R-3 sleeping or dwelling units, in Group I-3's dayrooms, sleeping rooms and dormitories, or on the seating side of Group A-4 and A-5 vomitories where signs in the concourse are readily apparent from the vomitories and egress lighting identifies each vomitory or opening in an emergency: exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.1 Where required. Exits and exit access doors shall be marked by an approved exit sign readily visible from any direction of egress travel. The path of egress travel to exits and within exits shall be marked by readily visible exit signs to clearly indicate the direction of egress travel in cases where the exit or the path of egress travel is not immediately visible to the occupants. Intervening means of egress doors within exits shall be marked by exit signs. Exit sign placement shall be such that any point in an exit access corridor or exit passageway is within 100 feet (30 480 mm) or the listed viewing distance of the sign, whichever is less, from the near- est visible exit sign. Exceptions: 1. Exit signs are not required in rooms or areas that require only one exit or exit access. 2. Main exterior exit doors or gates that are obviously and clearly identifiable as exits need not have exit signs where approved by the building official. 3. Exit signs are not required in occupancies in Group U and individual sleeping units or dwelling units in Group R-1, R-2 or R-3. 4. Exit signs are not required in dayrooms, sleeping rooms or dormitories in occupancies in Group I-3. 5. In occupancies in Groups A-4 and A-5, exit signs are not required on the seating side of vomitories or openings into seat- ing areas where exit signs are provided in the concourse that are readily apparent from the vomitories. Egress lighting is provided to identify each vomitory or opening within the seating area in an emergency. | drafted:general.bc_1013_1_exit_signs_where_required |
| 1013.2 | Low-level exit signs in Group R-1 | flag | draft | Where 1013.1 requires exit signs in Group R-1, low-level exit signs complying with 1013.5 are added in every area serving guest rooms, their bottom at least 10 and at most 18 inches above the floor, flush on the door or wall, a wall sign's edge within 4 inches of the door frame on the latch side; not required in a building sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1): exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.2 Low-level exit signs in Group R-1. Where exit signs are required in Group R-1 occupancies by Section 1013.1, additional low- level exit signs shall be provided in all areas serving guest rooms in Group R-1 occupancies and shall comply with Section 1013.5. The bottom of the sign shall be not less than 10 inches (254 mm) nor more than 18 inches (455 mm) above the floor level. The sign shall be flush mounted to the door or wall. Where mounted on the wall, the edge of the sign shall be within 4 inches (102 mm) of the door frame on the latch side. Exception: Low-level exit signs are not required in Group R-1 occupancies when the building is equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. | drafted:general.bc_1013_2_low_level_exit_signs_group_r1 |
| 1013.3 | Illumination | flag | draft | Exit signs are internally or externally lit (the tactile signs 1013.4 requires need not be): exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.3 Illumination. Exit signs shall be internally or externally illuminated. Exception: Tactile signs required by Section 1013.4 need not be provided with illumination. | drafted:general.bc_1013_3_exit_sign_illumination |
| 1013.4 | Raised character and braille exit signs | flag | draft | Where exit signs are provided at an area of refuge with direct access to a stairway, an exterior area for assisted rescue, an exit stairway or ramp, an exit passageway, a horizontal exit and the exit discharge, a sign reading "EXIT" in visual characters, raised characters and braille complying with ICC A117.1 is provided: signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.4 Raised character and braille exit signs. Where exit signs are provided at an area of refuge with direct access to a stairway, an exterior area for assisted rescue, an exit stairway or ramp, an exit passageway, a horizontal exit and the exit discharge, a sign stat- ing "EXIT" in visual characters, raised characters and braille and complying with ICC A117.1 shall be provided. | drafted:general.bc_1013_4_raised_character_braille_exit_signs |
| 1013.5 | Internally illuminated exit signs | flag | draft | Electrically powered, self-luminous and photoluminescent exit signs are listed and labeled to UL 924, installed to the maker's instructions and Chapter 27, and lit at all times: exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.5 Internally illuminated exit signs. Electrically powered, self-luminous and photoluminescent exit signs shall be listed and labeled in accordance with UL 924 and shall be installed in accordance with the manufacturer’s instructions and Chapter 27. Exit signs shall be illuminated at all times. | drafted:general.bc_1013_5_internally_illuminated_exit_signs |
| 1013.5.1 | Photoluminescent exit signs | flag | draft | A photoluminescent exit sign has a light source to charge it as its maker instructs: exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.5.1 Photoluminescent exit signs. Photoluminescent exit signs shall be provided with an illumination source to charge the exit sign in accordance with the manufacturer’s instructions. | drafted:general.bc_1013_5_1_photoluminescent_exit_signs |
| 1013.6 | Externally illuminated exit signs | reference | draft | A pointer: externally illuminated exit signs comply with 1013.6.1 to 1013.6.3, each labeled on its own; quoted by 1013.6.1's flag. | 1013.6 Externally illuminated exit signs. Externally illuminated exit signs shall comply with Sections 1013.6.1 through 1013.6.3. | drafted:general.bc_1013_6_1_exit_sign_graphics |
| 1013.6.1 | Graphics | flag | draft | Every externally lit exit sign and directional exit sign (1013.6) has plainly legible letters at least 6 inches high with principal strokes at least 3/4 inch wide; the letters of "EXIT", but the I, at least 2 inches wide and spaced at least 3/8 inch apart, larger signs in proportion; "EXIT" in high contrast with the background and clearly discernible whether or not the sign's lighting is on, and a chevron, if any, not readily turned (Gordium reads this section's "every exit sign" as the externally lit ones, 1013.6, a reading to confirm): exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.6.1 Graphics. Every exit sign and directional exit sign shall have plainly legible letters not less than 6 inches (152 mm) high with the principal strokes of the letters not less than 3/4 inch (19.1 mm) wide. The word “EXIT” shall have letters having a width not less than 2 inches (51 mm) wide, except the letter “I,” and the minimum spacing between letters shall be not less than 3/8 inch (9.5 mm). Signs larger than the minimum established in this section shall have letter widths, strokes and spacing in proportion to their height. The word “EXIT” shall be in high contrast with the background and shall be clearly discernible when the means of exit sign illu- mination is or is not energized. If a chevron directional indicator is provided as part of the exit sign, the construction shall be such that the direction of the chevron directional indicator cannot be readily changed. | drafted:general.bc_1013_6_1_exit_sign_graphics |
| 1013.6.2 | Exit sign illumination | flag | draft | An exit sign lit from an external source has at least 5 footcandles on its face: exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.6.2 Exit sign illumination. The face of an exit sign illuminated from an external source shall have an intensity of not less than 5 footcandles (54 lux). | drafted:general.bc_1013_6_2_exit_sign_illumination_intensity |
| 1013.6.3 | Power source | flag | draft | Exit signs are lit at all times, connected to an emergency power system (storage batteries, unit equipment or an on-site generator, installed per Chapter 27) that keeps them lit at least 90 minutes after primary power is lost, not by unit equipment batteries alone in Group I-2 Condition 2; approved types lit continuously for 90 minutes independently of external power need no connection (Gordium reads this for every exit sign, internally lit ones too, since 2702.2.6 sends every exit sign's emergency power here, a reading to confirm): exit signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1013.6.3 Power source. Exit signs shall be illuminated at all times. To ensure continued illumination for a duration of not less than 90 minutes in case of primary power loss, the sign illumination means shall be connected to an emergency power system provided from storage batteries, unit equipment or an on-site generator. The installation of the emergency power system shall be in accordance with Chapter 27. Group I-2, Condition 2 exit sign illumination shall not be provided by unit equipment batteries only. Exception: Approved exit sign illumination types that provide continuous illumination independent of external power sources for a duration of not less than 90 minutes, in case of primary power loss, are not required to be connected to an emergency electrical system. | drafted:general.bc_1013_6_3_exit_sign_power_source |
| 1016.2 | Egress through intervening spaces | rule | draft | Where egress may pass through intervening rooms: never through a room that can be locked (item 4), never through kitchens, storage rooms or closets (item 6, with its exceptions), never from dwelling units or sleeping areas through other sleeping areas or toilet rooms (item 5): measurable from the path; through other adjoining rooms only where accessory with a discernible path (items 2 and 3): a person's judgment. | 4. An exit access shall not pass through a room that can be locked to prevent egress. | drafted:general.bc_1016_2_intervening_spaces, drafted:general.bc_1016_2_other_occupancies, drafted:general.bc_1016_2_intervening_spaces_other_groups |
| 1016.2.1 | Multiple tenants | flag | draft | Each tenant space, dwelling unit and sleeping unit reaches the exits without passing through adjacent ones, with its exception: which spaces are tenants is the architect's statement, a person's check. | 1016.2.1 Multiple tenants. Where more than one tenant occupies any one floor of a building or structure, each tenant space, dwelling unit and sleeping unit shall be provided with access to the required exits without passing through adjacent tenant spaces, dwelling units and sleeping units. | drafted:general.bc_1016_2_1_multiple_tenants |
| 1017.2 | Limitations | rule | draft | Caps exit access travel distance at Table 1017.2's values: measurable per story. | 1017.2 Limitations. Exit access travel distance shall not exceed the values given in Table 1017.2. | drafted:general.bc_1017_2_travel_distance, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_a_e_f1_m_r_s1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_f2_s2_u, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h2, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h4, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i2_i3 |
| Table 1017.2 | EXIT ACCESS TRAVEL DISTANCE | rule | draft | The values 1017.2 uses, row by row: each group's longest exit access travel distance without an automatic sprinkler system (or not permitted) and with one (note b: 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2; note c: 903.3.1.1 only; note d: Group H's 903.2.5.1). | I-4 150 200[fnc] | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_2_6_r3_r4_nfpa_13d, drafted:general.bc_1017_2_travel_distance, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_other_occupancies, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_a_e_f1_m_r_s1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_f2_s2_u, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h2, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h4, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i2_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_note_a, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_note_d_group_h |
| 1017.2.1 | Exterior egress balcony increase | flag | draft | Permits up to 100 feet more where the last part of the exit access is on an exterior egress balcony (Section 1021, its own flags) at least as long as the increase: an allowance a person checks. | 1017.2.1 Exterior egress balcony increase. Exit access travel distances specified in Table 1017.2 shall be increased up to an additional 100 feet (30 480 mm) provided that the last portion of the exit access leading to the exit occurs on an exterior egress | drafted:general.bc_1017_2_travel_distance, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_a_e_f1_m_r_s1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_f2_s2_u, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h2, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h4, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i2_i3, drafted:general.bc_1017_2_1_balcony_increase |
| 1017.2.2 | Groups F-1 and S-1 increase | flag | draft | 400 feet in one-story Group F-1 or S-1 with 24-foot ceilings, sprinklered per 903.3.1.1: an allowance for groups other than I-4, a person checks. | 1017.2.2 Groups F-1 and S-1 increase. The maximum exit access travel distance shall be 400 feet (122 m) in Group F-1 or S-1 occupancies where all of the following conditions are met: | drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_a_e_f1_m_r_s1, drafted:general.bc_1017_2_2_f1_s1_increase |
| 1017.2.3 | Group H-5 increase | flag | draft | 300 feet in Group H-5 fabrication areas meeting four conditions: an allowance for Group H-5, a person checks. | 1017.2.3 Group H-5 increase. The maximum exit access travel distance shall be 300 feet (91 m) in the fabrication areas of Group H-5 occupancies where all of the following conditions are met: | drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h5, drafted:general.bc_1017_2_3_h5_increase |
| 1017.3 | Measurement | reference | draft | Says how exit access travel distance is measured (from the most remote point of each room, area or space, along the natural and unobstructed path, to the entrance to the nearest exit): the measure's definition, no limit. | 1017.3 Measurement. Exit access travel distance shall be measured from the most remote point of each room, area or space along the natural and unobstructed path of horizontal and vertical egress travel to the entrance to an exit. Where more than one exit is required, exit access travel distance shall be measured to the nearest exit. | drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_f2_s2_u |
| 1017.3.2.3 | Exit access travel distance at other than the level of exit discharge | flag | draft | Above or below the level of exit discharge, travel within the atrium is at most 200 feet: not measured from the drawing yet (which part of a path is in an atrium), a person checks. | 1017.3.2.3 Exit access travel distance at other than the level of exit discharge. Where the path of egress travel is not at the level of exit discharge from the atrium, that portion of the total permitted exit access travel distance that occurs within the atrium shall be not greater than 200 feet (60 960 mm). | drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_a_e_f1_m_r_s1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_f2_s2_u, drafted:general.bc_1017_3_2_3_atrium |
| 1018.1 | General | flag | draft | Aisles or aisle accessways are provided from every occupied portion of the exit access holding seats, tables, furnishings, displays and similar fixtures or equipment, their minimum width or required capacity unobstructed but for encroachments complying with 1005.7; in a room or space used for assembly, aisles and aisle accessways comply with 1030 (1018.2): furniture and fixtures aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1018.1 General. Aisles and aisle accessways serving as a portion of the exit access in the means of egress system shall comply with the requirements of this section. Aisles or aisle accessways shall be provided from all occupied portions of the exit access that contain seats, tables, furnishings, displays and similar fixtures or equipment. The minimum width or required capacity of aisles shall be unobstructed. Exception: Encroachments complying with Section 1005.7. | drafted:general.bc_1018_1_aisles_general |
| 1018.2 | Aisles in assembly spaces | reference | draft | A pointer: aisles and aisle accessways serving assembly spaces comply with 1030 (brought in whole by section groups 9h and 9i); quoted by 1018.1's flag. | 1018.2 Aisles in assembly spaces. Aisles and aisle accessways serving a room or space used for assembly purposes shall comply with Section 1030. | drafted:general.bc_1018_1_aisles_general |
| 1018.3 | Aisles in Groups B and M | flag | draft | In Groups B and M, an aisle is at least as wide as 1005.1 requires for the occupant load it serves and at least as wide as 1020.3 requires of corridors; a nonpublic aisle serving fewer than 50 people and not required to be accessible by Chapter 11 need not be wider than 28 inches: aisles between fixtures aren't drawn, so a person's check. | 1018.3 Aisles in Groups B and M. In Group B and M occupancies, the minimum clear aisle width shall be determined by Section 1005.1 for the occupant load served, but shall be not less than that required for corridors by Section 1020.3. Exception: Nonpublic aisles serving less than 50 people and not required to be accessible by Chapter 11 need not exceed 28 inches (711 mm) in width. | drafted:general.bc_1018_3_aisles_groups_b_m |
| 1018.4 | Aisle accessways in Group M | flag | draft | Group M merchandise pads: an aisle accessway on one side of each element, 30 inches clear, and a common path of at most 30 feet from any point in the pad (75 feet for not more than 50 occupants): the pads aren't drawn, so a person's check (general.bc_1018_4_merchandise_pads), and Table 1006.2.1's A, E, M row needs review over 30 feet where pads are stated or not ruled out. | 1018.4 Aisle accessways in Group M. An aisle accessway shall be provided on not less than one side of each element within the merchandise pad. The minimum clear width for an aisle accessway not required to be accessible shall be 30 inches (762 mm). The required clear width of the aisle accessway shall be measured perpendicular to the elements and merchandise within the merchan- | drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_a_e_m, drafted:general.bc_1018_4_merchandise_pads |
| 1018.5 | Aisles in other than assembly spaces and Groups B and M | flag | draft | Outside rooms or spaces used for assembly and outside Groups B and M, an aisle has the capacity 1005.1 requires for the occupant load it serves and is at least as wide as 1020.3 requires of corridors; a nonpublic aisle serving fewer than 50 people and not required to be accessible by Chapter 11 need not be wider than 28 inches: aisles between fixtures aren't drawn, so a person's check. | 1018.5 Aisles in other than assembly spaces and Groups B and M. In other than rooms or spaces used for assembly purposes and Group B and M occupancies, the minimum clear aisle capacity shall be determined by Section 1005.1 for the occupant load served, but the width shall be not less than that required for corridors by Section 1020.3. Exception: Nonpublic aisles serving less than 50 people and not required to be accessible by Chapter 11 need not exceed 28 inches (711 mm) in width. | drafted:general.bc_1018_5_aisles_other_occupancies |
| 1019.1 | General | reference | draft | Scope: exit access stairways and ramps comply with 1019, the stories they connect counting basements but not mezzanines; quoted by 1019.3's and 1019.4's flags. | 1019.1 General. Exit access stairways and ramps serving as an exit access component in a means of egress system shall comply with the requirements of this section. The number of stories connected by exit access stairways and ramps shall include basements, but not mezzanines. | drafted:general.bc_1019_3_exit_access_stairway_enclosure, drafted:general.bc_1019_4_exit_access_stairways_groups_i2_i3 |
| 1019.2 | All occupancies | reference | draft | A permission: an exit access stairway or ramp serving floor levels within one story needs no enclosure; quoted by 1019.3's and 1019.4's flags. | 1019.2 All occupancies. Exit access stairways and ramps that serve floor levels within a single story are not required to be enclosed. | drafted:general.bc_1019_3_exit_access_stairway_enclosure, drafted:general.bc_1019_4_exit_access_stairways_groups_i2_i3 |
| 1019.3 | Occupancies other than Groups I-2 and I-3 | flag | draft | Outside Groups I-2 and I-3, a floor opening holding an exit access stairway or ramp is enclosed in a shaft built to 713 (the stories it connects counting basements, not mezzanines; one serving floor levels within a single story needs no enclosure, 1019.2), except within a two-story opening complying with 712.1.9; connecting four stories or fewer within one Group R-1, R-2 or R-3 dwelling, sleeping or live/work unit; exit access stairways (not ramps) within a Group R-3 congregate residence or a Group R-4 facility; in a building sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 where the opening is no more than twice the stairway's or ramp's projected area, with a draft curtain and closely spaced sprinklers per NFPA 13 (outside Groups B and M, connecting no more than four stories); within an atrium complying with 404; in an open parking garage it alone serves; serving smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating within 1030.7's travel; between a balcony, gallery or press box and the main assembly floor; and exterior ones between occupiable roofs: which stairways are exit access stairways, and their enclosures, aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1019.3 Occupancies other than Groups I-2 and I-3. In other than Group I-2 and I-3 occupancies, floor openings containing exit access stairways or ramps shall be enclosed with a shaft enclosure constructed in accordance with Section 713. Exceptions: 1. Exit access stairways and ramps within a two-story opening complying with Section 712.1.9. 2. In Group R-1, R-2 or R-3 occupancies, exit access stairways and ramps connecting four stories or less serving and contained within an individual dwelling unit or sleeping unit or live/work unit. 3. Exit access stairways serving and contained within a Group R-3 congregate residence or a Group R-4 facility are not required to be enclosed. 4. Exit access stairways and ramps in buildings equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1, where the area of the vertical opening between stories does not exceed twice the horizontal projected area of the stairway or ramp and the opening is protected by a draft curtain and closely spaced sprinklers in accordance with NFPA 13. In other than Group B and M occupancies, this provision is limited to openings that do not connect more than four stories. 5. Exit access stairways and ramps within an atrium complying with the provisions of Section 404. 6. Exit access stairways and ramps in open parking garages that serve only the parking garage. 7. Exit access stairways and ramps serving smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating complying with the exit access travel distance requirements of Section 1030.7. 8. Exit access stairways and ramps between the balcony, gallery or press box and the main assembly floor in occupancies such as theaters, places of religious worship, auditoriums and sports facilities. 9. Exterior exit access stairways or ramps between occupiable roofs. | drafted:general.bc_1019_3_exit_access_stairway_enclosure |
| 1019.4 | Group I-2 and I-3 occupancies | flag | draft | In Groups I-2 and I-3, a floor opening between stories holding an exit access stairway or ramp is enclosed in a shaft built to 713 (the stories counting basements, not mezzanines, 1019.1; one serving floor levels within a single story needs no enclosure, 1019.2), but Group I-3's built to 408 need none: enclosures aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1019.4 Group I-2 and I-3 occupancies. In Group I-2 and I-3 occupancies, floor openings between stories containing exit access stair- ways or ramps are required to be enclosed with a shaft enclosure constructed in accordance with Section 713. Exception: In Group I-3 occupancies, exit access stairways or ramps constructed in accordance with Section 408 are not required to be enclosed. | drafted:general.bc_1019_4_exit_access_stairways_groups_i2_i3 |
| 1020.2 | Construction | rule | draft | Corridors are fire-resistance rated as Table 1020.2 asks, and walls required to be rated are fire partitions (Section 708, Chapter 7 part c's flags); exceptions 1 to 4 lift the rating (Group E rooms with doors straight outside, corridors within a dwelling or sleeping unit in Groups I-1 and R, open parking garages, a Group B space needing one means of egress), exception 5 allows unprotected openings on unrated exterior walls: the rating is checked per corridor against the table, the fire-partition construction a person's check. | 1020.2 Construction. Corridors shall be fire-resistance rated in accordance with Table 1020.2. The corridor walls required to be fire- resistance rated shall comply with Section 708 for fire partitions. | drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_h1_h2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_h4_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_a_b_e_f_m_s_u, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_r, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_i2, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_i1_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_i4, drafted:general.bc_1020_2_fire_partitions |
| Table 1020.2 | CORRIDOR FIRE-RESISTANCE RATING | rule | draft | The ratings 1020.2 uses, row by row, by occupancy and the occupant load the corridor serves, without and with an automatic sprinkler system (note c: 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 where allowed; note d: Group R-3 and R-4 with 903.3.1.3); Not Permitted without a sprinkler system for Groups H, R, I-1, I-2 and I-3. | H-1, H-2, H-3 All Not Permitted 1[fnc] H-4, H-5 Greater than 30 Not Permitted 1[fnc] A, B, E, F, M, S, U Greater than 30 1 0 R Greater than 10 Not Permitted 0.5[fnc]/1[fnd] I-2[fna] All Not Permitted 0 I-1, I-3 All Not Permitted 1[fnb,] [fnc] I-4 All 1 0 | drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_h1_h2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_h4_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_a_b_e_f_m_s_u, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_r, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_i2, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_i1_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_i4, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_other_occupancies, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_2_note_a_group_i2 |
| 1020.2.1 | Hoistway protection | flag | draft | Elevator hoistway doors in rated hoistway enclosures are protected per Section 716 (Chapter 7 part b's flags) and 3006.2 (not brought in): the elevators and their doors aren't drawn, so a person's check. | 1020.2.1 Hoistway protection. Elevator hoistway doors in elevators hoistway enclosures required to be fire-resistance rated shall be protected in accordance with Section 716. Elevator hoistway doors shall also be protected in accordance with Section 3006.2. | drafted:general.bc_1020_2_1_hoistway_protection |
| 1020.3 | Width and capacity | rule | draft | A corridor's width is at least Table 1020.3's, and its capacity that of 1005.1; in Group I-2, 96 inches only where beds or stretchers move (the exception): measurable per corridor from its width and the occupants it serves. | 1020.3 Width and capacity. The required capacity of corridors shall be determined as specified in Section 1005.1, but the minimum width shall be not less than that specified in Table 1020.3. | drafted:general.bc_1020_3_corridor_capacity, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_3_corridor_width |
| Table 1020.3 | MINIMUM CORRIDOR WIDTH | rule | draft | The widths 1020.3 uses, row by row: 44 inches for any facility not listed, 24 to mechanical, plumbing or electrical equipment, 36 with fewer than 50 occupants or within a dwelling unit, 72 in Group E with 100 or more and for stretcher traffic in ambulatory care facilities, 96 in Group I-2 where beds are moved. | Any facility not listed in this table 44 Access to and utilization of mechanical, plumbing or electrical systems or equipment 24 With an occupant load of less than 50 36 Within a dwelling unit 36 In Group E with a corridor having an occupant load of 100 or more 72 In corridors and areas serving stretcher traffic in ambulatory care facilities 72 Group I-2 in areas where required for bed movement 96 | drafted:general.bc_table_1020_3_corridor_width, drafted:general.bc_table_1020_3_other_occupancies |
| 1020.4 | Obstruction | flag | draft | A corridor's minimum width or required capacity stays unobstructed, but for encroachments per 1005.7 (its own flags since section group 5c): the width is measured between the walls and what the model draws, so doors, furniture and equipment it doesn't draw are a person's check. | 1020.4 Obstruction. The minimum width or required capacity of corridors shall be unobstructed. Exception: Encroachments complying with Section 1005.7. | drafted:general.bc_1020_4_obstruction |
| 1020.5 | Dead ends | rule | draft | Caps dead-end corridors at 20 feet where more than one exit or exit access doorway is required, or any length under 2.5 times their least width (exception 3); 50 feet for Groups B, E, F, I-1, M, R-1, R-2, S and U sprinklered per 903.3.1.1: measurable per corridor. | 1020.5 Dead ends. Where more than one exit or exit access doorway is required, the exit access shall be arranged such that dead-end corridors do not exceed 20 feet (6096 mm) in length. | drafted:general.bc_1020_5_dead_end, drafted:general.bc_1020_5_other_occupancies, drafted:general.bc_1020_5_dead_end_other_groups |
| 1020.6 | Air movement in corridors | flag | draft | Corridors don't serve as supply, return, exhaust, relief or ventilation air ducts, but for four exceptions (makeup air, return air within a dwelling unit or a small tenant space, health care pressurization): the air system isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1020.6 Air movement in corridors. Corridors shall not serve as supply, return, exhaust, relief or ventilation air ducts. | drafted:general.bc_1020_6_air_movement |
| 1020.6.1 | Corridor ceiling | reference | draft | A permission: the space above a corridor's ceiling may be a return air plenum under any of five conditions; part of 1020.6's check, a person's. | 1020.6.1 Corridor ceiling. Use of the space between the corridor ceiling and the floor or roof structure above as a return air plenum is permitted for one or more of the following conditions: | drafted:general.bc_1020_6_air_movement |
| 1020.7 | Corridor continuity | flag | draft | A fire-resistance-rated corridor runs unbroken from its entry to an exit, with no intervening room (foyers, lobbies and reception rooms built as corridors, and enclosed elevator lobbies, aside), its rating continued along an open stair or ramp: the routes don't follow a rating, so a person's check. | 1020.7 Corridor continuity. Fire-resistance-rated corridors shall be continuous from the point of entry to an exit, and shall not be interrupted by intervening rooms. | drafted:general.bc_1020_7_continuity |
| 1021.1 | General | flag | draft | A balcony used for egress meets the same requirements as a corridor for minimum width, required capacity, headroom, dead ends and projections: the routes measure interior corridors, not balconies, so a person's check. | 1021.1 General. Balconies used for egress purposes shall conform to the same requirements as corridors for minimum width, required capacity, headroom, dead ends and projections. | drafted:general.bc_1021_1_egress_balconies_as_corridors |
| 1021.2 | Wall separation | flag | draft | An exterior egress balcony is separated from the interior of the building by walls and opening protectives as required for corridors, except where it is served by at least two stairways and no dead-end travel passes an unprotected opening to reach a stairway: the walls' ratings and the balcony aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1021.2 Wall separation. Exterior egress balconies shall be separated from the interior of the building by walls and opening protec- tives as required for corridors. Exception: Separation is not required where the exterior egress balcony is served by not less than two stairways and a dead-end travel condition does not require travel past an unprotected opening to reach a stairway. | drafted:general.bc_1021_2_egress_balcony_separation |
| 1021.3 | Openness | flag | draft | The long side of an egress balcony is at least 50 percent open, the open area above the guards distributed so as to keep smoke or toxic gases from building up: balconies aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1021.3 Openness. The long side of an egress balcony shall be not less than 50 percent open, and the open area above the guards shall be so distributed as to minimize the accumulation of smoke or toxic gases. | drafted:general.bc_1021_3_egress_balcony_openness |
| 1021.4 | Location | flag | draft | An exterior egress balcony has a fire separation distance of at least 10 feet, measured at right angles from its exterior edge, to adjacent lot lines, to other portions of the building (treated as separate buildings) and to other buildings on the same lot unless their exterior walls and openings are protected per 705: balconies and their distances aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1021.4 Location. Exterior egress balconies shall have a minimum fire separation distance of 10 feet (3048 mm) measured at right angles from the exterior edge of the egress balcony to the following: 1. Adjacent lot lines. 2. Other portions of the building. 3. Other buildings on the same lot unless the adjacent building exterior walls and openings are protected in accordance with Section 705 based on fire separation distance. For the purposes of this section, other portions of the building shall be treated as separate buildings. | drafted:general.bc_1021_4_egress_balcony_location |
| 1022.1 | General | flag | draft | An exit is used for nothing that interferes with its use as a means of egress, its level of protection once reached isn't reduced before the exit discharge, and it is continuous from where it is entered to the exit discharge: what an exit is used for and its protection aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1022.1 General. Exits shall comply with Sections 1022 through 1027 and the applicable requirements of Sections 1003 through 1015. An exit shall not be used for any purpose that interferes with its function as a means of egress. Once a given level of exit protection is achieved, such level of protection shall not be reduced until arrival at the exit discharge. Exits shall be continuous from the point of entry into the exit to the exit discharge. | drafted:general.bc_1022_1_exits_general |
| 1022.2 | Exterior exit doors | flag | draft | A building used for human occupancy has at least one exterior door meeting 1010.1.1's size, and exterior exit doors meet 1010.1 as it applies (1022.2.1): each door's clear width and height are checked by 1010.1.1's rule, but that the building has such a door to the outside isn't worked out, so a person's check. | 1022.2 Exterior exit doors. Buildings or structures used for human occupancy shall have not less than one exterior door that meets the requirements of Section 1010.1.1. | drafted:general.bc_1022_2_exterior_exit_door |
| 1022.2.1 | Detailed requirements | reference | draft | A pointer: exterior exit doors comply with 1010.1 as it applies (brought in, its rules and flags); quoted by 1022.2's flag. | 1022.2.1 Detailed requirements. Exterior exit doors shall comply with the applicable requirements of Section 1010.1. | drafted:general.bc_1022_2_exterior_exit_door |
| 1022.2.2 | Arrangement | flag | draft | An exterior exit door leads directly to the exit discharge or the public way: what is outside a door isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1022.2.2 Arrangement. Exterior exit doors shall lead directly to the exit discharge or the public way. | drafted:general.bc_1022_2_2_exterior_exit_door_arrangement |
| 1023.1 | General | flag | draft | An interior exit stairway or ramp is enclosed and leads directly to the exterior, or is extended to it by an exit passageway meeting 1024, except as 1028.2 permits (no more than half of the exit stairways and ramps, by number and by width or capacity, through areas on the level of exit discharge or a vestibule, under its conditions), and is used only as a means of egress and a circulation path: which stairways are exit stairways, their enclosures and their use aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.1 General. Interior exit stairways and ramps serving as an exit component in a means of egress system shall comply with the requirements of this section. Interior exit stairways and ramps shall be enclosed and lead directly to the exterior of the building or shall be extended to the exterior of the building with an exit passageway conforming to the requirements of Section 1024, except as permitted in Section 1028.2. An interior exit stairway or ramp shall not be used for any purpose other than as a means of egress and a circulation path. | drafted:general.bc_1023_1_interior_exit_stairways_general |
| 1023.2 | Construction | flag | draft | An interior exit stairway or ramp enclosure is built as fire barriers per 707 or horizontal assemblies per 711, rated at least 2 hours where it connects four stories or more and at least 1 hour where fewer (counting basements, not mezzanines), and at least the rating of the floor assembly it passes through, up to 2 hours; except, as those sections allow, exit stairways and ramps in Group I-3 per 408.3.8, exit stairways (not ramps) within an atrium enclosed per 404.6, and exit stairways (not ramps) per 510.2 (a horizontal building separation): the enclosure's rating isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.2 Construction. Enclosures for interior exit stairways and ramps shall be constructed as fire barriers in accordance with Section 707 or horizontal assemblies constructed in accordance with Section 711, or both. Interior exit stairway and ramp enclosures | drafted:general.bc_1023_2_enclosure_construction |
| 1023.3 | Termination | flag | draft | An interior exit stairway or ramp ends at an exit discharge or a public way, or continues there through exit stairways, exit ramps and exit passageways built per 1023.2, 1023.3.1 and 1024 that form one continuous protected enclosure: where a stairway ends isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.3 Termination. Interior exit stairways and ramps shall terminate at an exit discharge or a public way. Exception: A combination of interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways, constructed in accordance with Sections 1023.2, 1023.3.1 and 1024, respectively, and forming a continuous protected enclosure, shall be permitted to extend an interior exit stairway or ramp to the exit discharge or a public way. | drafted:general.bc_1023_3_termination |
| 1023.3.1 | Extension | flag | draft | Where an exit passageway extends an interior exit stairway or ramp, a fire barrier per 707 or horizontal assembly per 711 rated at least as the stairway separates them, with a fire door assembly per 716 the only opening and no penetrations; but penetrations per 1023.5 are allowed, and no separation is needed where the extension has no openings or where the stairway and extension are pressurized per 909.20.4: the enclosures aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.3.1 Extension. Where interior exit stairways and ramps are extended to an exit discharge or a public way by an exit passage- way, the interior exit stairway and ramp shall be separated from the exit passageway by a fire barrier constructed in accordance with Section 707 or a horizontal assembly constructed in accordance with Section 711, or both. The fire-resistance rating shall be not less than that required for the interior exit stairway and ramp. A fire door assembly complying with Section 716 shall be installed in the fire barrier to provide a means of egress from the interior exit stairway and ramp to the exit passageway. Openings in the fire barrier other than the fire door assembly are prohibited. Penetrations of the fire barrier are prohibited. Exceptions: 1. Penetrations of the fire barrier in accordance with Section 1023.5 shall be permitted. 2. Separation between an interior exit stairway or ramp and the exit passageway extension shall not be required where there are no openings into the exit passageway extension. 3. Separation between an interior exit stairway or ramp and the exit passageway extension shall not be required where the interior exit stairway and the exit passageway extension are pressurized in accordance with Section 909.20.4. | drafted:general.bc_1023_3_1_extension |
| 1023.4 | Openings | flag | draft | Opening protectives of an interior exit stairway or ramp meet 716; its openings, other than unprotected exterior openings, are only those needed to enter it from normally occupied spaces and to leave it; and no elevator opens into it: the enclosure's openings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.4 Openings. Interior exit stairway and ramp opening protectives shall be in accordance with the requirements of Section 716. Openings in interior exit stairways and ramps other than unprotected exterior openings shall be limited to those required for exit access to the enclosure from normally occupied spaces and for egress from the enclosure. Elevators shall not open into interior exit stairways and ramps. | drafted:general.bc_1023_4_openings |
| 1023.5 | Penetrations | flag | draft | Nothing penetrates an interior exit stairway or ramp but its own ventilation or pressurization, fire protection, security and two-way communication systems, raceways for fire department communication or serving it (ending at a steel box of at most 16 square inches), and structural elements supporting it or a roof at its top, each protected per 714; no penetration or opening joins two adjacent exit stairways or ramps; membrane penetrations on the outside are allowed per 714.4.2: penetrations aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.5 Penetrations. Penetrations into or through interior exit stairways and ramps are prohibited except for the following: 1. Equipment and ductwork necessary for independent ventilation or pressurization. 2. Fire protection systems. 3. Security systems. 4. Two-way communication systems. 5. Electrical raceway for fire department communication systems. 6.
