Electrical raceway serving the interior exit stairway and ramp and terminating at a steel box not exceeding 16 square inches (0.010 m[fn2]). 7. Structural elements, such as beams or joists, supporting the interior exit stairway or ramp or enclosure. 8. Structural elements, such as beams or joists, supporting a roof at the top of the interior exit stairway or ramp. Such penetrations shall be protected in accordance with Section 714. There shall not be penetrations or communication open- ings, whether protected or not, between adjacent interior exit stairways and ramps. Exception: Membrane penetrations shall be permitted on the outside of the interior exit stairway and ramp. Such penetrations shall be protected in accordance with Section 714.4.2. | drafted:general.bc_1023_5_penetrations |
| 1023.6 | Ventilation | flag | draft | Equipment and ductwork ventilating an interior exit stairway or ramp is outside the building with ducts enclosed as shafts, or within the enclosure taking air from and discharging it directly outdoors (or through ducts enclosed as shafts), or within the building separated as shafts; openings into the rated construction are only those needed for maintenance and operation, protected per 716 for shaft enclosures; and the system is independent of the building's other ventilation: ventilation isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.6 Ventilation. Equipment and ductwork for interior exit stairway and ramp ventilation as permitted by Section 1023.5 shall comply with one of the following items: 1. Such equipment and ductwork shall be located exterior to the building and shall be directly connected to the interior exit stairway and ramp by ductwork enclosed in construction as required for shafts. 2. Where such equipment and ductwork is located within the interior exit stairway and ramp, the intake air shall be taken directly from the outdoors and the exhaust air shall be discharged directly to the outdoors, or such air shall be conveyed through ducts enclosed in construction as required for shafts. 3. Where located within the building, such equipment and ductwork shall be separated from the remainder of the building, including other mechanical equipment, with construction as required for shafts. In each case, openings into the fire-resistance-rated construction shall be limited to those needed for maintenance and operation and shall be protected by opening protectives in accordance with Section 716 for shaft enclosures. The interior exit stairway and ramp ventilation systems shall be independent of other building ventilation systems. | drafted:general.bc_1023_6_ventilation |
| 1023.7 | Interior exit stairway and ramp exterior walls | flag | draft | The exterior walls of an interior exit stairway or ramp meet 705; where nonrated walls or unprotected openings enclose its exterior and other parts of the building expose them at an angle under 180 degrees, construction within 10 feet meets 1023.7.1 and 1023.7.2: walls' ratings and the angles between them aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.7 Interior exit stairway and ramp exterior walls. Exterior walls of the interior exit stairway or ramp shall comply with the requirements of Section 705 for exterior walls. Where nonrated walls or unprotected openings enclose the exterior of the stairway or | drafted:general.bc_1023_7_exterior_walls |
| 1023.7.1 | Building exterior walls | flag | draft | Building exterior walls within 10 feet horizontally of a nonrated wall or unprotected opening of an interior exit stairway or ramp are rated at least 1 hour, their openings protected at least 3/4 hour, from the ground to a point 10 feet above the topmost landing or to the roof line, whichever is lower: walls' ratings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.7.1 Building exterior walls. Building exterior walls within 10 feet (3048 mm) horizontally of a nonrated wall or unprotected opening in an interior exit stairway or ramp shall have a fire-resistance rating of not less than 1 hour. Openings within such exterior walls shall be protected by opening protectives having a fire protection rating of not less than 3/4 hour. This construction shall extend vertically from the ground to a point 10 feet (3048 mm) above the topmost landing of the stairway or ramp, or to the roof line, whichever is lower. | drafted:general.bc_1023_7_1_building_exterior_walls |
| 1023.7.2 | Roof assemblies | flag | draft | Where an interior exit stairway or ramp rises above an adjacent roof of the same building, that roof is rated at least 1 hour and its openings protected at least 3/4 hour, for at least 10 feet from the stairway's exterior wall or to the roof's perimeter, whichever is less; but the roof need not be rated nor its openings protected beside the stairway's penthouse, unless the code otherwise requires it, nor where the stairway's exterior wall is rated 1 hour with openings protected at least 3/4 hour, extending at least 10 feet above the roof: roof ratings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.7.2 Roof assemblies. Where the interior exit stairway or ramp extends above an adjacent roof of the same building, the adjacent roof assembly shall have a fire-resistance rating of not less than 1 hour and openings shall be protected by opening protectives having a fire protection rating of not less than 3/4 hour. The fire-resistance rating and opening protection shall extend horizontally not less than 10 feet (3048 mm) from the exterior wall of the stairway or ramp, or to the perimeter of the adjacent roof, whichever is less. Exceptions: 1. The roof assembly need not be rated and openings in the roof need not be protected where they are adjacent to the penthouse of the stairway or ramp, unless otherwise required by this code. 2. The adjacent roof assembly need not be rated and adjacent openings in the roof need not be protected where the exterior wall of the stairway or ramp has a fire-resistance rating of 1 hour and openings are protected by opening protectives having a fire protection rating of not less than 3/4 hour, extending not less than 10 feet (3048 mm) above the roof. | drafted:general.bc_1023_7_2_roof_assemblies |
| 1023.8 | Barrier at level of exit discharge | flag | draft | An interior exit stairway or ramp continues below its level of exit discharge only with an approved barrier at that level to keep people from going on to the levels below, with directional exit signs per 1013: whether a stairway continues below is not in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.8 Barrier at level of exit discharge. An interior exit stairway and ramp shall not continue below its level of exit discharge unless an approved barrier is provided at the level of exit discharge to prevent persons from unintentionally continuing into levels below. Directional exit signs shall be provided as specified in Section 1013. | drafted:general.bc_1023_8_barrier_at_exit_discharge |
| 1023.9 | Stairway identification signs | flag | draft | At each floor landing of an interior exit stairway or ramp connecting more than three stories, a sign states the floor level, the top and bottom terminus, the stairway's identification, the story of and direction to the exit discharge, and whether the roof can be reached for the fire department, its bottom at least 5 feet above the landing and readily visible with the doors open or closed: signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.9 Stairway identification signs. A sign shall be provided at each floor landing in an interior exit stairway and ramp connecting more than three stories designating the floor level, the terminus of the top and bottom of the interior exit stairway and ramp and the identification of the stairway or ramp. The signage shall state the story of and direction to the exit discharge, and the availability of roof access from the interior exit stairway and ramp for the fire department. The bottom of the sign shall be located not less than 5 feet (1524 mm) above the floor landing in a position that is readily visible when the doors are in the open and closed positions. | drafted:general.bc_1023_9_stairway_identification_signs |
| 1023.9.1 | Signage requirements | flag | draft | A stairway identification sign is at least 18 by 12 inches, its stairway identification letters at least 1 1/2 inches high, its floor number at least 5 inches high in the center, other lettering at least 1 inch, with nonglare, contrasting characters, and of 1025.4's materials in a building 1025 covers: signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.9.1 Signage requirements. Stairway identification signs shall comply with all of the following requirements: 1. The signs shall be a minimum size of 18 inches (457 mm) by 12 inches (305 mm). 2. The letters designating the identification of the interior exit stairway and ramp shall be not less than 1 1/2 inches (38 mm) in height. 3. The number designating the floor level shall be not less than 5 inches (127 mm) in height and located in the center of the sign. 4. Other lettering and numbers shall be not less than 1 inch (25 mm) in height. 5. Characters and their background shall have a nonglare finish. Characters shall contrast with their background, with either light characters on a dark background or dark characters on a light background. 6. Where signs required by Section 1023.9 are installed in the interior exit stairways and ramps of buildings subject to Section 1025, the signs shall be made of the same materials as required by Section 1025.4. | drafted:general.bc_1023_9_1_stairway_sign_requirements |
| 1023.10 | Elevator lobby identification signs | flag | draft | At an interior exit stairway landing where two or more doors lead to the floor, a door opening directly into an enclosed elevator lobby is identified "Elevator Lobby" on or beside it, per 1023.9.1 items 4, 5 and 6: doors' signs and elevator lobbies aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.10 Elevator lobby identification signs. At landings in interior exit stairways where two or more doors lead to the floor level, any door with direct access to an enclosed elevator lobby shall be identified by signage located on the door or directly adjacent to the door stating “Elevator Lobby.” Signage shall be in accordance with Section 1023.9.1, Items 4, 5 and 6. | drafted:general.bc_1023_10_elevator_lobby_signs |
| 1023.11 | Tactile floor-level signs | flag | draft | Where an interior exit stairway or ramp has floor level signs, a floor-level sign in visual characters, raised characters and braille per ICC A117.1 is at each floor-level landing beside the door into the corridor: signs aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.11 Tactile floor-level signs. Where floor level signs are provided in interior exit stairways and ramps, a floor-level sign identify- ing the floor level in visual characters, raised characters and braille complying with ICC A117.1 shall be located at each floor-level landing adjacent to the door leading from the interior exit stairway and ramp into the corridor. | drafted:general.bc_1023_11_tactile_floor_level_signs |
| 1023.12 | Smokeproof enclosures | flag | draft | Where 403.5.4 (high-rise buildings), 405.7.2 (underground buildings) or 412.2.2.1 (airport traffic control towers) requires it, an interior exit stairway or ramp is a smokeproof enclosure per 909.20: smokeproof enclosures aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.12 Smokeproof enclosures. Where required by Section 403.5.4, 405.7.2 or 412.2.2.1, interior exit stairways and ramps shall be smokeproof enclosures in accordance with Section 909.20. | drafted:general.bc_1023_12_smokeproof_enclosures |
| 1023.12.1 | Termination and extension | flag | draft | A smokeproof enclosure ends at an exit discharge or a public way, or is extended per 1023.3 by an exit passageway with no openings but 1023.3.1's fire door assembly and those for leaving it, separated by 2-hour fire barriers per 707 or horizontal assemblies per 711; but openings into the passageway are allowed where it is protected and pressurized as the enclosure is and the openings are protected as for access from other floors, the barrier between the enclosure and the passageway isn't needed where the passageway is protected and pressurized as the enclosure is, and the enclosure may egress through areas on the level of exit discharge or vestibules as 1028 allows (1028.2: no more than half of the exit stairways and ramps, under its conditions): smokeproof enclosures aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.12.1 Termination and extension. A smokeproof enclosure shall terminate at an exit discharge or a public way. The smoke- proof enclosure shall be permitted to be extended by an exit passageway in accordance with Section 1023.3. The exit passageway shall be without openings other than the fire door assembly required by Section 1023.3.1 and those necessary for egress from the exit passageway. The exit passageway shall be separated from the remainder of the building by 2-hour fire barriers constructed in accordance with Section 707 or horizontal assemblies constructed in accordance with Section 711, or both. Exceptions: 1. Openings in the exit passageway serving a smokeproof enclosure are permitted where the exit passageway is protected and pressurized in the same manner as the smokeproof enclosure, and openings are protected as required for access from other floors. 2. The fire barrier separating the smokeproof enclosure from the exit passageway is not required, provided that the exit passageway is protected and pressurized in the same manner as the smokeproof enclosure. | drafted:general.bc_1023_12_1_smokeproof_termination |
| 1023.12.2 | Enclosure access | flag | draft | A stairway or ramp in a smokeproof enclosure is entered through a vestibule or an open exterior balcony, but not where it uses the pressurization alternative per 909.20.4: smokeproof enclosures aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.12.2 Enclosure access. Access to the stairway or ramp within a smokeproof enclosure shall be by way of a vestibule or an open exterior balcony. Exception: Access is not required by way of a vestibule or exterior balcony for stairways and ramps using the pressurization alternative complying with Section 909.20.4. | drafted:general.bc_1023_12_2_smokeproof_access |
| 1023.13 | Standpipes | flag | draft | Standpipes and standpipe hose connections are provided where 905.3 and 905.4 require them: standpipes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1023.13 Standpipes. Standpipes and standpipe hose connections shall be provided where required by Sections 905.3 and 905.4. | drafted:general.bc_1023_13_standpipes |
| 1024.1 | General | flag | draft | An exit passageway serving as an exit component is used for nothing but a means of egress and a circulation path: what a room is used for isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.1 General. Exit passageways serving as an exit component in a means of egress system shall comply with the requirements of this section. An exit passageway shall not be used for any purpose other than as a means of egress and a circulation path. | drafted:general.bc_1024_1_exit_passageways_general |
| 1024.2 | Width and capacity | flag | draft | An exit passageway has the capacity 1005.1 requires and is at least 44 inches wide, or 36 inches where it serves fewer than 50 occupants, its minimum width or required capacity unobstructed but for encroachments complying with 1005.7: the routes measure corridors' width, not an exit passageway's, so a person's check. | 1024.2 Width and capacity. The required capacity of exit passageways shall be determined as specified in Section 1005.1 but the minimum width shall be not less than 44 inches (1118 mm), except that exit passageways serving an occupant load of less than 50 shall be not less than 36 inches (914 mm) in width. The minimum width or required capacity of exit passageways shall be unobstructed. Exception: Encroachments complying with Section 1005.7. | drafted:general.bc_1024_2_exit_passageway_width |
| 1024.3 | Construction | flag | draft | An exit passageway's walls, floors and ceilings are rated at least 1 hour and at least as any interior exit stairway or ramp it connects to, built as fire barriers per 707 or horizontal assemblies per 711: ratings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.3 Construction. Exit passageway enclosures shall have walls, floors and ceilings of not less than a 1-hour fire-resistance rating, and not less than that required for any connecting interior exit stairway or ramp. Exit passageways shall be constructed as fire barri- ers in accordance with Section 707 or horizontal assemblies constructed in accordance with Section 711, or both. | drafted:general.bc_1024_3_exit_passageway_construction |
| 1024.4 | Termination | flag | draft | An exit passageway on the level of exit discharge ends at an exit discharge, and one on another level ends at an exit: where a passageway ends isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.4 Termination. Exit passageways on the level of exit discharge shall terminate at an exit discharge. Exit passageways on other levels shall terminate at an exit. | drafted:general.bc_1024_4_exit_passageway_termination |
| 1024.5 | Openings | flag | draft | Opening protectives of an exit passageway meet 716; except as 402.8.7 permits, its openings, other than unprotected exterior openings, are only those needed to enter it from normally occupied spaces and to leave it; one extending an interior exit stairway or ramp meets 1023.3.1; and no elevator opens into it: the openings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.5 Openings. Exit passageway opening protectives shall be in accordance with the requirements of Section 716. Except as permitted in Section 402.8.7, openings in exit passageways other than unprotected exterior openings shall be limited to those necessary for exit access to the exit passageway from normally occupied spaces and for egress from the exit passageway. Where an interior exit stairway or ramp is extended to an exit discharge or a public way by an exit passageway, the exit passageway shall comply with Section 1023.3.1. Elevators shall not open into an exit passageway. | drafted:general.bc_1024_5_exit_passageway_openings |
| 1024.6 | Penetrations | flag | draft | Nothing penetrates an exit passageway but its own ventilation or pressurization, fire protection, security and two-way communication systems, raceways for fire department communication or serving it (ending at a steel box of at most 16 square inches), and structural elements supporting a floor or roof at its top, each protected per 714; no penetration or opening joins two adjacent exit passageways; membrane penetrations on the outside are allowed per 714.4.2: penetrations aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.6 Penetrations. Penetrations into or through an exit passageway are prohibited except for the following: 1. Equipment and ductwork necessary for independent ventilation or pressurization. 2. Fire protection systems. 3. Security systems. 4. Two-way communication systems. 5. Electrical raceway for fire department communication. 6. Electrical raceway serving the exit passageway and terminating at a steel box not exceeding 16 square inches (0.010 m[fn2]). 7. Structural elements, such as beams or joists, supporting a floor or roof at the top of the exit passageway. Such penetrations shall be protected in accordance with Section 714. There shall not be penetrations or communicating open- ings, whether protected or not, between adjacent exit passageways. Exception: Membrane penetrations shall be permitted on the outside of the exit passageway. Such penetrations shall be protected in accordance with Section 714.4.2. | drafted:general.bc_1024_6_exit_passageway_penetrations |
| 1024.7 | Ventilation | flag | draft | Equipment and ductwork ventilating an exit passageway is outside the building with ducts enclosed as shafts, or within the passageway taking air from and discharging it directly outdoors (or through ducts enclosed as shafts), or within the building separated as shafts; openings into the rated construction are only those needed for maintenance and operation, protected per 716 for shaft enclosures; and the system is independent of the building's other ventilation: ventilation isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.7 Ventilation. Equipment and ductwork for exit passageway ventilation as permitted by Section 1024.6 shall comply with one of the following: 1. The equipment and ductwork shall be located exterior to the building and shall be directly connected to the exit passage- way by ductwork enclosed in construction as required for shafts. 2. Where the equipment and ductwork is located within the exit passageway, the intake air shall be taken directly from the outdoors and the exhaust air shall be discharged directly to the outdoors, or the air shall be conveyed through ducts enclosed in construction as required for shafts. 3. Where located within the building, the equipment and ductwork shall be separated from the remainder of the building, including other mechanical equipment, with construction as required for shafts. In each case, openings into the fire-resistance-rated construction shall be limited to those needed for maintenance and operation and shall be protected by opening protectives in accordance with Section 716 for shaft enclosures. Exit passageway ventilation systems shall be independent of other building ventilation systems. | drafted:general.bc_1024_7_exit_passageway_ventilation |
| 1024.8 | Exit passageway exterior walls | flag | draft | An exit passageway's exterior walls meet 705; where nonrated walls or unprotected openings enclose its exterior and other parts of the building expose them at an angle under 180 degrees, building exterior walls within 10 feet horizontally are rated at least 1 hour with openings protected at least 3/4 hour, from the ground to 10 feet above the passageway's floor or to the roof line, whichever is lower: ratings and angles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.8 Exit passageway exterior walls. Exterior walls of the exit passageway shall comply with Section 705. Where nonrated walls or unprotected openings enclose the exterior of the exit passageway and the walls or openings are exposed by other parts of the building at an angle of less than 180 degrees (3.14 rad), the building exterior walls within 10 feet (3048 mm) horizontally of a nonrated wall or unprotected opening shall have a fire-resistance rating of not less than 1 hour. Openings within such exterior walls shall be | drafted:general.bc_1024_8_exit_passageway_exterior_walls |
| 1024.9 | Standpipes | flag | draft | Standpipes and standpipe hose connections are provided where 905.3 and 905.4 require them: standpipes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1024.9 Standpipes. Standpipes and standpipe hose connections shall be provided where required by Sections 905.3 and 905.4. | drafted:general.bc_1024_9_exit_passageway_standpipes |
| 1025.1 | General | flag | draft | A high-rise building (one with an occupied floor or occupiable roof more than 75 feet above the lowest level of fire department vehicle access, 202) of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 has approved luminous egress path markings delineating the exit path in its interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways, per 1025.2.1 to 1025.2.6.3 (1025.2), but not on the level of exit discharge in lobbies serving as part of the exit path per 1028.2 exception 1: whether the building is a high-rise and its markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.1 General. Approved luminous egress path markings delineating the exit path shall be provided in high-rise buildings of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 occupancies in accordance with this section. Exception: Luminous egress path markings shall not be required on the level of exit discharge in lobbies that serve as part of the exit path in accordance with Section 1028.2, Exception 1. | drafted:general.bc_1025_1_luminous_markings_where_required |
| 1025.2 | Markings within exit components | reference | draft | Where the markings go (interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways, per 1025.2.1 to 1025.2.6.3): quoted by 1025.1's flag. | 1025.2 Markings within exit components. Egress path markings shall be provided in interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways, in accordance with Sections 1025.2.1 through 1025.2.6.3. | drafted:general.bc_1025_1_luminous_markings_where_required |
| 1025.2.1 | Steps | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): a solid, continuous stripe 1 to 2 inches wide runs the full length of each step's leading edge, placed within 1/2 inch of the edge and lapping down its face by at most 1/2 inch (Gordium's reading of the printed double negative, a person confirms) (the 1 inch minimum not applying to stripes listed per UL 1994): markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.1 Steps. A solid and continuous stripe shall be applied to the horizontal leading edge of each step and shall extend for the full length of the step. Outlining stripes shall have a minimum horizontal width of 1 inch (25 mm) and a maximum width of 2 inches (51 mm). The leading edge of the stripe shall be placed not more than 1/2 inch (12.7 mm) from the leading edge of the step and the stripe shall not overlap the leading edge of the step by not more than 1/2 inch (12.7 mm) down the vertical face of the step. Exception: The minimum width of 1 inch (25 mm) shall not apply to outlining stripes listed in accordance with UL 1994. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_1_steps |
| 1025.2.2 | Landings | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): the leading edge of each landing is marked with a stripe meeting the dimensions for steps: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.2 Landings. The leading edge of landings shall be marked with a stripe consistent with the dimensional requirements for steps. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_2_landings |
| 1025.2.3 | Handrails | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): handrails and their extensions carry a solid, continuous stripe at least 1 inch wide on the top surface for their whole length, newel post caps included, with no gap over 4 inches at bends and corners (the 1 inch minimum not applying to stripes listed per UL 1994): markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.3 Handrails. Handrails and handrail extensions shall be marked with a solid and continuous stripe having a minimum width of 1 inch (25 mm). The stripe shall be placed on the top surface of the handrail for the entire length of the handrail, including extensions and newel post caps. Where handrails or handrail extensions bend or turn corners, the stripe shall not have a gap of more than 4 inches (102 mm). Exception: The minimum width of 1 inch (25 mm) shall not apply to outlining stripes listed in accordance with UL 1994. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_3_handrails |
| 1025.2.4 | Perimeter demarcation lines | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): stair landings and other floor areas in interior exit stairways, ramps and exit passageways, but the sides of steps, have solid, continuous demarcation lines on the floor, the walls or both, 1 to 2 inches wide with breaks of at most 4 inches (the 1 inch minimum not applying to stripes listed per UL 1994): markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.4 Perimeter demarcation lines. Stair landings and other floor areas within interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways, with the exception of the sides of steps, shall be provided with solid and continuous demarcation lines on the floor or on the walls or a combination of both. The stripes shall be 1 to 2 inches (25 mm to 51 mm) wide with interruptions not exceeding 4 inches (102 mm). Exception: The minimum width of 1 inch (25 mm) shall not apply to outlining stripes listed in accordance with UL 1994. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_4_perimeter_demarcation_lines |
| 1025.2.4.1 | Floor-mounted demarcation lines | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): floor-mounted demarcation lines are within 4 inches of the wall, reach within 2 inches of the landings' edge markings and continue across the floor in front of every door, but not in front of exit discharge doors that lead out of the exit and that occupants must pass through to complete the exit path: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.4.1 Floor-mounted demarcation lines. Perimeter demarcation lines shall be placed within 4 inches (102 mm) of the wall and shall extend to within 2 inches (51 mm) of the markings on the leading edge of landings. The demarcation lines shall continue across the floor in front of all doors. Exception: Demarcation lines shall not extend in front of exit discharge doors that lead out of an exit and through which occupants must travel to complete the exit path. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_4_1_floor_mounted_lines |
| 1025.2.4.2 | Wall-mounted demarcation lines | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): wall-mounted demarcation lines have their bottom edge at most 4 inches above the finished floor, drop to the floor within 2 inches of the step or landing edge at the top and bottom of stairs, move to the floor where a floor line is the only practical way to outline the path, and continue across a door's face or the floor in front of it, but not in front of exit discharge doors that lead out of the exit and that occupants must pass through to complete the exit path: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.4.2 Wall-mounted demarcation lines. Perimeter demarcation lines shall be placed on the wall with the bottom edge of the stripe not more than 4 inches (102 mm) above the finished floor. At the top or bottom of the stairs, demarcation lines shall drop vertically to the floor within 2 inches (51 mm) of the step or landing edge. Demarcation lines on walls shall transition vertically to the floor and then extend across the floor where a line on the floor is the only practical method of outlining the path. Where the wall line is broken by a door, demarcation lines on walls shall continue across the face of the door or transition to the floor and extend across the floor in front of such door. Exception: Demarcation lines shall not extend in front of exit discharge doors that lead out of an exit and through which occupants must travel to complete the exit path. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_4_2_wall_mounted_lines |
| 1025.2.4.3 | Transition | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): where a wall-mounted demarcation line becomes a floor-mounted one, or the other way round, the wall line drops vertically to the floor to meet the floor line, making one continuous marking: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.4.3 Transition. Where a wall-mounted demarcation line transitions to a floor-mounted demarcation line, or vice versa, the wall-mounted demarcation line shall drop vertically to the floor to meet a complimentary extension of the floor-mounted demarcation line, thus forming a continuous marking. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_4_3_transition |
| 1025.2.5 | Obstacles | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): obstacles at or below 6 feet 6 inches high projecting more than 4 inches into the egress path (standpipes, hose cabinets, wall projections, low headroom) are outlined with markings at least 1 inch wide of alternating equal bands of luminous material and black, at most 2 inches thick, at 45 degrees, concealing no required information (the 1 inch minimum not applying to markings listed per UL 1994): markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.5 Obstacles. Obstacles at or below 6 feet 6 inches (1981 mm) in height and projecting more than 4 inches (102 mm) into the egress path shall be outlined with markings not less than 1 inch (25 mm) in width comprised of a pattern of alternating equal bands, of luminous material and black, with the alternating bands not more than 2 inches (51 mm) thick and angled at 45 degrees (0.79 rad). Obstacles shall include, but are not limited to, standpipes, hose cabinets, wall projections and restricted height areas. However, such markings shall not conceal any required information or indicators including but not limited to instructions to occu- pants for the use of standpipes. Exception: The minimum width of 1 inch (25 mm) shall not apply to markings listed in accordance with UL 1994. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_5_obstacles |
| 1025.2.6 | Doors within the exit path | reference | draft | Which doors carry the markings of 1025.2.6.1 to 1025.2.6.3 (those occupants pass through to complete the exit path): quoted by 1025.2.6.1's, 1025.2.6.2's and 1025.2.6.3's flags. | 1025.2.6 Doors within the exit path. Doors through which occupants must pass in order to complete the exit path shall be provided with markings complying with Sections 1025.2.6.1 through 1025.2.6.3. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_6_1_emergency_exit_symbol, drafted:general.bc_1025_2_6_2_door_hardware_markings, drafted:general.bc_1025_2_6_3_door_frame_markings |
| 1025.2.6.1 | Emergency exit symbol | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): each door occupants pass through to complete the exit path carries markings per 1025.2.6.1 to 1025.2.6.3 (1025.2.6): a low-location luminous emergency exit symbol per NFPA 170, at least 4 inches high, centered on the door with its top at most 18 inches above the floor: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.6.1 Emergency exit symbol. The doors shall be identified by a low-location luminous emergency exit symbol comply- ing with NFPA 170. The exit symbol shall be not less than 4 inches (102 mm) in height and shall be mounted on the door, centered horizontally, with the top of the symbol not higher than 18 inches (457 mm) above the finished floor. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_6_1_emergency_exit_symbol |
| 1025.2.6.2 | Door hardware markings | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): on each door occupants pass through to complete the exit path (1025.2.6), its hardware is marked with at least 16 square inches of luminous material behind, beside or on the handle or escutcheon, and a panic bar with a strip at least 1 inch wide along its whole actuating bar or touchpad: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.6.2 Door hardware markings. Door hardware shall be marked with not less than 16 square inches (10 323 mm[fn2)] of luminous material. This marking shall be located behind, immediately adjacent to, or on the door handle or escutcheon. Where a panic bar is installed, such material shall be not less than 1 inch (25 mm) wide for the entire length of the actuating bar or touchpad. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_6_2_door_hardware_markings |
| 1025.2.6.3 | Door frame markings | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): on each door occupants pass through to complete the exit path (1025.2.6), the top and sides of its frame carry a solid, continuous stripe 1 to 2 inches wide, on the surrounding wall where the molding has too little flat surface: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.2.6.3 Door frame markings. The top and sides of the door frame shall be marked with a solid and continuous 1-inch- to 2-inch-wide (25 mm to 51 mm) stripe. Where the door molding does not provide sufficient flat surface on which to locate the stripe, the stripe shall be permitted to be located on the wall surrounding the frame. | drafted:general.bc_1025_2_6_3_door_frame_markings |
| 1025.3 | Uniformity | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): markings are placed and sized consistently and uniformly throughout the same enclosure: markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.3 Uniformity. Placement and dimensions of markings shall be consistent and uniform throughout the same enclosure. | drafted:general.bc_1025_3_uniformity |
| 1025.4 | Self-luminous and photoluminescent | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): luminous egress path markings may be of any material, paint included, needing no electrical charge to keep their luminance (self-luminous or photoluminescent), complying with UL 1994 or ASTM E2072 (charged by 1 footcandle of fluorescent light for 60 minutes, at least 30 millicandelas per square meter at 10 minutes and 5 after 90 minutes): markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.4 Self-luminous and photoluminescent. Luminous egress path markings shall be permitted to be made of any material, including paint, provided that an electrical charge is not required to maintain the required luminance. Such materials shall include, but not be limited to, self-luminous materials and photoluminescent materials. Materials shall comply with either of the following standards: 1. UL 1994. 2. ASTM E2072, except that the charging source shall be 1 footcandle (11 lux) of fluorescent illumination for 60 minutes, and the minimum luminance shall be 30 milicandelas per square meter at 10 minutes and 5 milicandelas per square meter after 90 minutes. | drafted:general.bc_1025_4_self_luminous_photoluminescent |
| 1025.5 | Illumination | flag | draft | In the interior exit stairways, interior exit ramps and exit passageways of a high-rise building of Group A, B, E, I-1, M or R-1 (1025.1, 1025.2): photoluminescent exit path markings get at least 1 footcandle of light for at least 60 minutes before the building is occupied and all through occupancy: lighting and markings aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1025.5 Illumination. Where photoluminescent exit path markings are installed, they shall be provided with not less than 1 footcan- dle (11 lux) of illumination for not less than 60 minutes prior to periods when the building is occupied and continuously during occupancy. | drafted:general.bc_1025_5_illumination |
| 1026.1 | General | flag | draft | A horizontal exit is never the only exit from a portion of a building, and where two or more exits are required, horizontal exits are at most half of their number and of their total width or capacity; but two-thirds in Group I-2, and all of them in Group I-3 with at least 6 square feet of accessible space per occupant on each side for everyone in the adjoining compartments: which exits are horizontal exits, and their share, aren't worked out, so a person's check. | 1026.1 General. Horizontal exits serving as an exit in a means of egress system shall comply with the requirements of this section. A horizontal exit shall not serve as the only exit from a portion of a building, and where two or more exits are required, not more than one-half of the total number of exits or total exit minimum width or required capacity shall be horizontal exits. Exceptions: 1. Horizontal exits are permitted to comprise two-thirds of the required exits from any building or floor area for occupancies in Group I-2. 2. Horizontal exits are permitted to comprise 100 percent of the exits required for occupancies in Group I-3. Not less than 6 square feet (0.6 m2) of accessible space per occupant shall be provided on each side of the horizontal exit for the total number of people in adjoining compartments. | drafted:general.bc_1026_1_horizontal_exits_general |
| 1026.2 | Separation | flag | draft | The separation between buildings or refuge areas a horizontal exit connects is a fire wall per 706, or fire barriers per 707 or horizontal assemblies per 711, rated at least 2 hours, with opening protectives per 716 and duct and air transfer openings per 717, running through every level unless the floors are rated 2 hours with no unprotected openings, and a fire barrier continuous from exterior wall to exterior wall; but no rating is needed to an above-grade pedestrian walkway per 3104 where the buildings are more than 20 feet apart: horizontal exits aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1026.2 Separation. The separation between buildings or refuge areas connected by a horizontal exit shall be provided by a fire wall complying with Section 706; or by a fire barrier complying with Section 707 or a horizontal assembly complying with Section 711, or both. The minimum fire-resistance rating of the separation shall be 2 hours. Opening protectives in horizontal exits shall also comply with Section 716. Duct and air transfer openings in a fire wall or fire barrier that serves as a horizontal exit shall also comply with Section 717. The horizontal exit separation shall extend vertically through all levels of the building unless floor assemblies have a fire- resistance rating of not less than 2 hours and do not have unprotected openings. Exception: A fire-resistance rating is not required at horizontal exits between a building area and an above-grade pedestrian walk- way constructed in accordance with Section 3104, provided that the distance between connected buildings is more than 20 feet (6096 mm). Horizontal exits constructed as fire barriers shall be continuous from exterior wall to exterior wall so as to divide completely the floor served by the horizontal exit. | drafted:general.bc_1026_2_horizontal_exit_separation |
| 1026.3 | Opening protectives | flag | draft | Fire doors in a horizontal exit are self-closing or close automatically on a smoke detector per 716.2.6.6, and doors in a cross-corridor close automatically on a smoke detector so installed: door closers aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1026.3 Opening protectives. Fire doors in horizontal exits shall be self-closing or automatic-closing when activated by a smoke detector in accordance with Section 716.2.6.6. Doors, where located in a cross-corridor condition, shall be automatic-closing by acti- vation of a smoke detector installed in accordance with Section 716.2.6.6. | drafted:general.bc_1026_3_horizontal_exit_opening_protectives |
| 1026.4 | Refuge area | flag | draft | A horizontal exit's refuge area is occupied by the same tenant or is a public area, large enough for its own occupant load plus the load expected from the adjoining compartment (the lesser of what the horizontal exit doors into it can carry and that compartment's whole load): horizontal exits aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1026.4 Refuge area. The refuge area of a horizontal exit shall be a space occupied by the same tenant or a public area and each such refuge area shall be adequate to accommodate the original occupant load of the refuge area plus the occupant load anticipated from the adjoining compartment. The anticipated occupant load from the adjoining compartment shall be based on the capacity of the horizontal exit doors entering the refuge area or the total occupant load of the adjoining compartment, whichever is less. | drafted:general.bc_1026_4_refuge_area |
| 1026.4.1 | Capacity | flag | draft | A refuge area's capacity is worked out at 3 square feet of net floor area per occupant it must hold; where the horizontal exit also forms a smoke compartment, Groups I-1, I-2 and I-3 and ambulatory care facilities meet 407.5.3, 408.6.2, 420.6.1 and 422.3.2 as they apply: refuge areas aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1026.4.1 Capacity. The capacity of the refuge area shall be computed based on a net floor area allowance of 3 square feet (0.2787 m[fn2]) for each occupant to be accommodated therein. Where the horizontal exit also forms a smoke compartment, the capacity of the refuge area for Group I-1, I-2 and I-3 occupancies and ambulatory care facilities shall comply with Sections 407.5.3, 408.6.2, 420.6.1 and 422.3.2 as applicable. | drafted:general.bc_1026_4_1_refuge_area_capacity |
| 1026.4.2 | Number of exits | flag | draft | The refuge area a horizontal exit leads into has exits enough for its own occupants (not counting those entering through horizontal exits), at least one leading directly outside or to an interior exit stairway or ramp; but the adjoining compartment needs no stairway or door directly outside where the refuge area has them and no one must go back through the compartment they left: horizontal exits aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1026.4.2 Number of exits. The refuge area into which a horizontal exit leads shall be provided with exits adequate to meet the occupant requirements of this chapter, but not including the added occupant load imposed by persons entering the refuge area through horizontal exits from other areas. Not less than one refuge area exit shall lead directly to the exterior or to an interior exit stairway or ramp. | drafted:general.bc_1026_4_2_refuge_area_exits |
| 1026.5 | Standpipes | flag | draft | Standpipes and standpipe hose connections are provided where 905.3 and 905.4 require them: standpipes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1026.5 Standpipes. Standpipes and standpipe hose connections shall be provided where required by Sections 905.3 and 905.4. | drafted:general.bc_1026_5_horizontal_exit_standpipes |
| 1027.1 | General | reference | draft | Scope: exterior exit stairways and ramps serving as an exit component comply with 1027; quoted by 1027.2's flag. | 1027.1 General. Exterior exit stairways and ramps serving as an exit component in a means of egress system shall comply with the requirements of this section. | drafted:general.bc_1027_2_exterior_exit_stairways_use |
| 1027.2 | Use in a means of egress | flag | draft | Exterior exit stairways and ramps serving as an exit component meet 1027 (1027.1); an exterior exit stairway is not an element of a required means of egress in Group I-2, and outside Group I-2 no exterior exit stairway or ramp is one in a building more than six stories above grade plane or a high-rise building: which stairways are exterior exit stairways, and whether the building is a high-rise, aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1027.2 Use in a means of egress. Exterior exit stairways shall not be used as an element of a required means of egress for Group I-2 occupancies. For occupancies in other than Group I-2, exterior exit stairways and ramps shall not be used as an element of a required means of egress for buildings exceeding six stories above grade plane or that are high-rise buildings. | drafted:general.bc_1027_2_exterior_exit_stairways_use |
| 1027.3 | Open side | flag | draft | An exterior exit stairway or ramp serving as an element of a required means of egress is open on at least one side but for required structural columns, beams, handrails and guards, the open side having at least 35 square feet of open area at each floor level and intermediate landing, at least 42 inches above that floor or landing: exterior stairways aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1027.3 Open side. Exterior exit stairways and ramps serving as an element of a required means of egress shall be open on not less than one side, except for required structural columns, beams, handrails and guards. An open side shall have not less than 35 square feet (3.3 m[fn2]) of aggregate open area adjacent to each floor level and the level of each intermediate landing. The required open area shall be located not less than 42 inches (1067 mm) above the adjacent floor or landing level. | drafted:general.bc_1027_3_open_side |
| 1027.4 | Side yards | flag | draft | The open areas beside an exterior exit stairway or ramp are yards, courts or public ways; its other sides may be enclosed by the building's exterior walls: exterior stairways and what they open onto aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1027.4 Side yards. The open areas adjoining exterior exit stairways or ramps shall be either yards, courts or public ways; the remain- ing sides are permitted to be enclosed by the exterior walls of the building. | drafted:general.bc_1027_4_side_yards |
| 1027.5 | Location | flag | draft | An exterior exit stairway or ramp, landings included, has a fire separation distance of at least 10 feet, measured at right angles from its exterior edge, to adjacent lot lines, to other portions of the building (treated as separate buildings) and to other buildings on the same lot unless their exterior walls and openings are protected per 705; but at least 5 feet for one serving individual dwelling units of Group R-3: exterior stairways and their distances aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1027.5 Location. Exterior exit stairways and ramps shall have a minimum fire separation distance of 10 feet (3048 mm) measured at right angles from the exterior edge of the stairway or ramps, including landings, to: 1. Adjacent lot lines. 2. Other portions of the building. 3. Other buildings on the same lot unless the adjacent building exterior walls and openings are protected in accordance with Section 705 based on fire separation distance. For the purposes of this section, other portions of the building shall be treated as separate buildings. Exception: Exterior exit stairways and ramps serving individual dwelling units of Group R-3 shall have a minimum fire separation distance of 5 feet (1525 mm). | drafted:general.bc_1027_5_location |
| 1027.6 | Exterior exit stairway and ramp protection | flag | draft | An exterior exit stairway or ramp is separated from the interior as 1023.2 requires, with openings only those needed for egress from normally occupied spaces, and the exterior wall rated per 1023.7 where other parts of the building expose the plane of its edge at an angle under 180 degrees; but no separation is needed for occupancies other than Group R-1 or R-2 in a building of at most two stories above grade plane whose level of exit discharge is the first story above grade plane; nor where it is served by an exterior ramp or balcony connecting two remote exterior exit stairways or other approved exits, its perimeter at least 50 percent open (openings at least half the enclosing wall's height, their tops at least 7 feet above the balcony); nor from an open-ended corridor where the building is sprinklered throughout per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1), the corridors meet 1020 and connect at each end to an exterior exit stairway or ramp meeting 1027, the walls and openings beside it meet 1023.7, and each change of direction over 45 degrees has a clear opening of at least 35 square feet or an exterior stairway or ramp; nor in Group R-3 of at most four stories where it serves individual dwelling units and discharges directly to grade: exterior stairways, their separation and walls aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1027.6 Exterior exit stairway and ramp protection. Exterior exit stairways and ramps shall be separated from the interior of the building as required in Section 1023.2. Openings shall be limited to those necessary for egress from normally occupied spaces. Where a vertical plane projecting from the edge of an exterior exit stairway or ramp and landings is exposed by other parts of the building at an angle of less than 180 degrees (3.14 rad), the exterior wall shall be rated in accordance with Section 1023.7. Exceptions: 1. Separation from the interior of the building is not required for occupancies, other than those in Group R-1 or R-2, in build- ings that are not more than two stories above grade plane where a level of exit discharge serving such occupancies is the first story above grade plane. 2. Separation from the interior of the building is not required where the exterior exit stairway or ramp is served by an exte- rior exit ramp or balcony that connects two remote exterior exit stairways or other approved exits with a perimeter that is not less than 50 percent open. To be considered open, the opening shall be not less than 50 percent of the height of the enclosing wall, with the top of the openings not less than 7 feet (2134 mm) above the top of the balcony. 3. Separation from the open-ended corridor of the building is not required for exterior exit stairways or ramps, provided that Items 3.1 through 3.5 are met: 3.1. The building, including open-ended corridors, and stairways and ramps, shall be equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2. 3.2. The open-ended corridors comply with Section 1020. 3.3. The open-ended corridors are connected on each end to an exterior exit stairway or ramp complying with Section 1027. 3.4. The exterior walls and openings adjacent to the exterior exit stairway or ramp comply with Section 1023.7. 3.5. At any location in an open-ended corridor where a change of direction exceeding 45 degrees (0.79 rad) occurs, a clear opening of not less than 35 square feet (3.3 m[fn2]) or an exterior stairway or ramp shall be provided. Where clear openings are provided, they shall be located so as to minimize the accumulation of smoke or toxic gases. 4. In Group R-3 occupancies not more than four stories in height, exterior exit stairways and ramps serving individual dwell- ing units are not required to be separated from the interior of the building where the exterior exit stairway or ramp discharges directly to grade. | drafted:general.bc_1027_6_protection |
| 1028.1 | General | reference | draft | Scope: the exit discharge complies with 1028 and 1029 and 1003 to 1015 as they apply; quoted by 1028.2's flag. | 1028.1 General. The exit discharge shall comply with Sections 1028 and 1029 and the applicable requirements of Sections 1003 through 1015. | drafted:general.bc_1028_2_exit_discharge |
| 1028.2 | Exit discharge | flag | draft | The exit discharge meets 1028 and 1029 and 1003 to 1015 as they apply (1028.1). Exits discharge directly to the exterior, at grade or with a direct path of egress travel to grade, never reentering a building; but at most 50 percent of the number and the minimum width or required capacity of interior exit stairways and ramps may egress through areas on the level of exit discharge (a free and unobstructed path to a readily visible exterior exit door, the whole level separated from areas below as the enclosure is, the egress path sprinklered and the portions with access to it sprinklered per 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2 (a 13R system counts only in Group R, within 903.3.1.2's four stories, and in Group I-1 Condition 1, 903.2.6 exception 1) or separated from it, and an exit access stairway or ramp ending at least 30 feet, or a quarter of the building's greatest diagonal if less, from the exit stairway's discharge door), or through a vestibule (separated from areas below as the enclosure is, at most 10 feet deep and 30 feet long, separated from the rest of the level by a fire partition per 708, used only for egress and exiting directly outside), the two together at most 50 percent of the number and of the minimum width or required capacity of the required exits; and horizontal exits per 1026 need not discharge directly outside: where each exit discharges isn't worked out, so a person's check. | 1028.2 Exit discharge. Exits shall discharge directly to the exterior of the building. The exit discharge shall be at grade or shall provide a direct path of egress travel to grade. The exit discharge shall not reenter a building. The combined use of Exceptions 1 and 2 shall not exceed 50 percent of the number and minimum width or required capacity of the required exits. Exceptions: 1. Not more than 50 percent of the number and minimum width or required capacity of interior exit stairways and ramps is permitted to egress through areas, including atriums, on the level of discharge provided that all of the following condi- tions are met: 1.1. Discharge of interior exit stairways and ramps shall be provided with a free and unobstructed path of travel to an exterior exit door and such exit is readily visible and identifiable from the point of termination of the enclosure. 1.2. The entire area of the level of exit discharge is separated from areas below by construction conforming to the fire-resistance rating for the enclosure. 1.3. The egress path from the interior exit stairway and ramp on the level of exit discharge is protected throughout by an approved automatic sprinkler system. Portions of the level of exit discharge with access to the egress path shall be either equipped throughout with an automatic sprinkler system installed in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1 or 903.3.1.2, or separated from the egress path in accordance with the requirements for the enclosure of interior exit stairways or ramps. 1.4. Where a required interior exit stairway or ramp and an exit access stairway or ramp serve the same floor level and terminate at the same level of exit discharge, the termination of the exit access stairway or ramp and the exit discharge door of the interior exit stairway or ramp shall be separated by a distance of not less than 30 feet (9144 mm) or not less than one-fourth the length of the maximum overall diagonal dimension of the building, which- ever is less. The distance shall be measured in a straight line between the exit discharge door from the interior exit stairway or ramp and the last tread of the exit access stairway or termination of slope of the exit access ramp. 2. Not more than 50 percent of the number and minimum width or required capacity of the interior exit stairways and ramps is permitted to egress through a vestibule provided that all of the following conditions are met: 2.1. The entire area of the vestibule is separated from areas below by construction conforming to the fire-resistance rating of the interior exit stairway or ramp enclosure. 2.2. The depth from the exterior of the building is not greater than 10 feet (3048 mm) and the length is not greater than 30 feet (9144 mm). 2.3. The area is separated from the remainder of the level of exit discharge by a fire partition constructed in accor- dance with Section 708. Exception: The maximum transmitted temperature rise is not required. 2.4. The area is used only for means of egress and exits directly to the outside. 3. Horizontal exits complying with Section 1026 shall not be required to discharge directly to the exterior of the building. | drafted:general.bc_1028_2_exit_discharge |
| 1028.3 | Exit discharge width or capacity | flag | draft | The exit discharge's minimum width or required capacity is at least that of the exits it serves: the exit discharge outside the building isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1028.3 Exit discharge width or capacity. The minimum width or required capacity of the exit discharge shall be not less than the minimum width or required capacity of the exits being served. | drafted:general.bc_1028_3_exit_discharge_width |
| 1028.4 | Exit discharge components | flag | draft | Exit discharge components are open enough to the exterior to keep smoke and toxic gases from building up: the exit discharge isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1028.4 Exit discharge components. Exit discharge components shall be sufficiently open to the exterior so as to minimize the accu- mulation of smoke and toxic gases. | drafted:general.bc_1028_4_exit_discharge_components |
| 1028.5 | Access to a public way | flag | draft | The exit discharge gives direct and unobstructed access to a public way; where that can't be provided, a safe dispersal area of at least 5 square feet per person, on the same lot at least 50 feet from the building, permanently kept and identified as one, with a safe and unobstructed path from the building: the site outside the building isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1028.5 Access to a public way. The exit discharge shall provide a direct and unobstructed access to a public way. Exception: Where access to a public way cannot be provided, a safe dispersal area shall be provided where all of the following are met: 1. The area shall be of a size to accommodate not less than 5 square feet (0.46 m[fn2]) for each person. 2. The area shall be located on the same lot not less than 50 feet (15 240 mm) away from the building requiring egress. 3. The area shall be permanently maintained and identified as a safe dispersal area. 4. The area shall be provided with a safe and unobstructed path of travel from the building. | drafted:general.bc_1028_5_access_to_public_way |
| 1029.1 | General | reference | draft | Scope: egress courts serving as an exit discharge component comply with 1029; quoted by 1029.2's flag. | 1029.1 General. Egress courts serving as an exit discharge component in the means of egress system shall comply with the require- ments in this section. | drafted:general.bc_1029_2_egress_court_width |
| 1029.2 | Width or capacity | flag | draft | An egress court serving as an exit discharge component meets 1029 (1029.1): its capacity is as 1005.1 requires and its width at least 44 inches, or 36 inches serving Groups R-3 and U, and never less than the required capacity, the width and capacity unobstructed to a height of 7 feet but for encroachments complying with 1005.7: egress courts aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1029.2 Width or capacity. The required capacity of egress courts shall be determined as specified in Section 1005.1, but the mini- mum width shall be not less than 44 inches (1118 mm), except as specified herein. Egress courts serving Group R-3 and U occupancies shall be not less than 36 inches (914 mm) in width. The required capacity and width of egress courts shall be unobstructed to a height of 7 feet (2134 mm). The width of the egress court shall be not less than the required capacity. Exception: Encroachments complying with Section 1005.7. | drafted:general.bc_1029_2_egress_court_width |
| 1029.3 | Construction and openings | flag | draft | Where an egress court is less than 10 feet wide, its walls are rated at least 1 hour for 10 feet above its floor, openings in them protected at least 3/4 hour; but not for an egress court serving fewer than 10 occupants, one serving Group R-3, or one at grade giving direct and unobstructed access to a public way by two or more independent paths, each keeping the required width or capacity, whichever is greater: egress courts and their walls aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1029.3 Construction and openings. Where an egress court serving a building or portion thereof is less than 10 feet (3048 mm) in width, the egress court walls shall have not less than 1-hour fire-resistance-rated construction for a distance of 10 feet (3048 mm) | drafted:general.bc_1029_3_egress_court_construction |
| 1030.1 | General | reference | draft | Scope: a room or space used for assembly purposes that contains seats, tables, displays, equipment or other material complies with 1030; quoted by 1030.9's flag. | 1030.1 General. A room or space used for assembly purposes that contains seats, tables, displays, equipment or other material shall comply with this section. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_assembly_aisles_required |
| 1030.1.1 | Bleachers | flag | draft | Bleachers, grandstands and folding and telescopic seating that are not building elements comply with ICC 300: such seating isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.1.1 Bleachers. Bleachers, grandstands and folding and telescopic seating, that are not building elements, shall comply with ICC 300. | drafted:general.bc_1030_1_1_bleachers |
| 1030.1.1.1 | Spaces under grandstands and bleachers | flag | draft | Spaces under grandstands or bleachers are separated by fire barriers per 707 and horizontal assemblies per 711 of at least 1-hour fire-resistance-rated construction; but not ticket booths under 100 square feet, toilet rooms, or other accessory use areas of 1,000 square feet or less sprinklered per 903.3.1.1: grandstands, bleachers and the spaces under them aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.1.1.1 Spaces under grandstands and bleachers. Spaces under grandstands or bleachers shall be separated by fire barriers complying with Section 707 and horizontal assemblies complying with Section 711 with not less than 1-hour fire-resis- tance-rated construction. Exceptions: 1. Ticket booths less than 100 square feet (9.29 m[fn2]) in area. 2. Toilet rooms. 3. Other accessory use areas 1,000 square feet (92.9 m[fn2]) or less in area and equipped with an automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. | drafted:general.bc_1030_1_1_1_spaces_under_grandstands |
| 1030.2 | Assembly main exit | flag | draft | A building, room or space used for assembly purposes with an occupant load over 300 and a main exit: the main exit has capacity for at least half the occupant load, and at least the total required capacity of all the means of egress leading to it; where the building is classified Group A, the main exit fronts at least one street, or an unoccupied space at least 10 feet wide adjoining a street or public way; where there is no well-defined main exit, or there are several, the exits may be distributed around the building's perimeter if their total capacity is at least 100 percent of the required capacity: which exit is the main exit, and its capacity, aren't worked out, so a person's check. | 1030.2 Assembly main exit. A building, room or space used for assembly purposes that has an occupant load of greater than 300 and is provided with a main exit, that main exit shall be of sufficient capacity to accommodate not less than one-half of the occupant load, but such capacity shall be not less than the total required capacity of all means of egress leading to the exit. Where the building is classified as a Group A occupancy, the main exit shall front on not less than one street or an unoccupied space of not less than 10 feet (3048 mm) in width that adjoins a street or public way. In a building, room or space used for assembly purposes where there is not a well-defined main exit or where multiple main exits are provided, exits shall be permitted to be distributed around the perime- ter of the building provided that the total capacity of egress is not less than 100 percent of the required capacity. | drafted:general.bc_1030_2_assembly_main_exit |
| 1030.3 | Assembly other exits | flag | draft | Each level of a building used for assembly purposes with an occupant load over 300 and a main exit has, besides access to the main exit, more means of egress with capacity for at least half the total occupant load that level serves, complying with 1007.1; where there is no well-defined main exit, or there are several, each level's exits may be distributed around the building's perimeter if their total width is at least 100 percent of the required width: the main exit and the other exits' share aren't worked out, so a person's check. | 1030.3 Assembly other exits. In addition to having access to a main exit, each level in a building used for assembly purposes having an occupant load greater than 300 and provided with a main exit, shall be provided with additional means of egress that shall provide an egress capacity for not less than one-half of the total occupant load served by that level and shall comply with Section 1007.1. In a building used for assembly purposes where there is not a well-defined main exit or where multiple main exits are provided, exits for each level shall be permitted to be distributed around the perimeter of the building, provided that the total width of egress is not less than 100 percent of the required width. | drafted:general.bc_1030_3_assembly_other_exits |
| 1030.4 | Foyers and lobbies | flag | draft | In Group A-1, where people are let in while no seats are free, they may wait in a lobby or similar space that doesn't encroach on the minimum width or required capacity of the means of egress; such a foyer, unless every main entrance or exit connects it directly to a public street, has a straight and unobstructed corridor or path of travel to each of them: waiting spaces aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.4 Foyers and lobbies. In Group A-1 occupancies, where persons are admitted to the building at times when seats are not avail- able, such persons shall be allowed to wait in a lobby or similar space, provided that such lobby or similar space shall not encroach on the minimum width or required capacity of the means of egress. Such foyer, if not directly connected to a public street by all the main entrances or exits, shall have a straight and unobstructed corridor or path of travel to every such main entrance or exit. | drafted:general.bc_1030_4_foyers_and_lobbies |
| 1030.5 | Interior balcony and gallery means of egress | flag | draft | A balcony, gallery or press box seating 50 or more in a building, room or space used for assembly purposes has at least two means of egress, one from each side: balconies, galleries and press boxes aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.5 Interior balcony and gallery means of egress. For balconies, galleries or press boxes having a seating capacity of 50 or more located in a building, room or space used for assembly purposes, not less than two means of egress shall be provided, with one from each side of every balcony, gallery or press box. | drafted:general.bc_1030_5_balcony_and_gallery_egress |
| 1030.6 | Capacity of aisle for assembly | reference | draft | Which section sets an assembly aisle's capacity: 1030.6.1 without smoke protection, 1030.6.2 for smoke-protected seating, 1030.6.3 for open-air seating; quoted by 1030.6.1's flag. | 1030.6 Capacity of aisle for assembly. The required capacity of aisles shall be not less than that determined in accordance with Section 1030.6.1 where smoke-protected assembly seating is not provided, Section 1030.6.2 where smoke-protected assembly seating is provided and Section 1030.6.3 where open-air assembly seating is provided. | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_1_aisle_capacity_without_smoke_protection |
| 1030.6.1 | Without smoke protection | flag | draft | Aisles serving assembly seating have at least the capacity 1030.6.1 sets where the seating isn't smoke-protected (1030.6.2 sets it where it is, 1030.6.3 where it is open-air): at least 0.3 inch per occupant served on stepped aisles with risers of 7 inches or less and treads of 11 inches or more; at least 0.005 inch more per occupant for each 0.10 inch of riser above 7 inches; at least 0.075 inch more per occupant on the parts of a stepped aisle descent with no handrail within 30 inches horizontally; at least 0.22 inch per occupant on ramped aisles steeper than 1 in 12, and 0.20 inch on level aisles and ramped aisles not that steep: aisles and the occupants they serve aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.6.1 Without smoke protection. The required capacity in inches (mm) of the aisles for assembly seating without smoke protection shall be not less than the occupant load served by the egress element in accordance with all of the following, as applicable: 1. Not less than 0.3 inch (7.6 mm) of aisle capacity for each occupant served shall be provided on stepped aisles having riser heights 7 inches (178 mm) or less and tread depths 11 inches (279 mm) or greater, measured horizontally between tread nosings. 2. Not less than 0.005 inch (0.127 mm) of additional aisle capacity for each occupant shall be provided for each 0.10 inch (2.5 mm) of riser height above 7 inches (178 mm). 3. Where egress requires stepped aisle descent, not less than 0.075 inch (1.9 mm) of additional aisle capacity for each occu- pant shall be provided on those portions of aisle capacity that do not have a handrail within a horizontal distance of 30 inches (762 mm). 4. Ramped aisles, where slopes are steeper than one unit vertical in 12 units horizontal (8-percent slope), shall have not less than 0.22 inch (5.6 mm) of clear aisle capacity for each occupant served. Level or ramped aisles, where slopes are not | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_1_aisle_capacity_without_smoke_protection |
| 1030.6.2 | Smoke-protected assembly seating | flag | draft | An aisle for smoke-protected assembly seating has capacity of at least the occupant load it serves times Table 1030.6.2's factor for the total number of seats exposed to the same smoke-protected environment (interpolation between its values permitted), and a facility using the table's reduced widths has a life safety evaluation per NFPA 101: whether seating is smoke-protected, and its aisles, aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.6.2 Smoke-protected assembly seating. The required capacity in inches (mm) of the aisle for smoke-protected assembly seating shall be not less than the occupant load served by the egress element multiplied by the appropriate factor in Table 1030.6.2. The total number of seats specified shall be those within the space exposed to the same smoke-protected environment. Interpolation is permitted between the specific values shown. A life safety evaluation, complying with NFPA 101, shall be done for a facility utilizing the reduced width requirements of Table 1030.6.2 for smoke-protected assembly seating. | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_2_smoke_protected_aisle_capacity |
| Table 1030.6.2 | CAPACITY FOR AISLES FOR SMOKE-PROTECTED ASSEMBLY | flag | draft | Inches of aisle capacity per seat served in smoke-protected assembly seating, by the total number of seats in it: at 5,000 or fewer, 0.200 on stepped aisles with handrails within 30 inches, 0.250 on stepped aisles without, 0.150 on level aisles or ramped aisles not steeper than 1 in 10, and 0.165 on ramped aisles steeper than 1 in 10; at 10,000, 0.130, 0.163, 0.100 and 0.110; at 15,000, 0.096, 0.120, 0.070 and 0.077; at 20,000, 0.076, 0.095, 0.056 and 0.062; at 25,000 or more, 0.060, 0.075, 0.044 and 0.048: aisles and their seats aren't in the model, so a person's check. | TABLE 1030.6.2—CAPACITY FOR AISLES FOR SMOKE-PROTECTED ASSEMBLY TOTAL NUMBER INCHES OF CAPACITY PER SEAT SERVED OF SEATS IN THE SMOKE- Stepped aisles with Stepped aisles without Level aisles or ramped PROTECTED ASSEMBLY handrails within handrails within aisles not steeper than Ramped aisles steeper SEATING 30 inches 30 inches 1 in 10 in slope than 1 in 10 in slope Equal to or less than 5,000 0.200 0.250 0.150 0.165 10,000 0.130 0.163 0.100 0.110 15,000 0.096 0.120 0.070 0.077 20,000 0.076 0.095 0.056 0.062 Equal to or greater than 0.060 0.075 0.044 0.048 25,000 For SI: 1 inch = 25.4 mm. | drafted:general.bc_table_1030_6_2_smoke_protected_aisle_factors |
| 1030.6.2.1 | Smoke control | flag | draft | Aisles and aisle accessways serving smoke-protected assembly seating have a smoke control system per 909, or natural ventilation designed to keep the smoke level at least 6 feet above the floor of the means of egress: smoke control isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.6.2.1 Smoke control. Aisles and aisle accessways serving a smoke-protected assembly seating area shall be provided with a smoke control system complying with Section 909 or natural ventilation designed to maintain the smoke level not less than 6 feet (1829 mm) above the floor of the means of egress. | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_2_1_smoke_control |
| 1030.6.2.2 | Roof height | flag | draft | Where smoke-protected assembly seating has a roof, the lowest portion of the roof deck is at least 15 feet above the highest aisle or aisle accessway; but an outdoor stadium's roof canopy may be lower where nothing is less than 80 inches above the highest aisle or aisle accessway: roofs over seating aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.6.2.2 Roof height. A smoke-protected assembly seating area with a roof shall have the lowest portion of the roof deck not less than 15 feet (4572 mm) above the highest aisle or aisle accessway. Exception: A roof canopy in an outdoor stadium shall be permitted to be less than 15 feet (4572 mm) above the highest aisle or aisle accessway provided that there are no objects less than 80 inches (2032 mm) above the highest aisle or aisle accessway. | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_2_2_roof_height |
| 1030.6.2.3 | Automatic sprinklers | flag | draft | Enclosed areas with walls and ceilings in buildings or structures with smoke-protected assembly seating are protected by an approved automatic sprinkler system per 903.3.1.1; but not the floor area used for contests, performances or entertainment where the roof construction is more than 50 feet above the floor and the use is restricted to low fire hazard uses, nor press boxes and storage facilities under 1,000 square feet: whether seating is smoke-protected isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.6.2.3 Automatic sprinklers. Enclosed areas with walls and ceilings in buildings or structures containing smoke-protected assembly seating shall be protected with an approved automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. Exceptions: 1. The floor area used for contests, performances or entertainment provided that the roof construction is more than 50 feet (15 240 mm) above the floor level and the use is restricted to low fire hazard uses. 2. Press boxes and storage facilities less than 1,000 square feet (93 m[fn2]) in area. | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_2_3_smoke_protected_sprinklers |
| 1030.6.3 | Open-air assembly seating | flag | draft | In open-air assembly seating, an aisle has capacity of at least the total occupant load it serves times 0.08 inch where egress is by stepped aisle, and 0.06 inch where it is by level or ramped aisles; or 1030.6.2's capacity for the number of seats where that is less: whether seating is open-air, and its aisles, aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.6.3 Open-air assembly seating. In open-air assembly seating, the required capacity in inches (mm) of aisles shall be not less than the total occupant load served by the egress element multiplied by 0.08 (2.0 mm) where egress is by stepped aisle and multiplied by 0.06 (1.52 mm) where egress is by level aisles and ramped aisles. Exception: The required capacity in inches (mm) of aisles shall be permitted to comply with Section 1030.6.2 for the number of seats in the open-air assembly seating where Section 1030.6.2 permits less capacity. | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_3_open_air_aisle_capacity |
