| 1030.6.3.1 | Automatic sprinklers | flag | draft | Enclosed areas with walls and ceilings in buildings or structures with open-air assembly seating are protected by an approved automatic sprinkler system per 903.3.1.1; but not the floor area used for contests, performances or entertainment where the roof construction is more than 50 feet above the floor and the use is restricted to low fire hazard uses, press boxes and storage facilities under 1,000 square feet, or open-air seating facilities whose seating and means of egress in the seating area are essentially open to the outside: whether seating is open-air isn't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.6.3.1 Automatic sprinklers. Enclosed areas with walls and ceilings in buildings or structures containing open-air assem- bly seating shall be protected with an approved automatic sprinkler system in accordance with Section 903.3.1.1. Exceptions: 1. The floor area used for contests, performances or entertainment, provided that the roof construction is more than 50 feet (15 240 mm) above the floor level and the use is restricted to low fire hazard uses. 2. Press boxes and storage facilities less than 1,000 square feet (93 m[fn2]) in area. 3. Open-air assembly seating facilities where seating and the means of egress in the seating area are essentially open to the outside. | drafted:general.bc_1030_6_3_1_open_air_sprinklers |
| 1030.7 | Travel distance | reference | draft | How exit access travel is measured where aisles serve seating: along the aisles and aisle accessways, never over or on the seats; exceptions for smoke-protected and open-air seating allow more. Every travel row needs review while fixed seating is stated or not ruled out (assembly_fixed_seating), since the routes don't see the seats. | 1030.7 Travel distance. The exit access travel distance shall comply with Section 1017. Where aisles are provided for seating, the distance shall be measured along the aisles and aisle accessways without travel over or on the seats. Exceptions: | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_2_6_r3_r4_nfpa_13d, drafted:general.bc_1017_2_travel_distance, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_a_e_f1_m_r_s1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_f2_s2_u, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h1, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h2, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h4, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1017_2_i2_i3 |
| 1030.8 | Common path of egress travel | flag | draft | Fixed seating in any group (an E school's auditorium, a B lecture hall): the common path from any seat (30 ft; 75 ft under 50 occupants; 50 ft smoke-protected or open-air) is a person's check. | 1030.8 Common path of egress travel. The common path of egress travel for a room or space used for assembly purposes having fixed seating shall not exceed 30 feet (9144 mm) from any seat to a point where an occupant has a choice of two paths of egress travel to two exits. | drafted:general.bc_1006_2_1_two_exits, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_a_e_m, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_b, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_f, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h1_h2_h3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_h4_h5, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i1_i2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_i3, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r1, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r2, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_r3_r4, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_s, drafted:general.bc_table_1006_2_1_u, drafted:general.bc_1030_8_fixed_seating_common_path |
| 1030.8.1 | Path through adjacent row | flag | draft | Where one of the two paths of travel crosses the aisle through a row of seats to another aisle, there are at most 24 seats between the two aisles, and the row between them is at least 12 inches clear plus 0.6 inch for each seat above seven; for smoke-protected or open-air seating, at most 40 seats, and 12 inches plus 0.3 inch for each additional seat: seats and rows aren't drawn, so a person's check. | 1030.8.1 Path through adjacent row. Where one of the two paths of travel is across the aisle through a row of seats to another aisle, there shall be not more than 24 seats between the two aisles, and the minimum clear width between rows for the row between the two aisles shall be 12 inches (305 mm) plus 0.6 inch (15.2 mm) for each additional seat above seven in the row between aisles. Exception: For smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating there shall be not more than 40 seats between the two aisles and the minimum clear width shall be 12 inches (305 mm) plus 0.3 inch (7.6 mm) for each additional seat. | drafted:general.bc_1030_8_1_path_through_adjacent_row |
| 1030.9 | Assembly aisles are required | flag | draft | Every occupied portion of a building, room or space used for assembly purposes that contains seats, tables, displays, similar fixtures or equipment has aisles leading to exits or exit access doorways as 1030 requires (1030.1: a room or space used for assembly purposes containing seats, tables, displays, equipment or other material complies with 1030): aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9 Assembly aisles are required. Every occupied portion of any building, room or space used for assembly purposes that contains seats, tables, displays, similar fixtures or equipment shall be provided with aisles leading to exits or exit access doorways in accordance with this section. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_assembly_aisles_required |
| 1030.9.1 | Minimum aisle width | flag | draft | An aisle's clear width is at least: 48 inches for stepped aisles with seating on both sides, or 36 inches where they serve fewer than 50 seats; 36 inches for stepped aisles with seating on one side only, or 23 inches between a stepped aisle handrail and the seating where the aisle serves no more than five rows on one side; 23 inches between a stepped aisle handrail or guard and the seating where a mid-aisle handrail divides the stepped aisle; 42 inches for level or ramped aisles with seating on both sides, or 36 inches where they serve fewer than 50 seats, or 30 inches where they serve fewer than 15 seats and aren't part of an accessible route; 36 inches for level or ramped aisles with seating on one side only, or 30 inches where they serve fewer than 15 seats and aren't part of an accessible route: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.1 Minimum aisle width. The minimum clear width for aisles shall comply with one of the following: 1. Forty-eight inches (1219 mm) for stepped aisles having seating on both sides. Exception: Thirty-six inches (914 mm) where the stepped aisles serve less than 50 seats. 2. Thirty-six inches (914 mm) for stepped aisles having seating on only one side. Exception: Twenty-three inches (584 mm) between a stepped aisle handrail and seating where a stepped aisle does not serve more than five rows on one side. 3. Twenty-three inches (584 mm) between a stepped aisle handrail or guard and seating where the stepped aisle is subdi- vided by a mid-aisle handrail. 4. Forty-two inches (1067 mm) for level or ramped aisles having seating on both sides. Exceptions: 1. Thirty-six inches (914 mm) where the aisle serves less than 50 seats. 2. Thirty inches (762 mm) where the aisle serves less than 15 seats and does not serve as part of an accessible route. 5. Thirty-six inches (914 mm) for level or ramped aisles having seating on only one side. Exception: Thirty inches (762 mm) where the aisle serves fewer than 15 seats and does not serve as part of an accessible route. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_1_minimum_aisle_width |
| 1030.9.2 | Aisle catchment area | flag | draft | An aisle has capacity enough for the people in the catchment area it serves, the portion of the whole space that section of the aisle serves, worked out assuming a balanced use of all the means of egress, the number of people in proportion to egress capacity: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.2 Aisle catchment area. The aisle shall provide sufficient capacity for the number of persons accommodated by the catchment area served by the aisle. The catchment area served by an aisle is that portion of the total space served by that section of the aisle. In establishing catchment areas, the assumption shall be made that there is a balanced use of all means of egress, with the number of persons in proportion to egress capacity. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_2_aisle_catchment_area |
| 1030.9.3 | Converging aisles | flag | draft | Where aisles converge into a single path of egress travel, that path's required capacity is at least the combined required capacity of the converging aisles: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.3 Converging aisles. Where aisles converge to form a single path of egress travel, the required capacity of that path shall be not less than the combined required capacity of the converging aisles. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_3_converging_aisles |
| 1030.9.4 | Uniform width and capacity | flag | draft | The portions of aisles where egress is possible in either of two directions are uniform in minimum width or required capacity: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.4 Uniform width and capacity. Those portions of aisles, where egress is possible in either of two directions, shall be uniform in minimum width or required capacity. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_4_uniform_width_and_capacity |
| 1030.9.5 | Dead-end aisles | flag | draft | Each end of an aisle continues to a cross aisle, foyer, doorway, vomitory, concourse or stairway per 1030.9.7 with access to an exit; but a dead-end aisle may be up to 20 feet long; longer where the seats beyond its first 20 feet are at most 24 seats from another aisle, along a row at least 12 inches clear plus 0.6 inch for each seat above seven where the seats have backrests, or beyond 10 where they don't; one serving fewer than 50 seats and complying with 1030.8; and for smoke-protected or open-air seating, dead-end vertical aisles of 21 rows or fewer, or longer where the rows beyond are at most 40 seats from another aisle, along an aisle accessway at least 12 inches clear plus 0.3 inch for each seat above seven where the seats have backrests, or beyond 10 where they don't: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.5 Dead-end aisles. Each end of an aisle shall be continuous to a cross aisle, foyer, doorway, vomitory, concourse or stair- way in accordance with Section 1030.9.7 having access to an exit. Exceptions: 1. Dead-end aisles not greater than 20 feet (6096 mm) in length. 2. Dead-end aisles longer than 20 feet (6096 mm) where seats beyond the 20-foot (6096 mm) dead-end aisle are not more than 24 seats from another aisle, measured along a row of seats having a minimum clear width of 12 inches (305 | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_5_dead_end_aisles |
| 1030.9.6 | Aisle measurement | flag | draft | An aisle's clear width is measured to walls, edges of seating and tread edges, but for permitted projections; an aisle beside seating at tables may be measured per 1030.13.1 instead: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.6 Aisle measurement. The clear width for aisles shall be measured to walls, edges of seating and tread edges except for permitted projections. Exception: The clear width of aisles adjacent to seating at tables shall be permitted to be measured in accordance with Section 1030.13.1. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_6_aisle_measurement |
| 1030.9.6.1 | Assembly aisle obstructions | flag | draft | Nothing obstructs an aisle's minimum width or required capacity; but handrails may project into the required width of stepped and ramped aisles per 1014.9: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.6.1 Assembly aisle obstructions. There shall not be obstructions in the minimum width or required capacity of aisles. Exception: Handrails are permitted to project into the required width of stepped aisles and ramped aisles in accordance with Section 1014.9. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_6_1_assembly_aisle_obstructions |
| 1030.9.7 | Stairways connecting to stepped aisles | flag | draft | A stairway connecting a stepped aisle to a cross aisle or concourse may meet 1030.14's assembly aisle walking surface requirements, and the transitions between such stairways and stepped aisles comply with 1030.10: stepped aisles and their stairways aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.7 Stairways connecting to stepped aisles. A stairway that connects a stepped aisle to a cross aisle or concourse shall be permitted to comply with the assembly aisle walking surface requirements of Section 1030.14. Transitions between stairways and stepped aisles shall comply with Section 1030.10. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_7_stairways_connecting_to_stepped_aisles |
| 1030.9.8 | Stairways connecting to vomitories | flag | draft | A stairway connecting a vomitory to a cross aisle or concourse may meet 1030.14's assembly aisle walking surface requirements, and the transitions between such stairways and stepped aisles comply with 1030.10: vomitories and their stairways aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.9.8 Stairways connecting to vomitories. A stairway that connects a vomitory to a cross aisle or concourse shall be permit- ted to comply with the assembly aisle walking surface requirements of Section 1030.14. Transitions between stairways and stepped aisles shall comply with Section 1030.10. | drafted:general.bc_1030_9_8_stairways_connecting_to_vomitories |
| 1030.10 | Transitions | reference | draft | A pointer: transitions between stairways and stepped aisles comply with 1030.10.1 or 1030.10.2; quoted by 1030.10.1's flag. | 1030.10 Transitions. Transitions between stairways and stepped aisles shall comply with either Section 1030.10.1 or 1030.10.2. | drafted:general.bc_1030_10_1_transitions_keeping_stepped_aisle_dimensions |
| 1030.10.1 | Transitions to stairways that maintain stepped aisle riser and tread dimensions | flag | draft | A transition between a stairway and a stepped aisle meets 1030.10.1 or 1030.10.2: where the stepped aisles, transitions and stairways keep the stepped aisle's riser and tread dimensions, they comply with 1030.14 as one exit access component: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.10.1 Transitions to stairways that maintain stepped aisle riser and tread dimensions. Stepped aisles, transitions and stairways that maintain the stepped aisle riser and tread dimensions shall comply with Section 1030.14 as one exit access component. | drafted:general.bc_1030_10_1_transitions_keeping_stepped_aisle_dimensions |
| 1030.10.2 | Transitions to stairways that do not maintain stepped aisle riser and tread dimensions | reference | draft | A pointer: transitions to stairways with other riser and tread dimensions comply with 1030.10.2.1 to 1030.10.3; quoted by 1030.10.2.1's flag. | 1030.10.2 Transitions to stairways that do not maintain stepped aisle riser and tread dimensions. Transitions between stairways and stepped aisles having different riser and tread dimensions shall comply with Sections 1030.10.2.1 through 1030.10.3. | drafted:general.bc_1030_10_2_1_straight_run_transitions |
| 1030.10.2.1 | Stairways and stepped aisles in a straight run | flag | draft | Where a stairway and a stepped aisle with different riser and tread dimensions meet (1030.10.2 to 1030.10.3) in a straight run, the transition is at least 22 inches deep where the treads on its descending side are deeper, or at least 30 inches where they are shallower: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.10.2.1 Stairways and stepped aisles in a straight run. Where stairways and stepped aisles are in a straight run, transi- tions shall have one of the following: 1. A depth of not less than 22 inches (559 mm) where the treads on the descending side of the transition have greater depth. 2. A depth of not less than 30 inches (762 mm) where the treads on the descending side of the transition have lesser depth. | drafted:general.bc_1030_10_2_1_straight_run_transitions |
| 1030.10.2.2 | Stairways that change direction from stepped aisles | flag | draft | Where the stairway changes direction from the stepped aisle, the transition between them is at least 11 inches deep, or the stepped aisle's tread depth if that is more: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.10.2.2 Stairways that change direction from stepped aisles. Transitions where the stairway changes direction from the stepped aisle shall have a minimum depth of 11 inches (280 mm) or the stepped aisle tread depth, whichever is greater, between the stepped aisle and stairway. | drafted:general.bc_1030_10_2_2_transitions_changing_direction |
| 1030.10.3 | Transition marking | flag | draft | A distinctive marking stripe at least 1 inch and at most 2 inches wide is at each nosing or leading edge next to the transition, distinctly different from the stepped aisle's contrasting marking stripe: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.10.3 Transition marking. A distinctive marking stripe shall be provided at each nosing or leading edge adjacent to the tran- sition. Such stripe shall be not less than 1 inch (25 mm), and not more than 2 inches (51 mm), wide. The edge marking stripe shall be distinctively different from the stepped aisle contrasting marking stripe. | drafted:general.bc_1030_10_3_transition_marking |
| 1030.11 | Stepped aisles at vomitories | reference | draft | A pointer: stepped aisles changing direction at vomitories comply with 1030.11.1, and transitions above a vomitory with 1030.11.2; quoted by 1030.11.1's flag. | 1030.11 Stepped aisles at vomitories. Stepped aisles that change direction at vomitories shall comply with Section 1030.11.1 Tran- sitions between a stepped aisle above a vomitory and a stepped aisle to the side of a vomitory shall comply with Section 1030.11.2. | drafted:general.bc_1030_11_1_stepped_aisles_changing_direction_at_vomitories |
| 1030.11.1 | Stepped aisles that change direction at vomitories | flag | draft | Where a stepped aisle changes direction at a vomitory (1030.11), its treads there are at least 11 inches deep, or the stepped aisle's tread depth if that is more, and a tread's height above a transition at a vomitory complies with 1030.14.2.2: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.11.1 Stepped aisles that change direction at vomitories. Stepped aisle treads where the stepped aisle changes direction at a vomitory shall have a depth of not less than 11 inches (280 mm) or the stepped aisle tread depth, whichever is greater. The height of a stepped aisle tread above a transition at a vomitory shall comply with Section 1030.14.2.2. | drafted:general.bc_1030_11_1_stepped_aisles_changing_direction_at_vomitories |
| 1030.11.2 | Stepped aisle transitions at the top of vomitories | flag | draft | A transition between the stepped aisle above a vomitory and the stepped aisles beside it is at least 11 inches deep, or the stepped aisle's tread depth if that is more: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.11.2 Stepped aisle transitions at the top of vomitories. Transitions between the stepped aisle above a vomitory and stepped aisles to the side of a vomitory shall have a depth of not less than 11 inches (280 mm) or the stepped aisle tread depth, whichever is greater. | drafted:general.bc_1030_11_2_transitions_at_top_of_vomitories |
| 1030.12 | Construction | flag | draft | Aisles, stepped aisles and ramped aisles are built of materials consistent with the types permitted for the building's type of construction; wood handrails are permitted in every type: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.12 Construction. Aisles, stepped aisles and ramped aisles shall be built of materials consistent with the types permitted for the type of construction of the building. Exception: Wood handrails shall be permitted for all types of construction. | drafted:general.bc_1030_12_construction |
| 1030.12.1 | Walking surface | flag | draft | The surface of aisles, stepped aisles and ramped aisles is of slip-resistant materials, securely attached, and a stepped aisle's surface complies with 1011.7.1: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.12.1 Walking surface. The surface of aisles, stepped aisles and ramped aisles shall be of slip-resistant materials that are securely attached. The surface for stepped aisles shall comply with Section 1011.7.1. | drafted:general.bc_1030_12_1_walking_surface |
| 1030.12.2 | Outdoor conditions | flag | draft | Outdoor aisles, stepped aisles and ramped aisles, and the outdoor approaches to them, are designed so that water doesn't accumulate on the walking surface: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.12.2 Outdoor conditions. Outdoor aisles, stepped aisles and ramped aisles and outdoor approaches to aisles, stepped aisles and ramped aisles shall be designed so that water will not accumulate on the walking surface. | drafted:general.bc_1030_12_2_outdoor_conditions |
| 1030.13 | Aisle accessways | reference | draft | A pointer: aisle accessways for seating at tables comply with 1030.13.1, for seating in rows with 1030.13.2; quoted by 1030.13.1's flag. | 1030.13 Aisle accessways. Aisle accessways for seating at tables shall comply with Section 1030.13.1. Aisle accessways for seating in rows shall comply with Section 1030.13.2. | drafted:general.bc_1030_13_1_seating_at_tables |
| 1030.13.1 | Seating at tables | flag | draft | Aisle accessways serve seating at tables per 1030.13.1 and seating in rows per 1030.13.2: where seating at a table or counter is next to an aisle or aisle accessway, its required clear width is measured to a line 19 inches from and parallel to the table's or counter's edge, measured perpendicular to that side; other side boundaries are measured to walls, edges of seating and tread edges; where fixed seats serve the tables or counters, the width is measured from the back of the seat: tables and seats aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.13.1 Seating at tables. Where seating is located at a table or counter and is adjacent to an aisle or aisle accessway, the measurement of required clear width of the aisle or aisle accessway shall be made to a line 19 inches (483 mm) away from and parallel to the edge of the table or counter. The 19-inch (483 mm) distance shall be measured perpendicular to the side of the table or counter. In the case of other side boundaries for aisles or aisle accessways, the clear width shall be measured to walls, edges of seating and tread edges. Exception: Where tables or counters are served by fixed seats, the width of the aisle or aisle accessway shall be measured from the back of the seat. | drafted:general.bc_1030_13_1_seating_at_tables |
| 1030.13.1.1 | Aisle accessway capacity and width for seating at tables | flag | draft | An aisle accessway serving seating at tables or counters meets 1005.1's capacity and is at least 12 inches wide plus 1/2 inch for each additional foot, or part of one, beyond 12 feet of its length measured from the center of the seat farthest from an aisle; but a portion at most 6 feet long and used by at most four people in all is exempt: tables and seats aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.13.1.1 Aisle accessway capacity and width for seating at tables. Aisle accessways serving arrangements of seating at tables or counters shall comply with the capacity requirements of Section 1005.1 but shall not have less than 12 inches (305 mm) of width plus 1/2 inch (12.7 mm) of width for each additional 1 foot (305 mm), or fraction thereof, beyond 12 feet (3658 mm) of aisle accessway length measured from the center of the seat farthest from an aisle. Exception: Portions of an aisle accessway having a length not exceeding 6 feet (1829 mm) and used by a total of not more than four persons. | drafted:general.bc_1030_13_1_1_table_aisle_accessway_width |
| 1030.13.1.2 | Seating at table aisle accessway length | flag | draft | Travel along an aisle accessway at tables is at most 30 feet from any seat to the point where a person has a choice of two or more paths of egress travel to separate exits: tables and seats aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.13.1.2 Seating at table aisle accessway length. The length of travel along the aisle accessway shall not exceed 30 feet (9144 mm) from any seat to the point where a person has a choice of two or more paths of egress travel to separate exits. | drafted:general.bc_1030_13_1_2_table_aisle_accessway_length |
| 1030.13.2 | Clear width of aisle accessways serving seating in rows | flag | draft | Where rows have 14 or fewer seats, the aisle accessway between them is at least 12 inches clear, measured horizontally from the back of the row ahead to the nearest projection of the row behind: with automatic or self-rising seats raised, any other seat down, and folding tablet arms in use (or stored, where a tablet arm raised to vertical in one motion falls back to stored by gravity): seats and rows aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.13.2 Clear width of aisle accessways serving seating in rows. Where seating rows have 14 or fewer seats, the minimum clear aisle accessway width shall be not less than 12 inches (305 mm) measured as the clear horizontal distance from the back of the row ahead and the nearest projection of the row behind. Where chairs have automatic or self-rising seats, the measurement shall be made with seats in the raised position. Where any chair in the row does not have an automatic or self-rising seat, the measurements shall be made with the seat in the down position. For seats with folding tablet arms, row spacing shall be deter- mined with the tablet arm in the used position. Exception: For seats with folding tablet arms, row spacing is permitted to be determined with the tablet arm in the stored position where the tablet arm when raised manually to vertical position in one motion automatically returns to the stored position by force of gravity. | drafted:general.bc_1030_13_2_rows_of_seats |
| 1030.13.2.1 | Dual access | flag | draft | A row of seating served by aisles or doorways at both ends has at most 100 seats, and the 12 inches between rows grows by 0.3 inch for every seat beyond 14 where the seats have backrests, or beyond 21 where they don't, to at most 22 inches needed; for smoke-protected or open-air seating, Table 1030.13.2.1 gives the row lengths a 12-inch aisle accessway may serve before it widens: seats and rows aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.13.2.1 Dual access. For rows of seating served by aisles or doorways at both ends, there shall be not more than 100 seats per row. The minimum clear width of 12 inches (305 mm) between rows shall be increased by 0.3 inch (7.6 mm) for every addi- tional seat beyond 14 seats where seats have backrests or beyond 21 where seats are without backrests. The minimum clear width is not required to exceed 22 inches (559 mm). Exception: For smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating, the row length limits for a 12-inch-wide (305 mm) aisle access- way, beyond which the aisle accessway minimum clear width shall be increased, are in Table 1030.13.2.1. | drafted:general.bc_1030_13_2_1_dual_access |
| Table 1030.13.2.1 | SMOKE-PROTECTED OR OPEN-AIR ASSEMBLY AISLE ACCESSWAYS | flag | draft | The most seats a row of smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating may have with a 12-inch clear aisle accessway, by the total number of seats (with backrests and without, an aisle or doorway at both ends, then at one end only): fewer than 4,000, 14, 21, 7 and 10; 4,000 to 6,999, 15, 22, 7 and 10; 7,000 to 9,999, 16, 23, 8 and 11; 10,000 to 12,999, 17, 24, 8 and 11; 13,000 to 15,999, 18, 25, 9 and 12; 16,000 to 18,999, 19, 26, 9 and 12; 19,000 to 21,999, 20, 27, 10 and 13; 22,000 and more, 21, 28, 11 and 14: seats and rows aren't in the model, so a person's check. | TABLE 1030.13.2.1—SMOKE-PROTECTED OR OPEN-AIR ASSEMBLY AISLE ACCESSWAYS MAXIMUM NUMBER OF SEATS PER ROW PERMITTED TO HAVE A MINIMUM 12-INCH CLEAR WIDTH AISLE ACCESSWAY NUMBER OF SEATS IN THE SMOKE- Aisle or doorway at both ends of row Aisle or doorway at one end of row only PROTECTED OR OPEN-AIR ASSEMBLY SEATING Seats with Seats without Seats with Seats without backrests backrests backrests backrests Less than 4,000 14 21 7 10 4,000 to 6,999 15 22 7 10 7,000 to 9,999 16 23 8 11 10,000 to 12,999 17 24 8 11 13,000 to 15,999 18 25 9 12 16,000 to 18,999 19 26 9 12 19,000 to 21,999 20 27 10 13 22,000 and greater 21 28 11 14 For SI: 1 inch = 25.4 mm. | drafted:general.bc_table_1030_13_2_1_smoke_protected_row_lengths |
| 1030.13.2.2 | Single access | flag | draft | For a row served by an aisle or doorway at one end only, the 12 inches between rows grows by 0.6 inch for every seat beyond seven where the seats have backrests, or beyond 10 where they don't, to at most 22 inches needed; for smoke-protected or open-air seating, Table 1030.13.2.1 gives the row lengths a 12-inch aisle accessway may serve before it widens: seats and rows aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.13.2.2 Single access. For rows of seating served by an aisle or doorway at only one end of the row, the minimum clear width of 12 inches (305 mm) between rows shall be increased by 0.6 inch (15.2 mm) for every additional seat beyond seven seats where seats have backrests or beyond 10 where seats are without backrests. The minimum clear width is not required to exceed 22 inches (559 mm). Exception: For smoke-protected or open-air assembly seating, the row length limits for a 12-inch-wide (305 mm) aisle access- way, beyond which the aisle accessway minimum clear width shall be increased, are in Table 1030.13.2.1. | drafted:general.bc_1030_13_2_2_single_access |
| 1030.14 | Assembly aisle walking surfaces | reference | draft | A pointer: ramped aisles comply with 1030.14.1 to 1030.14.1.3, stepped aisles with 1030.14.2 to 1030.14.2.4; quoted by 1030.14.1's flag. | 1030.14 Assembly aisle walking surfaces. Ramped aisles shall comply with Sections 1030.14.1 through 1030.14.1.3. Stepped aisles shall comply with Sections 1030.14.2 through 1030.14.2.4. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_1_ramped_aisles |
| 1030.14.1 | Ramped aisles | flag | draft | Ramped aisles comply with 1030.14.1 to 1030.14.1.3 and stepped aisles with 1030.14.2 to 1030.14.2.4: an aisle sloped more than 1 in 20 (5 percent) is a ramped aisle; one that is part of an accessible route per 1009 and 1109.2 is at most 1 in 12 (8 percent), and any other at most 1 in 8 (12.5 percent): aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.1 Ramped aisles. Aisles that are sloped more than 1 unit vertical in 20 units horizontal (5-percent slope) shall be consid- ered to be a ramped aisle. Ramped aisles that serve as part of an accessible route in accordance with Sections 1009 and 1109.2 shall have a maximum slope of 1 unit vertical in 12 units horizontal (8-percent slope). The slope of other ramped aisles shall not exceed 1 unit vertical in 8 units horizontal (12.5-percent slope). | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_1_ramped_aisles |
| 1030.14.1.1 | Cross slope | flag | draft | A ramped aisle's slope measured perpendicular to the direction of travel is not steeper than 1 in 48 (2 percent): aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.1.1 Cross slope. The slope measured perpendicular to the direction of travel of a ramped aisle shall not be steeper than 1 unit vertical in 48 units horizontal (2-percent slope). | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_1_1_cross_slope |
| 1030.14.1.2 | Landings | flag | draft | Ramped aisles have landings per 1012.6 to 1012.6.5, which may overlap required aisles or cross aisles: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.1.2 Landings. Ramped aisles shall have landings in accordance with Sections 1012.6 through 1012.6.5. Landings for ramped aisles shall be permitted to overlap required aisles or cross aisles. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_1_2_ramped_aisle_landings |
| 1030.14.1.3 | Edge protection | flag | draft | Ramped aisles have edge protection per 1012.10 and 1012.10.1; but in assembly spaces with fixed seating, not on the sides where the ramped aisle gives access to the adjacent seating and aisle accessways: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.1.3 Edge protection. Ramped aisles shall have edge protection in accordance with Sections 1012.10 and 1012.10.1. Exception: In assembly spaces with fixed seating, edge protection is not required on the sides of ramped aisles where the ramped aisles provide access to the adjacent seating and aisle accessways. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_1_3_ramped_aisle_edge_protection |
| 1030.14.2 | Stepped aisles | flag | draft | An aisle sloped more than 1 in 8 (12.5 percent) is a series of risers and treads across its full width, complying with 1030.14.2.1 to 1030.14.2.4: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.2 Stepped aisles. Aisles with a slope exceeding one unit vertical in eight units horizontal (12.5-percent slope) shall consist of a series of risers and treads that extends across the full width of aisles and complies with Sections 1030.14.2.1 through 1030.14.2.4. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_2_stepped_aisles |
| 1030.14.2.1 | Treads | flag | draft | Stepped aisle treads are at least 11 inches deep and uniform, adjacent treads differing by at most 3/16 inch: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.2.1 Treads. Tread depths shall be not less than 11 inches (279 mm) and shall have dimensional uniformity. Exception: The tolerance between adjacent treads shall not exceed 3/16 inch (4.8 mm). | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_2_1_stepped_aisle_treads |
| 1030.14.2.2 | Risers | flag | draft | Where a stepped aisle's gradient matches the adjoining seating's, its risers are at least 4 and at most 8 inches high and uniform within each flight; but they may vary only as far as the seating's changing gradient needs for sightlines, each variation over 3/16 inch between adjacent risers marked by a distinctive stripe 1 to 2 inches wide on each tread at the nosing next to it, distinct from the contrasting marking stripe; and risers up to 9 inches are permitted where the adjacent seating's slope needs them for sightlines: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.2.2 Risers. Where the gradient of stepped aisles is to be the same as the gradient of adjoining seating areas, the riser height shall be not less than 4 inches (102 mm) nor more than 8 inches (203 mm) and shall be uniform within each flight. Exceptions: 1. Riser height nonuniformity shall be limited to the extent necessitated by changes in the gradient of the adjoining seating area to maintain adequate sightlines. Where nonuniformities exceed 3/16 inch (4.8 mm) between adjacent risers, the exact location of such nonuniformities shall be indicated with a distinctive marking stripe on each tread at the nosing or leading edge adjacent to the nonuniform risers. Such stripe shall be not less than 1 inch (25 mm), and not more than 2 inches (51 mm), wide. The edge marking stripe shall be distinctively different from the contrasting marking stripe. 2. Riser heights not exceeding 9 inches (229 mm) shall be permitted where they are necessitated by the slope of the adjacent seating areas to maintain sightlines. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_2_2_stepped_aisle_risers |
| 1030.14.2.2.1 | Construction tolerances | flag | draft | Adjacent risers designed equal differ by at most 3/16 inch; where the stepped aisle takes 1030.14.2.2's exception 1, each riser of unequal height differs from the adjacent ones, in the direction of descent, by at most 3/8 inch where its treads are less than 22 inches deep, and by at most 3/4 inch where they are 22 inches or more: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.2.2.1 Construction tolerances. The tolerance between adjacent risers on a stepped aisle that were designed to be equal height shall not exceed 3/16 inch (4.8 mm). Where the stepped aisle is designed in accordance with Exception 1 of Section 1030.14.2.2, the stepped aisle shall be constructed so that each riser of unequal height, determined in the direction of descent, is not more than 3/8 inch (9.5 mm) in height different from adjacent risers where stepped aisle treads are less than 22 inches (560 mm) in depth and 3/4 inch (19.1 mm) in height different from adjacent risers where stepped aisle treads are 22 inches (560 mm) or greater in depth. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_2_2_1_construction_tolerances |
| 1030.14.2.3 | Tread contrasting marking stripe | flag | draft | Each tread has a contrasting marking stripe at least 1 inch and at most 2 inches wide at its nosing or leading edge, so each tread is readily seen in descent; but it may be left out where the tread surfaces already make each tread readily apparent in descent: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.2.3 Tread contrasting marking stripe. A contrasting marking stripe shall be provided on each tread at the nosing or leading edge such that the location of each tread is readily apparent when viewed in descent. Such stripe shall be not less than 1 inch (25 mm) and not more than 2 inches (51 mm) wide. Exception: The contrasting marking stripe is permitted to be omitted where tread surfaces are such that the location of each tread is readily apparent when viewed in descent. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_2_3_tread_contrasting_marking_stripe |
| 1030.14.2.4 | Nosing and profile | flag | draft | A stepped aisle's nosing and riser profile comply with 1011.5.5 to 1011.5.5.3: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.14.2.4 Nosing and profile. Nosing and riser profile shall comply with Sections 1011.5.5 through 1011.5.5.3. | drafted:general.bc_1030_14_2_4_nosing_and_profile |
| 1030.15 | Seat stability | flag | draft | In a building, room or space used for assembly purposes, seats are securely fastened to the floor; but not where there are no ramped or tiered floors for seating and 200 or fewer seats, nor at tables without ramped or tiered floors; over 200 seats without ramped or tiered floors, they are fastened together in groups of at least three or fastened to the floor; where flexible seating is integral to a space on tiered levels, up to 200 seats need not be fastened (plans showing seating, tiers and aisles submitted for approval); nor groups of at most 14 seats on level floors set apart by railings, guards, partial height walls or similar barriers; nor seats for musicians or other performers set apart so: seats aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.15 Seat stability. In a building, room or space used for assembly purposes, the seats shall be securely fastened to the floor. Exceptions: 1. In a building, room or space used for assembly purposes or portions thereof without ramped or tiered floors for seating and with 200 or fewer seats, the seats shall not be required to be fastened to the floor. 2. In a building, room or space used for assembly purposes or portions thereof with seating at tables and without ramped or tiered floors for seating, the seats shall not be required to be fastened to the floor. 3. In a building, room or space used for assembly purposes or portions thereof without ramped or tiered floors for seating and with greater than 200 seats, the seats shall be fastened together in groups of not less than three or the seats shall be securely fastened to the floor. 4. In a building, room or space used for assembly purposes where flexibility of the seating arrangement is an integral part of the design and function of the space and seating is on tiered levels, not more than 200 seats shall not be required to be fastened to the floor. Plans showing seating, tiers and aisles shall be submitted for approval. 5. Groups of seats within a building, room or space used for assembly purposes separated from other seating by railings, guards, partial height walls or similar barriers with level floors and having not more than 14 seats per group shall not be required to be fastened to the floor. 6. Seats intended for musicians or other performers and separated by railings, guards, partial height walls or similar barri- ers shall not be required to be fastened to the floor. | drafted:general.bc_1030_15_seat_stability |
| 1030.16 | Handrails | flag | draft | Ramped aisles sloped more than 1 in 15 (6.7 percent) and stepped aisles have handrails per 1014, at one or both sides or within the aisle width; a stepped aisle with seating on one side and 74 inches wide or more has two, one within 30 inches horizontally of the side of the tiered floor next to it; but no handrails are needed on ramped aisles with seating on both sides, nor where a guard at the aisle's side has a top as graspable as a handrail per 1014.4, and no extensions at the top and bottom where they would block crossovers: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.16 Handrails. Ramped aisles having a slope exceeding one unit vertical in 15 units horizontal (6.7-percent slope) and stepped aisles shall be provided with handrails in compliance with Section 1014 located either at one or both sides of the aisle or within the aisle width. Where stepped aisles have seating on one side and the aisle width is 74 inches (1880 mm) or greater, two handrails are required. Where two handrails are required, one of the handrails shall be within 30 inches (762 mm) horizontally of the side of the tiered floor adjacent to the stepped aisle. Exceptions: 1. Handrails are not required for ramped aisles with seating on both sides. 2. Handrails are not required where, at the side of the aisle, there is a guard with a top surface that complies with the grasp- ability requirements of handrails in accordance with Section 1014.4. 3. Handrail extensions are not required at the top and bottom of stepped aisles and ramped aisles to permit crossovers within the aisles. | drafted:general.bc_1030_16_handrails |
| 1030.16.1 | Discontinuous mid-aisle handrails | flag | draft | Where there is seating on both sides of the aisle, or a stepped aisle needs two handrails, mid-aisle handrails are discontinuous, with gaps or breaks at most every five rows, each at least 22 and at most 36 inches clear, measured horizontally, and the handrail ends rounded or bent: aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.16.1 Discontinuous mid-aisle handrails. Where there is seating on both sides of the aisle, the mid-aisle handrails shall be discontinuous. Where a stepped aisle is required to have two handrails, the mid-aisle handrails shall be discontinuous. Gaps or breaks shall be provided at intervals not exceeding five rows to facilitate access to seating and to permit crossing from one side of the aisle to the other. These gaps or breaks shall have a clear width of not less than 22 inches (559 mm) and not greater than 36 inches (914 mm), measured horizontally, and the mid-aisle handrail shall have rounded terminations or bends. | drafted:general.bc_1030_16_1_discontinuous_mid_aisle_handrails |
| 1030.16.2 | Handrail termination | flag | draft | Handrails at the side of stepped aisles return to a wall, guard or the walking surface, or continue to the handrail of the next stepped aisle flight: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.16.2 Handrail termination. Handrails located on the side of stepped aisles shall return to a wall, guard or the walking surface or shall be continuous to the handrail of an adjacent stepped aisle flight. | drafted:general.bc_1030_16_2_handrail_termination |
| 1030.16.3 | Mid-aisle termination | flag | draft | Mid-aisle handrails don't extend beyond the lowest riser and end within 18 inches of it, measured horizontally, with no extensions needed; but they may extend beyond it where their extensions don't obstruct the cross aisle's width: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.16.3 Mid-aisle termination. Mid-aisle handrails shall not extend beyond the lowest riser and shall terminate within 18 inches (381 mm), measured horizontally, from the lowest riser. Handrail extensions are not required. Exception: Mid-aisle handrails shall be permitted to extend beyond the lowest riser where the handrail extensions do not obstruct the width of the cross aisle. | drafted:general.bc_1030_16_3_mid_aisle_termination |
| 1030.16.4 | Rails | flag | draft | Where stepped aisles have mid-aisle handrails, an additional rail is about 12 inches below the handrail, its strength and attachment per 1607.9.1.2: stepped aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.16.4 Rails. Where mid-aisle handrails are provided in stepped aisles, there shall be an additional rail located approximately 12 inches (305 mm) below the handrail. The rail shall be adequate in strength and attachment in accordance with Section 1607.9.1.2. | drafted:general.bc_1030_16_4_rails |
| 1030.17 | Assembly guards | flag | draft | Guards next to seating in a building, room or space used for assembly purposes are provided where 1015 requires them and built per 1015, except as 1030.17.1 to 1030.17.4 provide; at bleachers, grandstands and folding and telescopic seating, guards are provided where ICC 300 and 1030.17.1 require them: guards and seating aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.17 Assembly guards. Guards adjacent to seating in a building, room or space used for assembly purposes shall be provided where required by Section 1015 and shall be constructed in accordance with Section 1015 except where provided in accordance with Sections 1030.17.1 through 1030.17.4. At bleachers, grandstands and folding and telescopic seating, guards must be provided where required by ICC 300 and Section 1030.17.1. | drafted:general.bc_1030_17_assembly_guards |
| 1030.17.1 | Perimeter guards | flag | draft | Perimeter guards are provided where the footboards or walking surface of seating are more than 30 inches above the floor or grade below: at least 42 inches high measured from the seatboard where seatboards adjoin the perimeter, or from the floor where seats are self-rising, and measured per 1015.3 where an aisle lies between the seating and the perimeter; but guards that impact sightlines may meet 1030.17.3, and bleachers, grandstands and folding and telescopic seating need none where the seating is next to a wall less than 4 inches away: guards and seating aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.17.1 Perimeter guards. Perimeter guards shall be provided where the footboards or walking surface of seating facilities are more than 30 inches (762 mm) above the floor or grade below. Where the seatboards are adjacent to the perimeter, guard height shall be 42 inches (1067 mm) high minimum, measured from the seatboard. Where the seats are self-rising, guard height shall be 42 inches (1067 mm) high minimum, measured from the floor surface. Where there is an aisle between the seating and the perim- eter, the guard height shall be measured in accordance with Section 1015.3. Exceptions: 1. Guards that impact sightlines shall be permitted to comply with Section 1030.17.3. 2. Bleachers, grandstands and folding and telescopic seating shall not be required to have perimeter guards where the seating is located adjacent to a wall and the space between the wall and the seating is less than 4 inches (102 mm). | drafted:general.bc_1030_17_1_perimeter_guards |
| 1030.17.2 | Cross aisles | flag | draft | Cross aisles more than 30 inches above the floor or grade below have guards per 1015; where the drop between a cross aisle and the floor or grade below is 30 inches or less, guards at least 26 inches above the aisle floor; but none where the backs of the seats at the front of the cross aisle rise 24 inches or more above the aisle's floor: guards and cross aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.17.2 Cross aisles. Cross aisles located more than 30 inches (762 mm) above the floor or grade below shall have guards in accordance with Section 1015. Where an elevation change of 30 inches (762 mm) or less occurs between a cross aisle and the adjacent floor or grade below, guards not less than 26 inches (660 mm) above the aisle floor shall be provided. Exception: Where the backs of seats on the front of the cross aisle project 24 inches (610 mm) or more above the adjacent floor of the aisle, a guard need not be provided. | drafted:general.bc_1030_17_2_cross_aisle_guards |
| 1030.17.3 | Sightline-constrained guard heights | flag | draft | Unless 1030.17.4 applies, a fascia or railing system meeting 1015's guard requirements and at least 26 inches high is provided where the floor or footboard is more than 30 inches above the floor or grade below and the fascia or railing would otherwise interfere with the sightlines of the seating right beside it: guards and seating aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.17.3 Sightline-constrained guard heights. Unless subject to the requirements of Section 1030.17.4, a fascia or railing system in accordance with the guard requirements of Section 1015 and having a minimum height of 26 inches (660 mm) shall be provided where the floor or footboard elevation is more than 30 inches (762 mm) above the floor or grade below and the fascia or railing would otherwise interfere with the sightlines of immediately adjacent seating. | drafted:general.bc_1030_17_3_sightline_constrained_guard_heights |
| 1030.17.4 | Guards at the end of aisles | flag | draft | Where the foot of an aisle is more than 30 inches above the floor or grade below, a fascia or railing system meeting 1015's guard requirements runs the aisle's full width, at least 36 inches high and giving at least 42 inches measured diagonally between the top of the rail and the nosing of the nearest tread: guards and aisles aren't in the model, so a person's check. | 1030.17.4 Guards at the end of aisles. A fascia or railing system complying with the guard requirements of Section 1015 shall be provided for the full width of the aisle where the foot of the aisle is more than 30 inches (762 mm) above the floor or grade below. The fascia or railing shall be not less than 36 inches (914 mm) high and shall provide not less than 42 inches (1067 mm) measured diagonally between the top of the rail and the nosing of the nearest tread. | drafted:general.bc_1030_17_4_guards_at_end_of_aisles |
| 1031.2 | Where required | rule | draft | Where emergency escape and rescue openings are required (Group R-2 in stories with one exit, Groups R-3 and R-4: basements and sleeping rooms below the fourth story) and where they open: checkable per sleeping room from its marked openings, its story and its doors; basements and what lies outside each opening are person's checks. | 1031.2 Where required. In addition to the means of egress required by this chapter, emergency escape and rescue openings shall be provided in the following occupancies: | drafted:general.bc_1031_2_escape_opening_where_required, drafted:general.bc_1031_2_basements, drafted:general.bc_1031_2_openings_to_public_way |
| 1031.2.1 | Operational constraints and opening control devices | flag | draft | Escape openings operate from inside without keys or tools: how each opening works isn't in the model, a person's check where the building may be Group R-2, R-3 or R-4. | 1031.2.1 Operational constraints and opening control devices. Emergency escape and rescue openings shall be operational from inside the room without the use of keys or tools. Window-opening control devices complying with ASTM F2090 shall be permitted for use on windows serving as a required emergency escape and rescue opening. | drafted:general.bc_1031_2_1_operational_constraints |
| 1031.3.1 | Minimum size | rule | draft | Net clear opening at least 5.7 sq ft, 5 sq ft for a grade-floor opening: checkable per marked escape opening from its measured clear opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.3.1 Minimum size. Emergency escape and rescue openings shall have a minimum net clear opening of 5.7 square feet (0.53 m[fn2]). Exception: The minimum net clear opening for grade-floor emergency escape and rescue openings shall be 5 square feet (0.46 m[fn2]). | drafted:general.bc_1031_3_1_escape_opening_min_area |
| 1031.3.2 | Minimum dimensions | rule | draft | Net clear opening at least 24 inches high and 20 inches wide, in normal operation: checkable per marked escape opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.3.2 Minimum dimensions. The minimum net clear opening height dimension shall be 24 inches (610 mm). The minimum net clear opening width dimension shall be 20 inches (508 mm). The net clear opening dimensions shall be the result of normal operation of the opening. | drafted:general.bc_1031_3_2_escape_opening_min_height, drafted:general.bc_1031_3_2_escape_opening_min_width |
| 1031.3.3 | Maximum height from floor | rule | draft | Bottom of the clear opening at most 44 inches above the floor: checkable per marked escape opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.3.3 Maximum height from floor. Emergency escape and rescue openings shall have the bottom of the clear opening not greater than 44 inches (1118 mm) measured from the floor. | drafted:general.bc_1031_3_3_escape_opening_max_sill_height |
| 1031.4 | Emergency escape and rescue doors | rule | draft | A door provided as the escape opening is a swinging or sliding door: checkable per marked escape door, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.4 Emergency escape and rescue doors. Where a door is provided as the required emergency escape and rescue opening, it shall be a swinging door or a sliding door. | drafted:general.bc_1031_4_escape_door_swinging_or_sliding |
| 1031.5 | Area wells | rule | draft | An escape opening whose bottom is below the adjacent grade has an area well: checkable per marked escape opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.5 Area wells. An emergency escape and rescue opening with the bottom of the clear opening below the adjacent grade shall be provided with an area well in accordance with Sections 1031.5.1 through 1031.5.3. | drafted:general.bc_1031_5_area_well_required |
| 1031.5.1 | Minimum size | rule | draft | The area well at least 9 sq ft, 36 inches out and 36 inches wide (a ladder or steps may encroach 6 inches), letting the opening open fully: checkable per marked escape opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.5.1 Minimum size. The minimum horizontal area of the area well shall be 9 square feet (0.84 m[fn2]), with a horizontal projec- tion and width of not less than 36 inches (914 mm). The area well shall allow the emergency escape and rescue opening to be fully opened. Exception: The ladder or steps required by Section 1031.5.2 shall be permitted to encroach not more than 6 inches (152 mm) into the required dimensions of the area well. | drafted:general.bc_1031_5_area_well_required, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_1_area_well_min_projection, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_1_area_well_min_width, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_1_area_well_allows_full_opening |
| 1031.5.2 | Ladders or steps | rule | draft | An area well deeper than 44 inches has a fixed ladder or steps the open window doesn't block: checkable per marked escape opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.5.2 Ladders or steps. Area wells with a vertical depth of more than 44 inches (1118 mm) shall be equipped with an approved permanently affixed ladder or steps. The ladder or steps shall not be obstructed by the emergency escape and rescue opening when the window or door is in the open position. Ladders or steps required by this section shall not be required to comply with Section 1011. | drafted:general.bc_1031_5_area_well_required, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_1_area_well_min_projection, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_1_area_well_min_width, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_2_area_well_ladder_over_44, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_2_1_area_well_ladder, drafted:general.bc_1031_5_2_2_area_well_steps |
| 1031.5.2.1 | Ladders | rule | draft | The area well's ladder: 12 inches wide inside, 3 inches out from the wall, rungs 18 inches apart, full height: checkable per marked escape opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.5.2.1 Ladders. Ladders or rungs shall have an inside width of at least 12 inches (305 mm), shall project at least 3 inches (76 mm) from the wall and shall be spaced not more than 18 inches (457 mm) on center (o.c.) vertically for the full height of the area well. | drafted:general.bc_1031_5_2_1_area_well_ladder |
| 1031.5.2.2 | Steps | rule | draft | The area well's steps: 12 inches wide inside, treads over 5 inches, risers 18 inches at most, full height: checkable per marked escape opening, where 1031.2 requires it. | 1031.5.2.2 Steps. Steps shall have an inside width of not less than 12 inches (305 mm), shall have treads greater than 5 inches (127 mm) in depth and a riser height not greater than 18 inches (457 mm) for the full height of the area well. | drafted:general.bc_1031_5_2_2_area_well_steps |
| 1031.5.3 | Drainage | flag | draft | Area wells drain to the foundation drainage system, except on well-drained soils: drainage isn't in the model, a person's check where the building may be Group R-2, R-3 or R-4. | 1031.5.3 Drainage. Area wells shall be designed for proper drainage by connecting to the building’s foundation drainage system required by Section 1805. Exception: A drainage system for area wells is not required where the foundation is on well-drained soil or sand-gravel mixture soils in accordance with the United Soil Classification System, Group I Soils, in accordance with Section 1803.5.1. | drafted:general.bc_1031_5_3_area_well_drainage |
| 1031.6 | Bars, grilles, covers and screens | flag | draft | Bars, grilles, covers and screens over escape openings keep their size and release from inside: such devices aren't in the model, a person's check where the building may be Group R-2, R-3 or R-4. | 1031.6 Bars, grilles, covers and screens. Where bars, grilles, covers, screens or similar devices are placed over emergency escape and rescue openings or area wells that serve such openings, the minimum net clear opening size shall comply with Sections 1031.3 | drafted:general.bc_1031_6_bars_grilles_covers_screens |
